## Story & Purpose Auditor

### efigure1.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** At matched design bandwidth ($N=256$, $NW=4$), the four routes to a smoothed spectral estimate produce essentially the same main lobe but differ by tens of dB in sidelobe leakage — i.e., bandwidth is shared, leakage is what distinguishes the methods.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the visual logic (same lobe / different skirts) does read at a glance, but several elements cannot be decoded without text:
- The dotted vertical lines in the right panel are unlabeled; a reader must guess they mark $\pm W$ (the design band edge). The left panel's dotted box *is* in the legend, so the reader may wrongly assume they are the same object.
- The right panel has no legend; the reader must carry colors across panels.
- `kernel (linear, × N)` is jargon — it is not obvious that the curves are normalized so that the ideal box has height $1/(2NW)\cdot N$.
- `frequency (1/N)` requires the reader to know the Rayleigh-unit convention; nothing states the equivalent in Hz or cycles/sample.
- The right panel is clipped at the top, leaving an empty wedge between the two dotted lines. This reads as missing data rather than as "main lobe, off-scale."
- The figure shows the multitaper kernel (red) with the *highest* sidelobes of the three tapered routes (~$-20$ dB vs. $-60$/$-90$ dB). This is the opposite of what most readers expect from a figure about leakage, and nothing on the plot explains it (it follows from including all $K=7$ tapers at equal weight). Left unannotated, the figure appears to argue against the paper's own recommendation.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — the purpose is *comparing groups* (four kernels) on two properties that live on incompatible scales, and the linear/log panel pair is the standard and correct solution. Two refinements worth considering: (a) plot the right panel as a single-sided log-frequency plot, which removes the empty central wedge and expands the decay region; (b) or overlay a shaded band marking $|f|<W$ instead of clipping.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Left-panel legend box sits over the rising edge of the Bohman/Hann curves near $x\approx-3$ to $+3$, $y<0.05$; it collides with data.
- Left panel is drawn to $\pm10/N$ though nothing happens beyond $\pm6/N$; ~40% of the panel is empty floor.
- Mismatched x-ranges between panels ($\pm10$ vs. $\pm25$) with no visual cue that the left panel is a zoom of the right.
- Fine y-gridlines in the left panel (every 0.02) are denser than the claim requires.
- The superordinate title duplicates information ("at equal design bandwidth") that will also appear in the caption.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — the quantitative content (main-lobe half-power width, peak sidelobe, leakage fraction, degrees of freedom) is apparently tabulated in `Table~\ref{tab:dof}`, and the appendix text already states "all main lobes have nearly the same width; the skirts differ by 40 dB." The figure's value is that it makes the *shape* of the trade-off visible in a way a table cannot, so it earns its place — but it is correctly placed in the supplement, and the caption should not restate the table's numbers.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The draft caption supplied does not belong to this figure. It is the caption for a band-power table (`tab:bandpower`), followed by unrelated body text; the actual figure caption appears only at the very end, attached to `fig1_kernels.png`. The figure/caption pairing and the filename (`efigure1.png` vs. `fig1_kernels.png`) must be reconciled before review can be completed.
- [BLOCKING] The right panel's vertical clipping creates an apparently empty region between the dotted lines, which a reader will parse as absent data. Either extend the y-axis, shade the region, or split to log-frequency.
- [BLOCKING] Unlabeled dotted verticals in the right panel; ambiguity with the "ideal box" dotted line in the legend.
- [ADVISORY] The counter-intuitive result (multitaper has the worst skirts of the tapered routes) is unexplained on the figure and risks being read as self-defeating.
- [ADVISORY] No legend in the right panel; color-matching across panels is required.
- [ADVISORY] Legend overlaps data in the left panel.
- [ADVISORY] Axis label `kernel (linear, × N)` and frequency unit `1/N` are both under-specified.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Attach the correct caption; state the claim in the first clause ("Matched bandwidth, unmatched leakage: ..."), then the construction, then one sentence on why the $K=7$ multitaper kernel's sidelobes sit higher than Hann's.
- Label the dotted verticals directly on the right panel as $\pm W$ and change their style (e.g., thin grey solid) so they are visually distinct from the "ideal box" in the legend.
- Replace the clipped right panel with either (i) y-limits extended to $+10$ dB with a light grey shaded band over $|f|<W$ annotated "main lobe," or (ii) a single-sided plot with log x-axis from $W$ outward, which makes the $-20$/$-60$/$-90$ dB separation much more legible.
- Add an in-panel legend (or direct end-of-curve color labels) to the right panel; direct labeling would let you delete the left panel's legend box entirely and clear the data collision.
- Tighten the left panel to $\pm6/N$ and reduce y-gridlines to every 0.05.
- Add a horizontal reference line or annotation in the right panel at the stated peak-sidelobe threshold ($1.5\times$ the half-power half-width) if that quantity is reported in `Table~\ref{tab:dof}`, so figure and table read against the same landmark.
- Consider annotating the left panel once with "all half-power widths within X%" so the "same main lobe" half of the claim is quantitative rather than merely visual.

### efigure2.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: Discarding the highest-order (leakiest) Slepian taper lowers the broadband bias floor of the equal-weight multitaper estimate from ~10 dB above the true AR(4) spectrum to near-truth, matching the Hann-tapered/box-smoothed periodogram.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — The axes, title, and descriptive legend carry most of the setup, so a reader can see that some estimators sit above the true PSD at high frequency. But the figure's quantitative payload lives only in the caption: the "20 dB high" and "10 dB high" floors are never marked on the plot, and the reader must eyeball vertical offsets in a region where six curves overlap. "Drop the leakiest taper" is also uninterpretable without the companion figure that shows the individual taper leakage — the reader cannot tell which taper was dropped or why it is the leakiest.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate (with a caveat) — An overlaid log-power-vs-frequency plot is the standard and correct idiom for comparing spectral estimators. The mismatch is at the level of *emphasis*, not chart family: the claim is about small vertical offsets (bias), but the display is dominated by variance (the raw periodogram's ±20 dB scatter). Suggested upgrade: a two-panel figure — top, the current overlay (for context); bottom, bias in dB (estimate minus true PSD, smoothed) on a compressed ±25 dB axis, where the 20 dB / 10 dB / ~0 dB floors become flat, readable, separated lines. Alternatively, keep one panel and add horizontal annotated brackets at the floor levels.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- The grey raw periodogram: it occupies the largest share of ink, is plotted at full resolution, and its only role is to motivate smoothing. Lighten further, thin, or plot behind a semi-transparent layer.
- The "raw periodogram, then box" curve (blue): it is a strawman used to set the 20 dB scale; it competes for attention with the K=7 vs K=6 comparison that is the actual claim. Consider retaining but de-emphasizing (thin, muted).
- Empty vertical real estate: nothing of interest below about −25 dB except periodogram spikes; the −40 dB floor stretches the panel and compresses the differences that matter.
- Full grid: with six overlapping traces the gridlines add clutter; light horizontal gridlines only would suffice.
- Redundant axis breadth in the low-frequency region (0–0.2), where all estimators coincide and no claim is made — a candidate for horizontal compression or an inset.

REDUNDANCY RISK: High — The caption opens with "Another realization of the AR(4) process," which explicitly signals that an earlier figure shows the same estimators on the same process with the same N and W. Unless this realization demonstrates something the first does not (e.g., it is the case where K=7 visibly fails), the figure is a second draw from the same experiment and belongs in the supplement — or, better, should be merged with the companion figure as a two-panel "two realizations" display, or replaced by a table of median bias floors across many realizations.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] The central comparison (K=7 red vs K=6 orange) is rendered in the most heavily overplotted region of the figure, where red and orange traces of similar amplitude and high-frequency jitter visually fuse into a band. The ~10 dB separation claimed in the caption is not reliably readable from the graphic.
  - [BLOCKING] The quantitative claims ("floors 20 dB high," "floors 10 dB high") are caption-only. Nothing in the figure marks the reference level, the floors, or the offsets, so the figure cannot be checked against its own caption.
  - [BLOCKING] Single-realization evidence for a bias claim. Bias floors are ensemble properties; showing one more draw does not establish that K=6 is systematically better than K=7. The figure as drawn invites the reader to infer a general result from one noisy sample.
  - [ADVISORY] Possible legend/plot inconsistency for the truth: the legend swatch for "true PSD (AR4)" reads as a solid thick black line, while the trace in the high-frequency region appears dashed. If the dashing is intentional, the legend should match; if it is an artifact of overplotting, the truth is being obscured by the very curves being compared to it.
  - [ADVISORY] "drop the leakiest taper" is an internal shorthand. Without a pointer to the figure or equation that identifies taper leakage, the parenthetical in the legend is opaque to a first-time reader.
  - [ADVISORY] Six simultaneous traces exceeds comfortable discrimination, especially with red/orange adjacency — a problem for readers with red–green or red–orange color deficiency, compounded by green also being present.
  - [ADVISORY] Title encodes experimental parameters (N, W) that are also in the caption; this duplication costs a title line that could instead state the claim.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Convert the title into the claim: "Dropping the leakiest taper removes the multitaper bias floor" and move N and W to the caption or an in-panel annotation.
  - Add a bias panel: plot (estimate − true PSD) in dB versus frequency on a ±25 dB axis. The 20 dB, 10 dB, and ~0 dB floors become flat separated lines and the claim becomes self-evident without annotation.
  - Annotate the floors directly in the main panel with short horizontal rules and text labels ("+20 dB," "+10 dB") in the 0.35–0.5 frequency band.
  - Replace red/orange with a colorblind-safe pair of clearly different lightness (e.g., dark purple vs light amber), and distinguish K=7 from K=6 with linestyle as well as hue.
  - Demote the raw periodogram and the "raw, then box" curve to thin, light traces; promote the true PSD to the topmost z-order so it is never occluded.
  - Truncate the y-axis to roughly −30 to 50 dB and clip the periodogram spikes, or plot the periodogram with high transparency so clipping is visually honest.
  - Replace the single-realization display with an ensemble summary: median and interquartile band of each estimator's bias over, say, 200 realizations. This supports the bias claim rigorously and makes this figure non-redundant with the first realization figure.
  - If the single-realization version is retained for intuition, merge it as panel (a) with the first figure's realization, and keep the ensemble result as panel (b); move whichever is less load-bearing to the supplement.
  - Add a one-clause reminder in the legend or caption identifying the dropped taper by index (e.g., "K=6 omits the k=6 Slepian taper, the highest-order and leakiest") and cross-reference the figure where taper leakage is quantified.

### efigure3.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: At matched (measured) resolution, no single spectral estimator wins on all three axes — multitaper buys the most equivalent degrees of freedom per unit bandwidth, while tapered-periodogram-plus-smoothing variants buy orders of magnitude less broadband leakage and lower side lobes.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — the panel-level "higher/lower is better" annotations are a good instinct, but three things block caption-free reading: (1) the left panel is titled "variance: higher is better" while the y-axis is "equivalent dof" — literally contradictory, since higher dof means *lower* variance; (2) the right-panel values for the Gaussian and Welch kernels are not peak side lobes at all (they are main-lobe skirt levels), and that caveat exists only in the caption, so the flat grey Welch line reads as a genuine −7 dB side lobe; (3) curves terminate at different x-values (most stop near 16/N, Welch extends to 25/N) with no on-figure explanation.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate — plotting each quality metric against *measured* half-power bandwidth is exactly the right way to compare methods at equal resolution, and connected lines correctly encode the swept bandwidth parameter. One alternative worth considering for the headline version: a single 2-D trade-off plot (leakage on y, equivalent dof on x, resolution encoded as marker size or annotated along each curve), which would collapse panels 1–2 into one and make the Pareto frontier explicit. Keep the 3-panel version as the supplement.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- "(the 2015 whiteboard plan)" in the suptitle — informal internal note, remove before submission.
- "half-power bandwidth (1/N)" repeated three times; label once on a shared axis or keep only on the center panel.
- Marker dots on every swept point in the left panel add little where curves are near-linear; they matter more in the center/right panels where curvature is the story.
- Redundant y-tick decades in the center panel are fine, but the full 10⁻⁹–10⁻² range is driven by two Bohman/Hann points; consider whether the bottom decade earns its space.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Low — this is already an eFigure, and the three metrics are genuinely distinct axes of the trade-off. Mild overlap risk: the right panel's ordering of methods largely recapitulates the center panel's ordering (leakage and side lobe are correlated), so if space is tight the peak-side-lobe panel is the most droppable, or could become a table of single numbers at one reference bandwidth.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Left panel title "variance: higher is better" contradicts its own y-axis (equivalent dof). A reader scanning the figure will conclude higher variance is desirable.
  - [BLOCKING] The right panel mixes two different quantities under one axis label (true peak side lobe vs. main-lobe skirt for Gaussian and Welch). As drawn, Welch looks like the worst side-lobe performer by a wide margin when the number is not comparable.
  - [ADVISORY] Unexplained truncation of curves at different bandwidths; the reader cannot tell whether a method failed, was not swept further, or hit a parameter limit.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend appears only in the left panel and lists eight series with several similar hues (blue/purple, red/brown, green); cross-panel tracking is hard, especially for the two DPSS variants.
  - [ADVISORY] Suptitle contains a non-scientific aside.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Retitle left panel "equivalent dof ν (higher = lower variance)" and drop the word "variance" from the title, or relabel the axis as "1/variance (equivalent dof)".
  - Split or mark the right panel: plot true flat-top side lobes as solid lines and the two skirt-level entries as open markers/dashed lines, with a short in-panel note "open: main-lobe skirt (no flat top)". Alternatively move Gaussian/Welch out of that panel entirely.
  - Delete "(the 2015 whiteboard plan)" and state N=256 plus the sweep variable in the suptitle only.
  - Add a one-line in-figure note on why sweeps end where they do (e.g., "sweeps end at the largest bandwidth with K ≤ ... tapers"), or extend all curves to a common x-limit.
  - Move the legend to a single shared horizontal legend below the three panels, group the three multitaper variants with one hue family and the periodogram-plus-smoother family with another, and differentiate within family by marker shape.
  - Consider adding a faint vertical reference line at one bandwidth (e.g., 8/N) across all three panels so readers can read off a like-for-like comparison at a glance.

### efigure4.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Every one of these four spectral estimators (Welch averaging, periodogram smoothing, sinc-window sliding, Thomson multitaper) is algebraically a weighted taper bank $\hat S(f)=\sum_k c_k|v_k^\top x_f|^2$; the constructions differ only in the shape of the eigen-weight ladder $c_k$ (and hence the effective degrees of freedom and the leakage of the resulting kernel), not in kind.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the small-multiple grid is well organized and the row-wise logic (bank → eigen-tapers → weights → kernel) is legible, but several elements cannot be decoded without text:
- Rows 1–2: no y-axis label or scale; the reader must infer that the four/seven coloured traces are vertically offset copies (amplitude, not stacked data). Nothing states what the colours mean (taper index $k$ = 0..3) — no legend or in-panel $k$ labels.
- Row 4: the grey shaded band is never identified (presumably $|f|\le W$ or the $2W$ band referenced in the titles). This is load-bearing for the "mass beyond $2W$" annotation.
- $\nu = 2(\sum c)^2/\sum c^2$ appears as a formula with a number but no statement that larger $\nu$ = more stable estimate; the reader cannot tell which direction is "good."
- The headline claim of equivalence is asserted in the suptitle but not *shown*: nothing in the figure demonstrates that the four columns estimate the same spectrum. The visual evidence is only that all four admit a taper-bank decomposition.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — this is a comparison across four methods along four commensurate properties, and a 4×4 small-multiple matrix with shared axes per row is the right instrument (Tufte small multiples). Row 3 as bar charts and row 4 as log-magnitude line plots are both correct choices. One sub-mismatch: row 3's identical x-range to $k=15$ is good for comparability, but the differences that matter (ladder shape: geometric decay vs. flat vs. box-with-rolloff) would read faster as four overlaid step/line profiles in a single panel than as four separated bar charts.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Three-line panel titles in row 1 restate method parameters that belong in the caption or a table; combined with row-2/3/4 titles, the figure carries ~16 lines of prose.
- Suptitle says "**three** ways to build the bank from one window" while four columns (a)–(d) are shown — either stale text or (d) is not counted; as printed it is simply wrong.
- Row 3: the empty x-range from $k\approx 8$ to $15$ in columns (a), (c), (d) is ~half the panel width carrying no ink (defensible for comparison, but consider cropping to $k\le 10$).
- Repeated "sample t" x-label on all eight panels of rows 1–2; one per column suffices.
- Gridlines on row 3 only (inconsistent with rows 1, 2, 4).

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — the caption explicitly frames this as "Fig.~\ref{fig:banks} in full," so overlap with a main-text figure is intended and acceptable for an eFigure. However, rows 3 and 4 reduce to six numbers per column ($\nu$, half-power width, mass beyond $2W$, plus ladder shape), which are already printed as panel titles; if space is tight, rows 3–4 could collapse into a 4×3 table, and the figure could be rows 1–2 only. Conversely, if the main-text figure already shows the kernels, row 4 here is duplicative.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Caption/panel mismatch: the caption says the eigen-tapers are "for (c) the Slepian sequences; for (d), whose weights are equal, any orthonormal basis…", but the panel titles put the Slepian/equal-weight case in column (d) and the sliding-sinc case in (c). The caption's column letters appear shifted by one. Reader cannot resolve which is which.
- [BLOCKING] Suptitle says "three ways" but four constructions (a)–(d) are shown.
- [BLOCKING] Grey shaded band in row 4 is unexplained anywhere in figure or caption.
- [ADVISORY] Colour→$k$ mapping absent in rows 1–2; rows 1–2 have no y-axis label or indication of vertical offsetting.
- [ADVISORY] The stated equivalence between estimators is not demonstrated visually (no shared-spectrum comparison), so the suptitle over-claims relative to the evidence displayed.
- [ADVISORY] Column (a) shows 7 traces while (b)–(d) show 4; the reason ("4 shown" of many) is buried in a title line and inconsistent across columns.
- [ADVISORY] $\nu$ reported without indicating that higher = more stable; similarly half-power width (lower = sharper) and mass beyond $2W$ (lower = less leakage) lack direction cues.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Fix the caption's column letters to match the panels, and correct "three ways" → "four ways" (or state explicitly that (d) is the reference construction, not one of the three).
- Define the grey band once (e.g., "shading: $|f|\le W$") in the caption and, if possible, annotate it in the leftmost row-4 panel.
- Add a single shared legend for $k=0,1,2,3$ (or label each trace in the leftmost panel of rows 1–2) and a y-label such as "taper amplitude (offset)".
- Move the parameter details from the three-line panel titles into a compact methods table or the caption; keep one short title per column, e.g. "(a) Welch, Hann, $L=N/4$".
- Add a directional cue to the summary numbers, e.g. "$\nu=13.4$ (higher = more stable)" and "mass beyond $2W$: 0.1% (lower = less leakage)", or better, add a fifth row / inset ranking the four columns on the width–leakage–$\nu$ tradeoff so the punchline is visible.
- Consider overlaying the four eigen-weight ladders in one panel (step plots, one colour per method) to make the "flat vs. decaying" contrast immediate, freeing a row.
- To support the equivalence claim, add one small panel showing the four estimates of the *same* test spectrum lying on top of each other; without it the suptitle is an assertion rather than a result.
- Harmonize gridlines and drop repeated axis labels within a column.

### figure1.png

---

MAIN CLAIM: Three apparently different spectral estimators — full-rank eigenvalue-weighted multitaper, box-smoothed periodogram, and sliding sinc-window periodogram average — produce the same PSD estimate, with (c) identical to (a) at machine precision and (b) agreeing to ~0.06 dB.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — mostly, but with three gaps. (i) The left panel shows three curves that are deliberately indistinguishable; because (a) is drawn thick and black underneath, a reader cannot verify that three curves are actually plotted rather than one curve with a redundant legend. (ii) The two panel titles carry the claim in prose, so the *figure* is readable only because the titles are doing caption work — if the titles were removed the right panel would just be "a flat line and some noise." (iii) The caption's key quantitative claim, "converges as $1/L$," has no supporting graphical element anywhere in the figure; only a single $L=16N$ is shown.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate (with one substantive refinement) — overlaid spectra plus a difference panel is the right idiom for "these estimators agree." However, the right panel forces two errors that differ by ~11 orders of magnitude (2.7e-13 dB vs 0.056 dB) onto one linear axis, where the machine-precision curve collapses onto the zero line and conveys no information beyond "flat." Plot $|{\rm difference}|$ on a log y-axis instead: both curves then become visible, and the separation between "exact identity" and "asymptotic convergence" becomes the visual message rather than a legend annotation. A small log–log inset of $\max|{\rm (b)-(a)}|$ versus $L$ (a few values of $L$, with a $1/L$ reference slope) would convert the caption's convergence claim into something the figure actually shows.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- Right panel y-range of $\pm0.20$ dB when data occupy $\pm0.06$: roughly 70% of the panel is empty, shrinking the only informative trace.
- Panel titles restate the caption verbatim; one of the two should go.
- Legend in the left panel is large and sits over the spectral peaks in the 8–16 Hz region.
- Gridlines in the right panel serve little purpose once the axis is rescaled or log-transformed.
- Reporting "max 2.7e-13 dB" / "max 0.056 dB" inside the legend mixes annotation with key results; these belong in the caption or as text labels beside the traces.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — the left panel contains no discriminating information (by construction all three curves coincide); its entire function is to establish that the test spectrum is non-trivial and structured, which it does well but could do at half the width. If the manuscript contains another EEG spectrum figure, the left panel is a candidate for shrinking to an inset of the right panel, or for the supplement. The three max-error numbers could be a two-line table.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Caption asserts $1/L$ convergence; the figure shows a single $L$ and therefore cannot support the claim. Either add the $L$-sweep panel/inset or remove the rate claim from the caption.
  - [BLOCKING] Three-curve overlay is unverifiable as drawn: thick black (a) occludes (b) and (c). Reader cannot confirm the figure shows what the legend says.
  - [ADVISORY] Linear $\pm0.2$ dB axis renders the (c)−(a) trace informationless and wastes ~70% of the panel.
  - [ADVISORY] Claims live in panel titles rather than in the graphics; figure degrades badly if titles are stripped by a journal's style.
  - [ADVISORY] Left-panel legend occludes data.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Draw (a) as a thick light-grey line, (c) as a solid dark line, and (b) as a dashed line on top, so all three are individually visible; add a small zoomed inset over a 2–3 Hz window with a sharp peak to demonstrate coincidence at resolution scale.
  - Convert the right panel to $\log_{10}|{\rm difference}|$ in dB, or to a two-row broken-axis panel, so the 1e-13 and 1e-2 curves are both legible and their separation is the visual point.
  - Add an inset (or third panel) of $\max|{\rm (b)-(a)}|$ vs $L\in\{N,2N,4N,8N,16N\}$ on log–log with a $1/L$ guide line; this converts the strongest theoretical claim into evidence.
  - Move the numeric max-error values out of the legend into direct labels adjacent to each trace, and delete one of the two panel titles.
  - Relocate the left-panel legend below the axes or into the empty lower-right region above 30 Hz.
  - Consider a 1:2 width ratio favouring the difference panel, since that panel carries the claim.

### figure2.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Every spectral estimate is the true spectrum convolved with the estimator's kernel plus noise, so the taper sets leakage and the smoothing half-width sets the variance–resolution trade-off: the "just right" box (W = 4/N, panels c/g) is the one whose width matches the narrowest feature that must survive.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the figure gets ~80% of the way there, which is unusually good, but three annotations block a caption-free read:
- "mass beyond 2W = 1.3% / <0.001% / 66%" appears in all four bottom titles and is defined *nowhere*, not even in the caption. The reader cannot tell what mass (of the kernel? in dB or linear? over what interval?) is being measured, yet these numbers carry the leakage argument.
- ν = 2.0 / 8.9 / 50.0 is unlabeled in the panels; only the caption says it is equivalent degrees of freedom.
- The grey filled region in the bottom row is labeled "true spectrum near its peaks, for scale" only in panel (e)'s legend, and it is plotted on a "kernel (dB re peak)" axis — a different quantity on the same ordinate. Readers will initially misread it as a second kernel.
- The light-blue shading (|f| ≤ W) in (g)/(h) has no on-figure label at all; in (h) there are two visibly different blue tones (|f|≤W vs |f|≤2W?) with no key.

What *does* work without text: the plain-language panel titles ("leaky and noisy", "still noisy", "just right", "too smooth") carry the narrative arc by themselves. Keep them.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — this is a comparison of four estimators (categorical, ordered by smoothing) on a shared continuous domain, plus the mechanism (kernel) beneath each. Small multiples with shared axes are the right choice, and the two-row estimate/kernel pairing is exactly the "effect above, cause below" structure the claim needs. dB ordinates are conventional and necessary given the 65 dB dynamic range.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Four repetitions of the x-axis label "frequency (cycles/sample)" and "frequency offset (1/N)"; one per row suffices.
- Duplicate y-tick labels on panels (b)(c)(d) and (f)(g)(h); ranges are identical, so inner ticks can go.
- Panel (e): ~100 resolved Fejér side-lobe oscillations down to −80 dB is a solid red block of ink that obscures the one thing that matters (skirt decay rate). Plot the side-lobe *envelope*, or the envelope over a light rendering of the lobes.
- Two competing measures of kernel width in the top row (blue bar length + "width x/N" text + ν in the title) — three encodings of roughly one idea.
- Grid lines are fine but slightly heavy against the low-contrast grey spectrum fill in the bottom row.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Low — each column contributes a distinct step in the argument (no taper → taper → right smoothing → too much smoothing), and no column could be dropped without breaking the chain. The numeric annotations (ν, kernel width, mass beyond 2W) do amount to a small table embedded in the figure; if a table of estimator properties already exists in the text, these are the duplicated part, not the panels.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] "mass beyond 2W" is undefined in both the figure and the caption, yet it is the quantitative backbone of the leakage claim in (e)–(h). Define it or remove it.
- [BLOCKING] The two key scales that justify the words "just right" and "too smooth" — the 7/N peak width and the 30/N peak separation — are stated only in the caption and never marked on the top-row panels. Without them, (c) vs (d) is an aesthetic judgment rather than a demonstrated one.
- [ADVISORY] The grey "true spectrum, for scale" overlay shares an axis with a differently-normalized quantity (dB re kernel peak). Needs an explicit on-figure note that only the *vertical extent*, not the absolute level, is meaningful.
- [ADVISORY] Two shades of blue shading in (h) are unexplained; in (e)/(f) there is no shading at all, so the reader cannot tell whether W is zero or simply not drawn.
- [ADVISORY] Blue bar lengths are given in units of 1/N while the top-row abscissa is in cycles/sample; the reader must know N = 1024 to check the bar against the peaks.
- [ADVISORY] Caption is ~200 words and re-derives the whole argument. If the figure carries the story (it nearly does), the caption should shrink to definitions plus the one-sentence claim.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Replace "mass beyond 2W" with a defined, spelled-out quantity, e.g. "leakage: 1.3% of kernel power outside |f| ≤ 2W", and state the definition once in the caption.
- Annotate the top-row panels with a short horizontal bar or bracket for the 7/N peak width and the 30/N peak separation (draw it once in panel a, repeat faintly in c and d). Then "just right" and "too smooth" become readable as geometry rather than assertion.
- Add a compact key inside panel (e) or as a row-level legend: grey = true spectrum (vertical scale only), blue shading = |f| ≤ W, red = kernel.
- Collapse the three width encodings in the top row to two: keep the blue bar (spatial, directly comparable to the peaks) and ν (statistical), and drop the redundant "width x/N" text, or move it into the bar's tooltip-style label only.
- In panel (e), draw the Fejér side-lobe envelope as a heavier line over faint lobes so the −6 dB/octave skirt decay is legible against the (f) comparison.
- Strip inner tick labels and duplicate axis titles; label each row's axes once. This recovers roughly 15% of the panel area for the data.
- Trim the caption to: bold claim sentence, the process/parameters (N, peak width, separation, dynamic range), definitions of ν and the leakage metric, and one sentence on the Fejér-vs-Hann skirt trade. Let the panel titles do the rest.

---

### figure3.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** As the smoothing box half-width $W$ grows, variance falls and blur-bias rises, so the total RMS error against the true spectrum has a broad minimum — near $W\approx 6$–$10/N$ over the whole band, but at smaller $W$ if only the peak region is scored.

That is the claim of panel (b). Panel (a) makes a *different*, unrelated claim (what Hann tapering does to a record), so the figure as a whole is currently two claims stapled together under one title.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — panel (b) is close, but several encodings cannot be decoded without the caption or a prior figure:
- $\nu = 2, 9, 50$ appears nowhere in the axes or legend. A reader cannot know these are equivalent degrees of freedom (or how they map to $W$).
- "peak region" (dashed curve) is undefined — which frequency band? The two dotted reference lines hint at it but don't define it.
- "RMS error (dB)" does not say *error relative to what* (the true/theoretical AR(4) spectrum). Without that, the y-axis is uninterpretable.
- The red circles are meaningful only by cross-reference to Fig. 1(b–d); on its own the reader sees three arbitrary highlighted points with qualitative labels.
- The relation between black (total), blue (noise) and orange (bias) is unstated — they evidently combine in quadrature, not additively, which a reader will try and fail to verify by eye at $W=0$.
- Panel (a): "Hann taper (scaled)" — scaled by what? The dashed envelope is at $\pm70$ while the taper's actual amplitude is 1. The green "tapered record" is also not obviously the product of the gray record and the taper at a glance, because the gray trace's amplitude is not visibly modulated.

**CHART TYPE:** Mismatch (composite) —
- Panel (b): **Appropriate.** This is a relationship / tradeoff question and a line plot with decomposed components is exactly right. Good choice of log-like dB y-axis and annotated regimes.
- Panel (a): **Mismatch for this figure's purpose.** A time-domain trace explains a *process* (tapering), not the bandwidth choice the figure is titled around. If tapering needs its own explanation, it belongs in the tapering figure; if the point is only "this is the record used," it belongs in Fig. 1 and should be cross-referenced, not redrawn.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Panel (a) in its entirety, as far as the stated claim is concerned (see redundancy below).
- The gray untapered record in (a): dense high-frequency ink that overplots the green trace and obscures the very amplitude modulation the panel is meant to show.
- The arbitrarily scaled Hann envelope (dashed, $\pm70$) in (a) — it's a decorative annotation drawn in data coordinates it does not live in.
- Gridlines in both panels are doing little; in (a) they compete with the dense trace.
- The panel-(a) legend (three entries) costs a full legend row for a panel that may not survive.
- Redundant qualitative labels: "noise (std), falls with $W$" / "bias (blur), grows with $W$" duplicate what the curve shapes already show *and* what the caption says; the direction-of-change text can go.
- "too noisy / just right / too smooth" is a Goldilocks annotation that is charming but partly editorial; keep at most if the $\nu$ values are properly defined.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** High — the caption itself says panel (a) is "the record of Fig.~\ref{fig:motif}" and that the circled points "are panels (b), (c) and (d) of Fig.~\ref{fig:motif}." So roughly half this figure re-displays data already shown, and the highlighted points are pointers into another figure. Panel (b)'s three annotated regimes are also likely to be re-narrated in the text and possibly re-plotted as spectra elsewhere.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Two unrelated claims in one figure. The title says "Choosing the bandwidth" but panel (a) is about tapering a record. A reader cannot state one takeaway.
- [BLOCKING] $\nu$ (degrees of freedom) is used as the primary annotation of the three regimes but is never defined in the figure; the reader has no way to connect $\nu$ to $W$.
- [BLOCKING] The y-axis quantity is incomplete: "RMS error" with no reference spectrum named, and "peak region" undefined.
- [ADVISORY] The composition rule for black = f(blue, orange) is unstated; readers will attempt to check additivity and conclude the plot is inconsistent.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a)'s "scaled" taper mixes two vertical units in one axis without a secondary axis.
- [ADVISORY] Red is used only for annotation while also being a high-salience color; the circles + red text pull the eye away from the minimum, which is the actual point.
- [ADVISORY] The broad minimum — arguably the most important qualitative message — is not marked. The eye is drawn to the three circled points instead.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Drop panel (a) and promote panel (b) to a single-panel figure titled around the tradeoff (e.g. "Bias–variance tradeoff in the smoothing bandwidth"). Replace panel (a) with a one-line cross-reference in the caption ("same record as Fig. 1"). This alone fixes the main-claim and redundancy problems.
- Add a secondary top axis labeled "equivalent degrees of freedom $\nu$" with ticks at the relevant values, so $\nu=2,9,50$ is readable from the plot rather than from memory.
- Rename the y-axis "RMS error vs. true AR(4) spectrum (dB)" and shade the "peak region" band, or state its frequency limits in the legend entry.
- Mark the minimum explicitly: a light vertical band spanning the broad flat region of the solid black curve, labeled "broad optimum." Let that, not the red circles, be the visual focus.
- Shorten legend entries to "total (whole band)", "total (peak region)", "noise", "bias", and move "falls/grows with $W$" into the caption; add one clause stating how total combines noise and bias.
- If panel (a) must stay for narrative reasons, drop the gray untapered trace, plot the Hann taper on a right-hand axis in its true $[0,1]$ units, and retitle the panel so it claims something ("tapering suppresses the record edges") rather than merely labeling it.
- Consider moving the three circled exemplars entirely to Fig. 1's caption, so this figure carries one clean curve family and one message.

### figure4.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Summing the eigenvalue-weighted Slepian spectral windows tiles the band $[-W,W]$, so multitaper spectrum estimation is equivalent to smoothing with an (approximately) boxcar kernel — and tapers beyond $k=2NW$ contribute only out-of-band leakage.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the panel titles do a lot of good work (they are declarative, which is the right instinct), but three of four panels have no $y$-axis label, so the reader cannot tell *what quantity* is plotted. Specifically:
- Top left: no $y$ axis at all (acceptable given "offset for display," but the tick marks are absent while the box frame remains — ambiguous).
- Top right: $y$ is unlabeled; is $|U_k(f)|^2$ normalized to unit energy, unit peak, or something else? The reader must guess why $k=0$ peaks at 0.49.
- Bottom left: $y$ unlabeled; the quantity $\sum_{k<K}\lambda_k|U_k(f)|^2$ appears only in the caption. The dashed box height (0.125) is unexplained — presumably $1/(2W\cdot N)$ or similar normalization, but nothing on the figure says so.
- The shaded region in the top right is never identified on the figure as $[-W,W]$; the dashed box in the bottom left is likewise unlabeled.
- Units "frequency offset (1/N)" require the reader to infer that $W=4/N$ maps to $\pm 4$.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — this is an explanatory/mechanism figure, and the sequence (individual tapers → individual windows → cumulative sum → eigenvalue cutoff) is a sound build-up. Overlaid line plots are the correct choice for showing how windows interleave and accumulate. One caveat: the bottom-right panel answers a different question (where to truncate $K$) than the other three (why the kernel is a box), so it reads as a fourth claim rather than the conclusion of the first three.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Bottom right: the $y$ axis spans to $10^{-16}$ while the data bottom out near $10^{-10}$ — six empty decades of white space compressing the informative region.
- Bottom left: the "first 9 tapers" (purple) curve is essentially invisible, hidden beneath the red/black traces, yet occupies a legend slot. Either drop it or offset/thin the overlapping curves.
- Bottom left: six legend entries where three (1, 3, all $N$) would carry the same message.
- Top right: eight-curve legend with two columns; individual $k$ identities are not actually needed for the claim ("each fills the gaps left by the previous ones"). The legend is decoding effort that buys nothing.
- Top left: legend box sits on top of the $k=2$ and $k=3$ traces.
- Inconsistent grid usage — only the bottom-right panel has gridlines.
- Taper count mismatch: top left shows six tapers, top right shows eight. No reason is given for the change.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — the top-right panel is largely subsumed by the bottom-left panel, which shows the same windows in accumulated form and makes the tiling argument more directly. The top-left panel (tapers in the time domain) is the least load-bearing: it is textbook illustration, not evidence for the claim, and is the most obvious supplement candidate. A three-panel or even two-panel version (tiling + cumulative sum, or cumulative sum + eigenvalue ladder) would carry the argument.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Caption says that with all $N$ tapers the sum is *exactly* the box, but the black curve visibly has rounded shoulders, ripple in the passband, and small sidelobes outside $\pm 4$ — it looks nearly identical to the red ($K=7$) curve. Either the plotted black curve is not the all-$N$ sum, or the normalization/plot resolution obscures the identity. As drawn, the figure contradicts the central claim it is meant to demonstrate. This must be resolved before the figure can be published.
- [BLOCKING] Missing $y$-axis labels on the top-right and bottom-left panels. The quantity that the entire argument rests on ($\sum_k \lambda_k|U_k(f)|^2$) is never named on the figure.
- [ADVISORY] The shaded band and the dashed box are the visual anchors of the claim but are unannotated; add an in-axes label such as $[-W,W]$, $W=4/N$.
- [ADVISORY] Legend entry for a curve that is not visible (bottom left, "first 9 tapers").
- [ADVISORY] Wasted log-axis range in the bottom-right panel.
- [ADVISORY] Top-left and top-right show different numbers of tapers without explanation.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Resolve the "exactly the box" discrepancy: plot the all-$N$ sum on a separate inset or add a residual trace ($\text{sum} - \text{box}$) on a second axis to show it is zero to machine precision. If numerical, state the achieved max deviation in the panel.
- Add $y$-axis labels: "$\lambda_k |U_k(f)|^2$" (top right) and "$\sum_{k<K}\lambda_k|U_k(f)|^2$" (bottom left); state the normalization convention in the caption.
- Annotate $[-W,W]$ directly inside the shaded band and next to the dashed box; drop the reliance on caption text.
- Reduce the bottom-left legend to three curves ($K=1$, $K=2NW-1$, all $N$) and label them with in-line text at the curve shoulders instead of a legend box.
- Drop the top-right legend entirely; instead annotate only $k=0$ and one high-$k$ window, and let the rest be light gray "supporting" curves. This makes the tiling pattern the visual message rather than the color code.
- Clip the bottom-right $y$ axis to $[10^{-11}, 10^{0}]$ and label the two vertical lines directly on the axis rather than in a legend; add a shaded "leakage-dominated" region to the right of $2NW$.
- Move the top-left panel (time-domain tapers) to the supplement, or demote it to a small inset in the top-right panel, and promote the remaining three panels to a 1×3 row so the logical chain reads left-to-right.
- Apply consistent gridlines (or none) across all panels.

---

### figure5.png

---
**MAIN CLAIM:** Four seemingly different spectral estimators (Welch averaging, kernel-smoothed periodogram, a slid sinc window, and Thomson multitaper) are all the same object — a weighted bank of tapers — and they differ only in the shape of the eigen-weight ladder, which is what sets the effective degrees of freedom ν.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the top row is not readable without the caption, and one internal inconsistency actively misleads.

- The top row has **no y-axis label and no y ticks**. In (b), (c), (d) the four curves are *vertically offset different tapers*; in (a) the seven curves are *segments on a shared baseline shifted in time*. These are two different visual grammars in the same row, and nothing in the graphic marks the difference. A reader scanning left to right will read (a) as "seven tapers stacked" or read (b)–(d) as "four windows at four time offsets."
- Panel (a) shows 7 curves, (b)–(d) show 4 (with "4 shown" stated only in (c)). The reader cannot tell whether the count is a property of the method or a plotting choice.
- The link that carries the whole argument — "flatter/longer ladder ⇒ larger ν" — is left to the reader to infer from four scattered numbers (13.4, 11.1, 17.2, 14.0). Nothing in the figure ranks or connects them, and (c) having the largest ν despite bars *lower* than (d)'s may read as a contradiction unless the reader knows ν depends on both the number and the flatness of weights.
- The overall title says "**three** ways to build the bank from one window" while the figure shows **four** panels (a)–(d). This is a direct internal contradiction the reader will hit first.
- It is not stated in-figure whether the four constructions are bandwidth-matched (L = N/4, σ = 3/N, W = 4/N, K = 7). If they are not matched, the ν comparison is not an apples-to-apples comparison and the main claim weakens.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — small multiples (line plots of basis functions above bar charts of weights) is the right form for a "same object, different parameterization" comparison, and the shared axes across columns make the ladders directly comparable. The two-row structure correctly separates "what the bank looks like" from "how it is weighted." One refinement: the bottom row is a comparison of *shapes of decay*, so overlaying all four ladders as stepped lines in a single panel (or plotting cumulative weight) would make the flatness claim far more direct than four separate bar panels the eye must shuttle between.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- The phrase "eigen-weight ladder $c_k$: $\nu = 2(\sum c)^2/\sum c^2 =$" repeated verbatim four times. Say the formula once (row label or caption) and leave only "ν = 13.4" etc. in each panel.
- Three-line subtitles on every top panel; these are caption text pasted into the plot area. Keep the method name plus one distinguishing line; move parameters to the caption.
- Repeated axis labels: "sample t" ×4 and "taper index k" ×4 on shared-scale axes.
- Repeated y-tick labels on columns 2–4 of the bottom row (axes are shared).
- The estimator equation in the suptitle duplicates what will appear in the body text as a numbered equation.
- Empty right half of every bottom panel (k = 10–16 with no bars) — defensible for showing ladder length, but the axis could stop at ~12 and reclaim space.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — the caption itself points to Fig. \ref{fig:banksfull}, which contains "the eigen-tapers and kernels of all four." This figure is therefore a truncated preview of a fuller figure. Either this one earns its place by being the *argument* figure (weights and ν only, tapers minimal) and the full one goes to the supplement, or the two should merge. The four ν values alone are table material; only the ladder *shapes* justify a figure.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Suptitle says "three ways" but four panels are shown. Resolve: either label (d) as the reference/canonical case ("...three ways to build the bank from one window, compared with Thomson's") or say four.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (a)'s top plot uses a different visual convention (shared baseline, time-shifted segments) from (b)–(d) (vertically offset tapers). Unify the convention or explicitly mark it in-panel.
- [BLOCKING] Top row has no y-axis and no statement that curves are offset for clarity, so amplitude/normalization is uninterpretable and the offsets may be read as data.
- [ADVISORY] Inconsistent number of curves shown per panel (7 vs 4) with only one panel annotated "4 shown."
- [ADVISORY] The causal claim "flatness sets ν" is not visually supported; ν appears only as four bare numbers in titles.
- [ADVISORY] Bandwidth-matching across the four constructions is not asserted in-figure, leaving the fairness of the ν comparison open.
- [ADVISORY] Heavy in-panel text (12 lines of subtitle across the top row) competes with the data for attention.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Fix the "three vs four" contradiction in the suptitle; reduce the suptitle to the claim ("Every estimator is a weighted bank of tapers") and move the equation and N, W to the caption.
- Add a left-hand row label: "taper bank (offset for clarity)" and "eigen-weights $c_k$", then delete the four repeated bottom titles, keeping only "ν = 13.4" as an in-axes annotation.
- Show the same number of basis elements (e.g. k = 0–3) in all four top panels and add small "k = 0, 1, 2, 3" labels beside the curves; if (a) must show time-shifted segments, give it a visibly different framing (e.g. thin baseline ticks) and say so in-panel.
- Delete duplicate x-axis labels and y-tick labels on shared axes; label only the leftmost/bottom.
- Make the ν claim visual: overlay the four normalized ladders in one extra panel (stepped lines, one colour per method) or sort the columns by ν and add a single annotation "flatter, longer ladder → larger ν".
- State bandwidth matching explicitly, e.g. "all four tuned to half-bandwidth W = 4/N" in the caption, or note where they differ.
- Decide the division of labour with Fig. \ref{fig:banksfull}: keep this as the compact argument figure and move the full eigen-taper/kernel display to the supplement, or merge the two and cite once.
---

### figure6.png

---
**MAIN CLAIM:** At matched half-power resolution, the four standard spectral estimators differ mainly in *bias floor*, and the difference is explained by the far-field leakage of their effective smoothing kernels (bottom row) — untapered box leaks worst, Hann-based kernels leak least.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the visual story is ~80% readable, but three things stall the reader.
- The headline result, *median error vs. the truth* (11.6 / 4.6 / 1.3 / 0.8 dB), exists only in the caption. The figure's quantitative punchline is invisible on the figure.
- `ν = 17.2 / 14.0 / 8.9 / 17.6` appears in every panel title with no definition or units anywhere in the graphic. A reader who doesn't already know "equivalent degrees of freedom" cannot use it, and will not know whether higher is better.
- The gray shaded "true spectrum near its peaks, for scale" in the kernel row is a hybrid overlay: its horizontal axis is *frequency offset in 1/N*, yet it depicts an absolute spectrum feature. How the gray curve is registered to offset 0 is not deducible, and it invites the wrong reading (that the kernel and the spectrum are on the same abscissa in the same sense).
- The small blue square + "width 8.2/N" annotation in the top row is cryptic: is it a marker at a frequency, a legend entry, or a scale bar? It reads as a stray glyph.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — this is a comparison-of-methods figure with a mechanism row, and linked small multiples with shared axes is the right choice. Two refinements rather than a replacement:
- The top row asks the reader to judge *log-scale vertical gaps* between a thick black curve and a thin red one across four panels. A companion residual view (estimate − truth, dB, one panel with four traces) would make the bias floor directly readable instead of inferred.
- Panel (h) mixes a deep-null kernel (Welch/Hann segment) with panels whose kernels are smooth; the 80 dB range makes (g) plunge off the bottom while (h) fills the panel with nulls. Consider clipping the floor at −60 dB or plotting the kernel's cumulative leakage mass as a function of offset, which is what the "mass beyond 2W" number in the titles actually measures.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Eight repetitions of "frequency (cycles/sample)" / "frequency offset (1/N)" x-axis labels; one per row suffices.
- Repeated y-tick labels on columns 2–4 of both rows (axes are already shared).
- The gray "true spectrum for scale" band — decorative in (g) and (h), where the kernel never approaches it; it mainly adds ink.
- The deep null spikes below −70 dB in (h) carry no argumentative content.
- The blue square marker accompanying the width annotation.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Low — the four-method comparison and the kernel mechanism are the paper's core argument and belong together in the main text. The only duplicated content is the caption's four median-error numbers, which are data, not prose, and should migrate onto the panels.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The figure's own evidence does not support the implied ordering. "Mass beyond 2W" is 1.4 % (e), 0.22 % (f), <0.001 % (g), 0.48 % (h), yet median errors are 11.6, 4.6, 1.3, 0.8 dB. Panel (h) has ~500× more out-of-band mass than (g) but *lower* error. A reader who takes the kernel row as the explanation for the top row will immediately hit this contradiction. Either the ordering metric must change (e.g. leakage weighted by the true spectrum, which is presumably why the gray curve is there) or the caption must explicitly say that total mass is not the operative quantity and name what is.
- [BLOCKING] `ν` is undefined on the figure and its direction of merit is ambiguous; (c) has the lowest ν yet the second-best error, so the reader cannot tell whether ν is being offered as a cost or a benefit.
- [ADVISORY] Median errors — the actual result — are caption-only.
- [ADVISORY] The registration of the gray "true spectrum" overlay to the offset axis is unexplained.
- [ADVISORY] "width 8.2/N" annotations claim matched resolution, but the four values (8.2, 7.2, 8.2, 7.8) are not equal; the caption says Welch was "chosen for the same half-power width." State the tolerance.
- [ADVISORY] Sub-panel lettering runs (a)–(d) then (e)–(h), so the vertical pairing (a↔e) must be inferred. 

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Annotate each top panel with its median error, e.g. "median error 11.6 dB", in the panel corner. This is the claim; put it in the ink.
- Resolve the mass-beyond-2W inconsistency: replace it with a spectrum-weighted leakage figure of merit (integral of kernel × true PSD outside the band), or add a one-line note in the caption stating that unweighted mass is not predictive and why (h)'s nulls sit where the spectrum is small.
- Define ν in the caption on first use and give its direction ("equivalent degrees of freedom; higher = less variance"), or move it to a table if it is not part of this figure's claim.
- Re-label panels as (a1)/(a2) … (d1)/(d2), or add a light vertical divider between columns, so the estimator↔kernel pairing is immediate.
- Collapse duplicate axis labels to one per row and drop redundant y-tick labels; clip the kernel row at −60 dB.
- Either explain the gray overlay's alignment in a short in-figure note ("true PSD near its peaks, centred at offset 0, for amplitude scale") or drop it from (g) and (h) where it does no work.
- Consider adding a fifth narrow panel with the four residual curves (estimate − truth) overplotted; it would carry the bias-floor claim more directly than four separate overlay panels.

### figure7.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** The three "everyday" spectral estimators (multitaper $K=7$, smoothed Hann periodogram, Welch) give essentially the same EEG spectrum, with residual differences no larger than each estimator's own sampling noise.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — three specific gaps:
- The right panel is the load-bearing evidence, but it contains no reference for "how big is the estimators' own noise." Without a plotted band at $\pm 4.3\sqrt{2/\nu}$ (≈±1.6, ±1.5 dB) the reader cannot verify the claim from the graphic; they must take the caption's arithmetic on faith.
- The legend labels run **(a), (c), (b)** in the left panel and **(c)−(a), (b)−(a)** in the right. The out-of-order lettering plus the fact that (a)/(b)/(c) are *curves*, not panels, invites the reader to hunt for nonexistent subpanels. Neither panel carries a panel letter.
- The caption's punchline — "the largest differences are in the valleys between harmonics, which the seventh Slepian taper's side lobes fill in" — is a visual claim with no visual marking. Nothing in the figure directs the eye to the valleys or links the orange excursions to harmonic troughs.

Also note: the 95% values in the right legend (4.9 and 5.9 dB) are 3–4× the quoted noise size. A reader reading the legend alone may conclude the estimators *disagree*. The figure currently undercuts its own headline unless the tail behaviour is explained or bracketed.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — overlaid spectra for the comparison, difference-vs-frequency (a Bland–Altman in the frequency domain) for the agreement claim. This is the right pairing. Two refinements worth considering: (i) add a shaded tolerance band to the difference panel, converting it from "here are the residuals" to "here are the residuals *against the expected noise floor*"; (ii) if the valley/side-lobe mechanism matters, a third narrow panel or an inset zoom on one harmonic + valley would carry it far better than prose.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Both panel titles restate caption content verbatim ($\nu$ values, half-power widths, medians). Pick one home for these numbers; the titles are the weaker choice because they crowd the plot and cannot be typeset properly.
- Left-panel legend entries carry full method recipes ("box of half-width W", "9 Hann segments of 1.5 s, step 0.81 s"). These belong in the caption or a methods table; the legend needs only (a) multitaper, (b) Welch, (c) smoothed Hann.
- The left panel's y-axis extends to −10 dB to accommodate a single sub-1 Hz orange excursion, compressing the informative 15–47 dB range. Clip or break the axis.
- Horizontal gridlines in the left panel add little when the curves are this dense.
- The zero line in the right panel is drawn in a color close to the blue trace; it should be neutral gray or black and clearly subordinate.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — the left panel re-plots the EEG epoch already shown in Fig.~\ref{fig:abstract}, and at this scale the three curves are nearly coincident by design, so it carries little information beyond "they overlap." Its job could be done by a much smaller inset, freeing the width for the difference panel. The accompanying Table~\ref{tab:dof} already tabulates $\nu$ and half-power widths for these estimators, so the numbers in the panel titles are a third copy.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The central claim ("agree to within their own noise") is not testable from the graphic. No noise band, no median lines, no annotation of the predicted ±1.6/±1.5 dB envelope in the right panel.
- [BLOCKING] The submitted caption block is corrupted: it opens mid-caption for Table~\ref{tab:dof} (DOF/kernel properties), includes the full tabular environment, and only then reaches the actual figure caption. The figure also includes `fig7_everyday_eeg.png` while the file under review is `figure7.png`. Resolve the file/label mismatch before submission.
- [ADVISORY] Curve lettering (a), (c), (b) out of order and used without panel letters; right-panel legend order is also inverted relative to the left.
- [ADVISORY] Legend statistics (95% = 4.9, 5.9 dB) appear to contradict the headline without explanation.
- [ADVISORY] The side-lobe/valley mechanism is asserted in text but unmarked in the figure.
- [ADVISORY] Left y-axis range dominated by one low-frequency outlier.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Add a shaded horizontal band at $\pm 4.3\sqrt{2/\nu}$ to the right panel (one band per comparison, or a single band at the tighter value), plus thin dashed lines at each median. This single change makes the figure self-sufficient.
- Reorder everything to (a), (b), (c) consistently and add explicit panel letters **A** (spectra) and **B** (differences), reserving (a)/(b)/(c) for estimators only.
- Strip the panel titles; move $\nu$ and half-power widths into the caption or cite Table~\ref{tab:dof}. Shorten legend entries to the estimator name plus $\nu$.
- Shrink the left panel (or make it an inset) and give the difference panel the majority of the width — it is where the claim lives.
- Mark two or three harmonic valleys in both panels with light vertical guides, or add an inset zoom on one harmonic-plus-valley, so the side-lobe explanation is visible rather than asserted.
- Clip the left y-axis to roughly 10–50 dB and note the sub-1 Hz excursion in the caption.
- In the caption, state explicitly that the *median* magnitude matches the predicted noise while the upper tail is concentrated in the valleys, so the 95% figures do not read as a contradiction.
- Repair the caption block and confirm the correct graphics filename.

### figure8.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** For a seizure recording, multitaper and box-smoothed-periodogram spectral estimates with comparable degrees of freedom are essentially interchangeable (median |difference| 1.5 dB), and both are far less noisy than the raw 2-s Hann periodogram.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the layout and titles get you ~80% of the way, but three things block a caption-free read: (i) it is not stated (only implied by one colorbar sitting beside panel d) that c, d and e share a single dB color scale, which is exactly what the equivalence claim rests on; (ii) the dashed vertical markers in (a) (blue/red) and in (c–e) (white) are never keyed to anything on-figure, so the link between (a), (f) and (g) must be inferred; (iii) panel (h)'s title refers to "(d) minus (e)" rather than to the estimators by name, forcing a lookup. A reader also cannot tell from the figure alone that ν denotes equivalent degrees of freedom, which is the quantity that makes the three panels comparable.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — spectrograms for time–frequency change, overlaid line spectra for estimator comparison at fixed instants, histogram for the residual distribution. One caveat: the central claim is "c, d, e are nearly the same," and three separate heatmaps are a weak instrument for demonstrating sameness — differences of 1–2 dB are invisible by eye across panels. A difference image (d − e, same axes, diverging colormap centered at 0) would show it directly, with (h) as its marginal distribution. Consider replacing one of the three spectrograms (or adding a fourth small panel) with that difference map.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Duplicated "dB" y-axis label on panel (g) (appears twice) — clear rendering bug.
- Panel (b): a 6-s zoom of the ictal rhythm is decorative with respect to the estimator-comparison claim; it supports "there is a seizure," which (a) already establishes.
- Unexplained artifact at ~365–400 s (flat/low-amplitude segment and vertical stripes in all spectrograms) draws the eye and is never addressed; either annotate it or crop it.
- Redundant x-tick labels on (c) and (d) given the shared time axis with (e); axis label "time (s)" appears only on (e), which is inconsistent with the tick labels being repeated.
- Colorbar tick labels (20, 40) carry no unit.
- Clipping at ±500 µV in (a) creates flat-topped spikes that read as data.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — (f) and (g) re-present, as slices, information that (c–e) already carry, and (h) reduces to a single number quoted in its own title. Panels (b) and, arguably, (f) are supplement candidates. Conversely, nothing here appears to duplicate another figure in an obvious way; the redundancy is internal.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Ambiguous color scaling: a single colorbar is attached to panel (d) only. If c, d, e share limits this must be stated on the figure; if they do not, the visual comparison underlying the main claim is invalid.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (g) has a duplicated "dB" axis label — an obvious defect that will be caught at proof stage.
- [BLOCKING] The markers linking (a) to (f)/(g) are unlabeled and change color between panels (blue/red vs. white), so the cross-panel correspondence is not readable without the caption.
- [ADVISORY] The 1.5 dB equivalence is asserted numerically but not shown spatially; a reader cannot verify *where* the two estimators disagree (edges of the ictal burst? low frequencies?).
- [ADVISORY] Panel (h) lacks a zero reference line, a median marker, and an indication of the tail extent; the histogram is also unnormalized ("pixels"), so its width is not comparable to anything.
- [ADVISORY] The ~365–400 s discontinuity/artifact is unannotated and could be mistaken for post-ictal suppression or an estimator failure.
- [ADVISORY] Legend in (f) occupies a substantial fraction of the panel and overlaps the low-frequency data; (g) has no legend, so the reader must shuttle between panels.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b) has no y-axis context beyond "µV" and no indication of which channel or where in (a) it comes from other than its title.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Make the shared dB scale explicit: one horizontal colorbar spanning c–e (or vertically centered against the stack), labeled "power (dB)", with a note "common scale" in the axis label.
- Fix the duplicated "dB" label in (g); add the (f) legend to (g) or place one shared legend outside both.
- Add small on-figure annotations for the two instants — e.g. tick-marks labeled "f" and "g" on the time axes of (a) and (c–e) — and use a single consistent marker color/style throughout.
- Replace or supplement one spectrogram with a (d) − (e) difference image on the same time–frequency axes using a diverging colormap centered at 0 dB and symmetric limits (e.g. ±5 dB); re-title (h) as "multitaper − smoothed periodogram" so it stands alone.
- Rewrite panel titles to name estimators rather than panel letters, and put ν values in a consistent bracketed suffix; define ν once in the figure (e.g. "ν = equivalent d.o.f.") or in the colorbar region.
- Drop tick labels on (c) and (d), keep one "time (s)" label under (e), and recover the space for the difference panel.
- Move panel (b) (and optionally (f)) to the supplement, or shrink (b) to an inset of (a).
- Annotate the ~365–400 s segment (e.g. "amplifier saturation / artifact") or exclude that interval.
- In (h), add a dashed line at 0, mark the median and, say, the 95th percentile of |diff|, and label the y-axis as a fraction of pixels.

### figure9.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: With matched degrees of freedom, multitaper and box-smoothed periodogram spectrograms render the descending harmonic stack of two seizures essentially identically, while the raw 2-df periodogram buries that structure in estimator noise.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — The panel titles carry the key parameters (ν = 2, 6, 4.8) and even flag the shading, which is unusually good practice, and a reader can see that (b) is speckled while (c) and (d) are smooth. But three things break without the caption: (i) nothing in the spectrograms marks where the two seizures are — the shaded windows in (a) are not carried down as vertical guides, so the reader must eyeball time alignment across four panels; (ii) the central claim of a *harmonic stack descending from ~20 Hz to ~3 Hz* is never annotated, so the reader sees warm blobs, not harmonics; (iii) the single colorbar sits beside panel (c) and it is ambiguous whether it governs (b) and (d) as well. Also the ±500 µV clipping in (a) is caption-only, yet the flat-topped transients at ~190 s and ~200 s clearly are clipped.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate — This is simultaneously a comparison of three estimators and a display of time–frequency evolution; stacked, time-aligned spectrograms sharing one x-axis and one color scale is the right instrument, and anchoring them to the raw trace in (a) is the correct move. One caveat: the claim that (c) and (d) "render it identically" is an equality claim, and side-by-side heatmaps are a weak way to demonstrate equality. A small fifth panel showing the (c) − (d) difference in dB (on a symmetric diverging scale) would prove the point far more convincingly than asking the eye to fail to find a difference.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- The `jet` colormap: its non-monotonic luminance manufactures ring-like contours around the cyan/yellow transitions, which in a figure whose whole subject is "real structure vs. estimator artifact" is actively hazardous. Perceptually uniform (viridis/magma) or, if warm-for-strong is required, `turbo`.
- The 500 s–600 s tail appears to carry no argument; the clip could likely be trimmed, buying vertical resolution for the seizures.
- Redundant y-axis tick labeling at 0–30 Hz on three identical axes is harmless but could be thinned to 0/10/20/30.
- Panel (a)'s ±250 µV ticks add little given the trace is clipped anyway.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — The caption's own "Spectrograms as in Fig. \ref{fig:eeg}" concedes that the three-estimator layout has already been established on an earlier clip. The incremental content here is narrow: a second clip, two events instead of one, and the harmonic-stack morphology. If the earlier figure already carried the ν-matching argument, this one is a confirmatory example and belongs in the supplement — unless the two-seizure aspect supports a claim the first clip could not (e.g. reproducibility of the descending stack across events within a patient), in which case say so explicitly in the caption and title.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] The headline morphological claim (harmonic stack, 20 Hz → 3 Hz over two minutes) is asserted in prose but not marked on the image. A reader cannot verify it from the figure.
  - [BLOCKING] Colorbar scope is ambiguous: positioned against panel (c), it may be read as applying to that panel only. If all three share one dB scale — which the comparison requires — that must be unmistakable.
  - [ADVISORY] Seizure intervals are shaded only in (a); no time alignment aids in (b–d).
  - [ADVISORY] The shaded windows (~50–190 s, ~300–450 s) are considerably wider than the "two minutes" of descending activity in the caption, so shading and text disagree about event duration.
  - [ADVISORY] `jet` colormap risks fabricating contour artifacts in a figure about distinguishing artifact from signal.
  - [ADVISORY] Clipping at ±500 µV is visible in (a) but disclosed only in the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] "Render it identically" is an equality claim shown only by visual similarity.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Annotate the harmonic stack in panel (c) or (d) only: a thin dashed line tracing the fundamental from ~20 Hz down to ~3 Hz, with tick marks or a bracket labeling the 2nd/3rd harmonics. Annotate one seizure, not both, and let the reader generalize.
  - Extend the seizure shading to (b–d) as faint vertical bands, or drop vertical dotted lines at the four onset/offset times through all four panels.
  - Move the colorbar to span the full height of panels (b)–(d), or add "shared dB scale, all panels" to its label.
  - Add "clipped at ±500 µV" as small in-axes text in panel (a).
  - Replace `jet` with a perceptually uniform map.
  - Add a (c) − (d) difference panel on a symmetric diverging scale, with the max absolute difference quoted in dB; this converts "looks the same" into a measurement.
  - Reconcile the shaded durations with the "two minutes" statement, or distinguish in the caption between full event duration and the duration of the descending phase.
  - Rewrite the caption's lead so it states the claim rather than the contents: e.g. "Matched degrees of freedom, matched picture: multitaper (ν = 6) and box-smoothed periodogram (ν = 4.8) recover the same descending harmonic stack in both seizures, which the ν = 2 periodogram obscures."
  - Decide the figure's role: if it is confirmatory relative to Fig. \ref{fig:eeg}, move it to the supplement; if it establishes cross-event reproducibility, make that the stated claim and title.

---

## Composition & Layout Inspector

### efigure1.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — full four-sided frames on both panels (top/right spines carry no information); light gridlines on both axes of both panels are acceptable but could be reduced to horizontal-only (or deleted) since the message is shape, not readout; the left panel's legend box adds a frame plus 5 line swatches that duplicate information obtainable by direct labeling. Curve ink itself is well-chosen (thin, distinct hues). Right panel has no legend at all, so the reader must carry colors across from the left panel — a lookup cost imposed by the legend rather than solved by it.

READING FLOW:     Problematic — the intended flow (linear view → dB view) is left→right and works, but the color key lives only in the left panel while the quantitative payoff ("skirts differ by 40 dB") is in the right panel, forcing a backtrack for every curve identification. Also the two panels use different x-ranges (±10 vs ±25) with no visual cue that the right panel is zoomed out, which momentarily misreads.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Legend frame + swatches in left panel (replaceable by inline labels).
  - Top and right spines on both panels.
  - Right panel's y-axis extends above 0 dB to ~+5, showing clipped curve tips that convey nothing; the empty strip above 0 dB is decorative headroom.
  - "sidelobes = leakage" title uses an equals sign as rhetoric rather than a label; plain "sidelobes (leakage)" suffices.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate overall, but unevenly distributed — the left panel's lower corners (|f|>6, kernel≈0) are large empty regions while the legend is jammed into the one region where four curves are steeply rising. Inter-panel gutter is wider than needed.

AXES:             Issues — left panel y begins at 0 (correct). Right panel is dB (log), so a non-zero baseline is justified; document the -100 dB floor choice. Right panel top limit should be clamped at 0 dB (peak value) rather than +5 with clipped peaks. The ±4/N reference lines in the right panel are unlabeled and fall between ticks; add a tick or an inline "±W" label. Left panel x-ticks at 2.5 intervals vs right panel at 10 — harmonize or explicitly mark the left panel's range as an inset of the right.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — appropriate, these are deterministic expected-value kernels. State in the caption that no variability is shown (expected values, not realizations) so absence is not read as omission.

PANEL LABELS:     Inconsistent — no A/B labels at all; descriptive titles ("main lobe", "sidelobes = leakage") stand in for them. Since the caption refers to "left" and "right", add bold **A** and **B** at the top-left of each panel and reference them in the caption.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — ~1.7:1 per panel suits functions of frequency; the left panel's flat-top comparison benefits from the extra width.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Replaceable — the dotted vertical lines at ±4 in the right panel are the right kind of annotation (reference line, no box) but need a one-word inline label. No callout bubbles or shadowed text boxes elsewhere. Good.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box — and it overlaps data. The 5-entry legend occupies roughly x ∈ [-2.5, 4.5], y ∈ [0.035, 0.115] in the left panel, which is exactly where the falling skirts of the red (multitaper), orange (Bohman) and green (Hann) curves and the dotted ideal-box edge at x = +4 pass through. The legend's opaque fill masks the right-hand transition region of at least three series and the ideal box's vertical edge — the very feature the panel exists to compare.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Legend box in the left panel overlaps and occludes plotted data (right-hand skirts of multitaper/Hann/Bohman curves and the ideal-box edge at x=+4).
  - [BLOCKING] Caption/figure mismatch: the draft caption supplied for efigure1.png is a table caption about ictal-minus-pre-ictal band powers; the figure shown is the kernel comparison whose true caption ("Kernels at the same design bandwidth", `fig1_kernels.png`) appears later in the source. Filename and caption must be reconciled before submission, and the panel titles (N=256, NW=4) must match the caption's parameters (N=256, W=4/N) in notation.
  - [ADVISORY] Right panel carries no series identification; colors must be recovered from the left panel.
  - [ADVISORY] Right panel curves are clipped at the top of the axes (~+5 dB) with no peak reference; the 0 dB normalization point is not visible as a landmark.
  - [ADVISORY] ±4 reference lines unlabeled in the right panel.
  - [ADVISORY] Non-data ink: top/right spines, dual-direction gridlines.
  - [ADVISORY] Different, unsignalled x-ranges between panels invite misreading of the left panel as the same span as the right.
  - [ADVISORY] y-label "kernel (linear, × N)" is cryptic; the normalization convention should be stated in words or in the caption.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Delete the legend box. Label the five series inline in the left panel where they separate most cleanly: place "multitaper, K=7" in red just above the ripple crest near x ≈ -1 (the red curve is the topmost there, y ≈ 0.14, leaving clean space above); "raw periodogram → box" in blue at the flat top near x ≈ 0 (it is the lowest plateau, y ≈ 0.118, label just below); "ideal box" in black beside the dotted plateau at x ≈ -3.2. For Hann and Bohman, which are nearly coincident on the plateau, label them in the right panel instead, where they diverge widely (green at x ≈ -18, y ≈ -50; orange at x ≈ -18, y ≈ -78), using short thin leader lines in the matching color. This eliminates both the occlusion and the cross-panel lookup.
  - Stagger the two skirt labels vertically by ≥15 dB and keep ≥1 text-height clearance from each curve so nothing clips at final column width; verify at the printed width, not on screen.
  - Clamp the right panel y-axis to [-100, 0] dB and state the normalization (peak = 0 dB) in the caption; this removes the clipped peaks and the empty headroom.
  - Label the ±4/N reference lines directly: a small "−W" and "+W" in black just above the x-axis at each dotted line, replacing any need for extra ticks.
  - Remove top and right spines on both panels; keep only horizontal gridlines (or drop gridlines in the left panel entirely, since the comparison is of shape, and keep the 20 dB horizontal lines in the right panel where the reader does read values).
  - Add bold **A** and **B** at the top-left of each panel, outside the axes, same font size and weight, and rewrite the caption to reference (A) and (B) rather than "left" and "right".
  - Signal the zoom relationship: either annotate the right panel with a light vertical span marking the left panel's ±10 range, or extend the left panel's x-range/shrink the right panel's so the two are comparable at a glance.
  - Narrow the inter-panel gutter and recover the width for the panels themselves; the left panel's near-zero tails beyond |f| ≈ 7 can be trimmed to about ±8 now that the legend no longer needs that space.
