  - Expand "kernel (linear, × N)" to state the normalization (e.g., "kernel, N·H(f) (linear)") and note in the caption that the curves are expected-value smoothing kernels, not spectral estimates, so the absence of error bars is understood as intentional.
  - Spell out "K = 2NW − 1 = 7" once in the caption rather than in a legend entry, so the inline label can stay short.

### efigure2.png

---
**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — the plot is enclosed in a full four-sided frame; top and right spines carry no information and should be erased. The legend has a bounding box and an opaque fill. Gridlines are appropriately light (lighter than the data), so those are acceptable. The largest single ink problem is the gray periodogram: it is high-variance, dense, and consumes roughly 40% of the ink in the figure while contributing only the qualitative message "raw periodogram is noisy" — it currently obscures the very floor comparisons the caption is about (which curve floors at −2, −8, −16, −20 dB).

**READING FLOW:** Natural — left→right frequency axis, peaks first, floors at right. The argument of the caption resolves at the right edge, which is also where the eye exits; good. The cost is the legend lookup in the upper right, which forces a jump back and forth between the right-edge curve separation and the legend.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Legend frame and fill (6 entries, boxed, upper right)
- Top and right axis spines
- Redundant encoding: the black dashed "true PSD" and the green "Hann, then box" trace essentially the same path from 0.25–0.5, doubling ink for one visual message

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate — the upper-right region the legend occupies is genuinely empty of data, so the legend is not stealing data space. Minor excess below −30 dB on the left half; the y-limit of −40 is set by a few periodogram spikes only.

**AXES:** OK — y-axis in dB is a log scale, so the zero-baseline convention does not apply and the choice of −40 to ~48 is defensible. x begins at 0 and ends at Nyquist (0.5), field standard. Tick density is reasonable. One note: "dB" alone as a y-label is under-specified; "power (dB)" or "PSD (dB)" would be better.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — acceptable for spectral estimates, but see ADVISORY: the central claim is about bias of the floor, and multitaper estimates have a known χ²₂K sampling distribution. A shaded ±1 jackknife/χ² band on the K=7 and K=6 curves (or a stated equivalent-degrees-of-freedom in the caption) would make the 10 dB bias claim quantitative rather than visual.

**PANEL LABELS:** Not applicable — single panel.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — roughly 1.9:1 for a frequency-domain trace; wider-than-tall is correct here and the peak structure near 0.11–0.14 is resolvable.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Replaceable — there are no callouts, but the caption's quantitative claims ("floors 20 dB high", "floors 10 dB high") are nowhere marked on the figure. These are exactly the annotations Tufte would want *in* the plot: a short horizontal reference tick or a direct dB value at the right edge, not prose in the caption that the reader must re-derive by eye.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — six entries in a framed box in the upper right. It does not overlap data (curves in that x-range sit between −20 and 0 dB), so this is not blocking. However, at x = 0.5 the five estimators are cleanly vertically separated (blue ≈ −2, red ≈ −8, orange ≈ −16, green/black ≈ −20 dB). This is an unusually good candidate for direct labeling: the separation at the right edge *is* the result. Two curves (true PSD and Hann-then-box) converge near −20 dB and would need vertical staggering or a short leader line.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Detached six-entry legend box on a line chart where the series separate cleanly at the right edge; imposes an unnecessary lookup cost. Recommend inline labeling.
- [ADVISORY] Legend order (true PSD, periodogram, raw box, Hann box, K=7, K=6) does not match the caption's narrative order (periodogram, raw box, K=7, K=6, Hann box). Reader must reconcile two orderings.
- [ADVISORY] Red (K=7) and orange (K=6) are adjacent hues used for the two curves the figure exists to contrast; in the dense floor region above x = 0.3 they overplot into an indistinct red/orange band. The 8 dB difference between them is the point of the figure and is hard to read.
- [ADVISORY] The gray periodogram overplots and visually swamps the smoothed estimates across the entire floor region (x > 0.25). Its variance is the context, not the result.
- [ADVISORY] Black dashed true PSD is partially hidden beneath the green Hann curve from 0.25–0.5; the reader cannot confirm the green curve tracks truth because truth is occluded.
- [ADVISORY] The quantitative claims (+20 dB, +10 dB floor bias) appear only in the caption, not as marks on the graphic.
- [ADVISORY] Non-data ink: legend frame/fill, top and right spines.
- [ADVISORY] y-axis label "dB" is ambiguous without the measured quantity.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Delete the legend box. Place inline labels at x ≈ 0.5, right-aligned just inside or just outside the axis, each in its series color: "raw, then box" at −2, "multitaper K=7" at −8, "multitaper K=6" at −16, "Hann, then box" at −20. Stagger "true PSD" slightly below −20 with a thin black leader line so it does not collide with the green label. Label "periodogram" in gray inline somewhere in the 0.30–0.40 region where the gray hash is dense and no estimator label sits.
- Drop the gray periodogram to a much lighter gray (alpha ≈ 0.25) and draw it first so all estimators sit on top of it; or move it to a thin companion panel above so the floor comparison is unobstructed.
- Re-color the two multitaper curves to a clearly distinguishable pair (e.g., dark red and a light/dashed variant of the same hue, or red vs. purple) so the K=7 vs. K=6 contrast is legible in the overplotted floor.
- Draw the true PSD last, on top, as a white-cased black dashed line so it remains visible where the Hann curve coincides with it.
- Encode the caption's numbers in the plot: add two thin unlabeled reference segments (or a small bracket with the value "+20 dB" and "+10 dB" set inline, no box, no fill) spanning from the true-PSD floor to the raw-box and K=7 floors near x ≈ 0.45.
- Remove the top and right spines; keep the light gridlines as they are.
- Raise the lower y-limit to about −32 dB and let the few periodogram spikes clip, recovering vertical space for the floor region where all the information lives.
- Change the y-label to "PSD (dB)" and reorder the series in any remaining list to match the caption's narrative order.
- Optionally add a shaded χ²-based uncertainty band (or state the equivalent degrees of freedom) for the K=7 and K=6 estimates so the bias claim is separable from sampling variability.

### efigure3.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — all three panels carry a full four-sided frame (top and right spines are pure non-data ink); the x-axis title "half-power bandwidth (1/N)" and its full tick set are repeated three times for what is a common axis; panel titles partially duplicate the y-axis labels ("variance: higher is better" over "equivalent dof (2K-like)"). Gridlines are appropriately lighter than the data, and markers-on-lines are justified because they mark the swept parameter values.

READING FLOW:     Natural — left→right progression (variance → leakage → side lobe) matches the caption's "Left / Center / Right" ordering. The only backtrack cost is the legend: the reader must return to panel 1's upper-left to decode colours while reading panels 2 and 3.

CHARTJUNK:
  - "(the 2015 whiteboard plan)" in the suptitle — informal working note, carries no analytical content; delete before submission.
  - Top and right spines on all three panels.
  - Triplicated x-axis label and tick labels.
  - Large legend box (8 rows, ~30% of panel 1's plotting area) sitting inside the data region.

WHITE SPACE:      Crowded in panel 1 (legend occupies the upper-left third of the axes), Excessive in the right ~35% of all three panels: the x-range runs to ~25 only because the single grey Welch point sits at 24.6, while every other series terminates near 16. Two-thirds of the horizontal ink budget carries one marker per panel.

AXES:             OK — left panel y begins at 0. Non-zero origins in the centre (log kernel mass) and right (dB) panels are field-standard and scientifically justified; dB and log-mass have no meaningful zero. x begins near 1 rather than 0, acceptable since bandwidth 0 is undefined. No inverted axes. Recommend documenting in the caption that centre panel is log-scaled.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — acceptable, these are deterministic kernel properties, not sampled estimates. Consider one clause in the caption stating that the quantities are analytic/deterministic so a reader does not wonder about missing uncertainty.

PANEL LABELS:     Inconsistent — no A/B/C labels anywhere; the caption refers to panels positionally ("Left: ... Center: ... Right:"). Positional reference breaks if the figure is re-flowed or panels are re-ordered in production.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — each panel is modestly wider than tall, suitable for these monotone trend curves; the 3×1 strip suits a two-column page width.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — no callouts or text boxes over the data. Panel titles are plain text above the axes and the "higher/lower is better" tags are genuinely informative; keep them.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box — detached 8-entry legend inside panel 1, with a visible frame. It does not overlap any plotted series (verified: the nearest curve at x≈5 sits near y≈10, well below the legend block), so this is ADVISORY rather than BLOCKING. Eight series exceeds the 2–5 range where full inline labelling is comfortable, but the curves separate cleanly at their right-hand endpoints in panel 1 (dof from ~14 to ~60) and in panel 3, so direct endpoint labels are feasible for at least the well-separated families.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Framed legend box consumes roughly a third of panel 1's plotting area and imposes a colour-lookup cost on all three panels.
  - [ADVISORY] "(the 2015 whiteboard plan)" is an internal working note in the figure title.
  - [ADVISORY] ~35% of the horizontal extent of every panel is empty, driven by a single Welch point at bandwidth ≈24.6.
  - [ADVISORY] No panel letters; caption relies on positional wording.
  - [ADVISORY] x-axis title and ticks duplicated three times on a common axis.
  - [ADVISORY] Top/right spines and partially redundant title/y-label pairs add non-data ink.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Retitle to "Resolution / variance / leakage per method, N = 256" and drop the parenthetical.
  - Replace the legend with direct labels at each curve's right endpoint in panel 1, coloured to match the series (e.g. "Welch (Hann, 50%)", "raw + box", "DPSS K=2NW−1", "sine tapers", "DPSS K=2NW−2", "Hann + Gaussian", "Hann + box", "Bohman + box"). Where the DPSS/sine-taper curves run close together near x≈15, stagger the labels vertically or add thin same-colour leader lines rather than compressing the text. If eight inline labels prove too tight, at minimum remove the legend frame (`frameon=False`) and move it outside the axes strip so it does not eat plotting area.
  - Reclaim the empty right third: either set x-limits to ~1–17 and annotate the off-scale Welch point inline (e.g. "Welch continues to 24.6, ν = 60"), or switch the shared x-axis to log scale, which also spreads the crowded 2–6 region where most curves bend.
  - Add bold **A**, **B**, **C** at the top-left of each panel, identically positioned and sized, and change the caption to "(A) ν. (B) kernel mass beyond twice the half-power half-width. (C) peak side lobe...".
  - Link the three x-axes and show the axis title once, centred beneath the strip; keep tick labels on all three panels only if the panels may be separated in production.
  - Remove top and right spines on all panels; keep gridlines at their current light weight.
  - State in the caption that the centre panel uses a logarithmic ordinate and that all plotted quantities are deterministic kernel properties (hence no error bars).

### efigure4.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — the structure is commendably lean (no gridlines, no 3D, no shadows, flat bar fills), but there is substantial redundant ink in the repeated scaffolding: the x-axis label "sample t" appears 8 times, "taper index k" 4 times, "frequency offset (1/N)" 4 times; y-tick labels (0.0–0.4 in row 3, −80–0 in row 4) are repeated on all four columns although the scales are identical and shared. All 16 panels carry a full four-spine box; top and right spines encode nothing. Rows 1–2 have y-axes with no ticks, no labels, and no scale — the spine itself is pure non-data ink there.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — the 4×4 matrix reads left→right by construction and top→bottom by quantity, and the column identity is stable down all four rows. One friction point: the rows are identified only by repeated per-panel titles, so the reader infers the row semantics rather than reading them. A single left-margin row label ("bank", "eigen-tapers of Q", "weights $c_k$", "kernel") would let you delete the 8 repeated "hidden tapers: eigenvectors of Q (k = 0..3)" / "eigen-weight ladder" strings and read the grid as a table.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Repeated identical per-panel titles in rows 2 and 3 (three of four are verbatim duplicates in row 2).
- Repeated axis labels and tick labels across columns sharing a scale.
- Top/right spines on all 16 panels.
- The grey 2W band in row 4 is *not* junk — it encodes the bandwidth and should stay.

**WHITE SPACE:** Crowded in the header region, excessive inside row 3. The three- and four-line column titles in row 1 crowd the panels and push them down; meanwhile row 3 wastes roughly a third of each panel's height (y-limit 0.45, data max 0.30) and, in columns (a) and (d), roughly half the plot width is empty because bars stop at k≈7 while the axis runs to 15. The empty width is defensible (it shows the bank is short), but the vertical headroom is not.

**AXES:** Issues —
- Row 3 correctly begins at zero; row 4's dB scale is a documented log convention; both fine.
- **Row 4 clips the data.** The kernel main lobes rise above the top y-limit and are cut off by the frame in all four panels — in (c) and (d) the mainlobe is sheared into two truncated branches with the peak invisible. If the kernels are peak-normalized the maximum should sit at 0 dB and be visible; as drawn the reader cannot see where the peak is or whether the panels are comparably normalized.
- Rows 1–2 have no y-scale at all. Given that traces are vertically offset this is acceptable practice, but the vertical offsets mean the panels cannot be read as amplitude; state "arbitrary vertical offset" once.
- Row 1(c): the top two traces collide with the upper frame and the orange trace appears clipped at x = 0.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — appropriate for deterministic windows and kernels.

**PANEL LABELS:** Inconsistent — (a)–(d) appear only on the four row-1 panels. The remaining 12 panels are unlabelled, so the caption's references to "(c)" and "(d)" are ambiguous as to which of four stacked panels is meant. Either label all 16 (a1…d4) or explicitly declare the column/row scheme in the caption's first sentence.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — rows 1, 2 and 4 are wider than tall, suiting the time- and frequency-domain traces; row 3's bar panels are the weakest, being taller than the data require (see white space).

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — no callout boxes, bubbles, or bordered text. Quantitative annotations (ν, half-power width, mass beyond 2W) are set as plain panel titles, which is the correct Tufte-style solution; they neither overlap nor compete with the data.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — no legend at all, which is worse than either alternative here. Four coloured traces per panel in rows 1–2 are supposed to be tapers k = 0…3, but nothing maps colour to k. With four series this is exactly the case for direct labelling: put a small coloured "k = 0", "k = 1", … at the right endpoint of each trace in the leftmost column only, in the trace colour. Note also a semantic colour collision: red denotes taper k = 3 in rows 1–2 but denotes the kernel in row 4.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Row 4: the kernel main lobes are clipped by the top of the axes in all four panels; the peak value is not visible and cross-panel normalisation cannot be verified.
- [BLOCKING] The suptitle says "three ways to build the bank from one window" but four constructions, (a)–(d), are shown. Text and figure disagree.
- [BLOCKING] Caption/figure mismatch: the caption attributes "the Slepian sequences" to (c) and "equal weights / any orthonormal basis" to (d), but the figure places the Slepian-and-equal-weights note in column (d) and the sinc-window construction in (c). The caption's (c)/(d) assignment must be corrected before submission.
- [BLOCKING] Panel letters are absent from 12 of 16 panels, so the caption's panel references are not resolvable.
- [ADVISORY] Four coloured traces per panel in rows 1–2 with no colour-to-k mapping anywhere in figure or caption.
- [ADVISORY] Row 1(c): traces collide with / are clipped by the panel frame at the top and at x = 0.
- [ADVISORY] Redundant axis labels (16 total across rows where 4 would do) and redundant y-tick labels on columns 2–4 of rows 3 and 4.
- [ADVISORY] Verbatim-duplicated panel titles in row 2 (three identical) and row 3.
- [ADVISORY] Excessive vertical headroom in row 3 (data max 0.30 against a 0.45 limit).
- [ADVISORY] Top and right spines on all 16 panels carry no information.
- [ADVISORY] Red serves two meanings (taper k = 3 in rows 1–2, kernel in row 4).
- [ADVISORY] Three- and four-line column titles in row 1 crowd the top of the figure; much of this text belongs in the caption.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Raise the row-4 y-limit (or renormalise each kernel to 0 dB peak) so the full main lobe is visible in all four panels; keep the limits identical across columns so widths are visually comparable.
- Fix the suptitle to "four ways" and correct the (c)/(d) assignment in the caption.
- Add panel letters to every panel, or replace per-panel titles with a single row of column headers "(a) … (d)" plus four left-margin row labels ("the bank", "eigen-tapers of $Q$", "eigen-weights $c_k$", "kernel"), and reference panels in the caption as (a1)–(d4).
- Label the four traces in rows 1–2 directly: coloured "k = 0…3" at each trace's right endpoint, leftmost column only. Change the row-4 kernel colour to black or grey to break the collision with k = 3.
- Share axes: keep x-tick labels and "sample t" / "taper index k" / "frequency offset (1/N)" on the bottom panel of each block only; keep y-tick labels on column (a) only in rows 3 and 4.
- Set row-3 y-limit to ~0.32 to recover the wasted headroom; the empty right-hand width is informative, so keep it but note in the caption that the common k-axis shows how many tapers each bank actually uses.
- Remove top and right spines throughout; in rows 1–2 remove the y-spine entirely and add "traces offset vertically; arbitrary units" once, in the caption.
- Move the long descriptive sub-lines of the row-1 column titles into the caption, keeping only a one-line header per column.
- Add a short leader line or nudge the row-1(c) traces down so nothing touches the frame.

---

### figure1.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — Full four-sided frames on both panels (top/right spines carry no information); two large opaque legend boxes with borders; panel titles that duplicate the caption verbatim; the heavy black (a) trace is much wider than (b) and (c), spending extra ink to obscure the two curves it is being compared against. Gridlines are appropriately light.

READING FLOW:     Problematic — Left→right panel order is natural, but the left legend lists the series as (a), (c), (b), so a reader tracking the labels must backtrack; the caption then re-lists them in the same non-sequential order. Also, the right-panel legend orders (c) before (b) while the y-axis label refers back to (a) defined only in the left panel.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Per-panel titles restating caption content ("8 s of EEG at 128 Hz, N = 1024, W = 4/N = 0.50 Hz" appears in both title and caption).
  - Right-panel title ("(c) equals (a) to machine precision; (b) converges to it as 1/L") duplicates the numbers already in the right-panel legend — the same fact stated three times (title, legend, caption).
  - Legend entries written as full descriptive sentences; the legend is functioning as a second caption.
  - Boxed legend frames and opaque white fills.
  - Top and right spines on both panels.

WHITE SPACE:      Excessive (right panel) / Adequate (left) — Right panel y-range is −0.20 to +0.20 dB while the data occupy roughly −0.06 to +0.01 dB: about 80 % of that panel is empty, and the orange (c) trace is visually indistinguishable from the zero line at this scale.

AXES:             Issues — Left y-axis starts at 15 dB rather than zero; this is defensible for a decibel (log power) scale but is not stated. Right y-axis is symmetric about zero and includes zero (good) but is far wider than the data warrant. x-axis is duplicated between panels with identical ticks and label; a shared x-axis would remove one label and one tick row.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — none required, but note the right panel is itself the uncertainty/agreement display, so no issue.

PANEL LABELS:     Inconsistent — No panel letters on either panel; the caption navigates by "Left:" / "Right:". Worse, (a)/(b)/(c) are used for *methods*, which will be read by most journal audiences as panel identifiers. This collision must be resolved.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — Wide panels suit the 0–40 Hz spectral axis; both panels are equal width, which correctly implies a shared abscissa.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Replaceable — The two legend boxes are the only annotation elements; both carry heavy borders and opaque fills. The right-panel legend's numeric claims ("max 2.7e-13 dB", "max 0.056 dB") would be better as short inline text placed next to each trace, or as the sole content of the caption.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box — Left panel: a three-entry boxed legend in the upper right. Its lower-left corner sits at roughly 14.7 Hz, 41.3 dB, while the (a)/(b)/(c) peak at ~16.3 Hz reaches ~40.2 dB. That is ~1 dB of clearance; at single-column publication width the box border will visually clip that peak. Right panel: two-entry boxed legend in a region that contains no data, so no overlap, but with only two series a detached box is unnecessary.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Method labels (a), (b), (c) collide with conventional panel labels in a two-panel figure that has no panel letters. A reader cannot tell whether "(b)" is a panel or a curve.
  - [ADVISORY] Left legend box clears the 16.3 Hz peak by only ~1 dB; will collide at final print scale.
  - [ADVISORY] Right panel y-limits (±0.20 dB) leave ~80 % white space and render the (c) − (a) trace visually identical to zero, defeating the panel's purpose.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel titles duplicate the caption; right title duplicates the right legend's numbers.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend order (a), (c), (b) forces backtracking.
  - [ADVISORY] Detached legend boxes on line charts with 3 and 2 series respectively — inline labeling would eliminate the lookup cost.
  - [ADVISORY] Top and right spines, and duplicated x-axis label/ticks, are non-data ink.
  - [ADVISORY] Left y-axis truncation at 15 dB is unexplained.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add bold panel letters **A** and **B** at the top-left of each panel (outside the axes), and rename the three methods to non-parenthetical tags — e.g. MT (multitaper), SM (smoothed periodogram), SW (slid sinc window) — so method names cannot be mistaken for panels. Update the caption accordingly.
  - Replace both legends with inline labels colored to match each series: in panel A, label the black trace "multitaper (all N Slepian tapers, weights λk)" on the flat 28–30 dB shelf near 20–24 Hz, and place "smoothed periodogram" and "slid sinc window" as short color-matched text at the right end (36–40 Hz) with thin leader lines, staggered vertically so they do not collide.
  - Tighten panel B to the data: y-limits of about −0.07 to +0.02 dB, or plot |difference| on a log y-axis so the 10⁻¹³ dB and 10⁻² dB residuals are both legible in one frame. Annotate each trace inline with its maximum ("max 0.056 dB", "max 2.7×10⁻¹³ dB") beside the curve, and drop the legend.
  - Delete both panel titles; the information already lives in the caption. Keep only a single short title if the journal requires one.
  - Reduce the (a)/multitaper linewidth to ~1.2 pt (or draw it as the widest trace but in a light grey underlay) so the two comparison curves remain visible where they agree; the point of the figure is coincidence, which reads better as three distinguishable thin lines than one thick line with slivers.
  - Remove top and right spines on both panels; share the x-axis so "frequency (Hz)" and its ticks appear once, beneath the two panels.
  - Add one clause to the caption documenting the truncated ordinate, e.g. "power axis truncated at 15 dB; dB scale is already logarithmic in power."
---

### figure2.png

---
**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — the data curves themselves are efficiently drawn (thin red estimate, heavy black truth, light grey reference spectrum all read clearly), but a substantial amount of non-data ink is duplicated across the 2×4 grid:
- Four-sided panel boxes on all eight panels (8 × 2 redundant spines); top and right spines encode nothing.
- The x-axis label "frequency (cycles/sample)" is printed four times in row 1 and "frequency offset (1/N)" four times in row 2. One label per row (or one centered under each row) suffices.
- y tick labels (40/20/0/−20/−40 and 0/−20/…/−80) are repeated on all four panels of each row even though the scales are identical. Keep them on the leftmost panel only.
- Gridlines are appropriately light and subordinate to the data — good.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — left→right progression (no taper → taper → correct smoothing → over-smoothing) matches the argument, and the kernel panel sits directly beneath the estimate it explains, so the column pairing reads without backtracking. One friction point: the two-line titles (method on line 1, ν on line 2) push the reader's eye up and out of the plot frame for a quantity that belongs inside the panel next to the blue width bar.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Redundant full bounding boxes on every panel.
- Two boxed legends that could both be replaced by direct labels.
- Double-shaded blue bands in (g) and (h): the light-blue and darker-blue overlap over the grey true-spectrum fill produces three ambiguous tones whose meaning is nowhere encoded on the figure itself (defined only in caption prose).
- Blue is used for the width bar and again, in two different opacities, for the |f|≤W shading, while carrying no series identity — a third color doing two unrelated jobs.

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate — panels are not crowded, margins are economical, and the 2×4 arrangement uses the canvas well. Minor excess: the double-height titles add vertical dead space above each top-row panel.

**AXES:** OK — dB (logarithmic) ordinate legitimately does not start at zero; ranges are shared and comparable across each row, which is essential here and correctly done. No inverted axes. Frequency axes correctly span 0–0.5 (Nyquist). Tick density is reasonable.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — defensible, since ν (equivalent degrees of freedom) is stated per panel and is the correct summary of estimator variance for a smoothed spectral estimate. However, ν is only interpretable by specialists; consider one short vertical chi-square 95% interval bar (length 10log₁₀ of the ratio of χ²ν quantiles) plotted in a blank corner of each top panel, labeled once. This converts an abstract number into a directly readable uncertainty and would satisfy reviewers who require a stated interval.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a)–(h) present on every panel, uniformly positioned at the start of each title, uniform size. They are, however, run into the descriptive title text in regular weight rather than set as standalone bold identifiers, which makes them slower to find when the caption cross-references them.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — each panel is roughly 430×340 px, close to square, which suits these frequency-domain traces; the wide overall canvas is justified by four columns. No distortion of peak sharpness.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — the blue half-power width bars with adjacent "width X/N" text are exactly the right kind of annotation: unboxed, unshadowed, inline, and placed in empty sky above the spectrum where they cannot collide with data. In (a) the bar is only a few pixels long and reads as a stray dot; give it a minimum rendered length or a small tick-mark form so it survives reduction to column width.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — and in one case overlapping.
- Panel (e): the boxed legend sits in the lower-left quadrant at roughly −70 to −80 dB, x ≈ −40 to −5, which is precisely where the Fejér kernel's sidelobe spikes reach the −80 dB floor. The legend frame occludes the red data it is describing.
- Panel (a): the boxed legend at lower-left sits in empty space below the noise floor, so it does not occlude, but it still imposes a lookup cost for only two series.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Legend box in panel (e) overlaps the red kernel's sidelobe structure in the lower-left region; plotted data is hidden behind the legend frame and its swatches.
- [ADVISORY] Detached boxed legends on both rows for only two series each; direct labeling would eliminate the lookup entirely.
- [ADVISORY] Redundant axis labels (×4 per row) and redundant y tick labels (×4 per row) across shared-scale panels.
- [ADVISORY] Full four-sided panel boxes on all eight panels.
- [ADVISORY] Meaning of the grey fill and of the light/dark blue |f|≤W bands exists only in the caption; the two blue opacities in (g)/(h) are not visually distinguishable in purpose and muddy against the grey.
- [ADVISORY] Blue width bar in (a) is too short to read as a bar at publication scale.
- [ADVISORY] ν reported in the title rather than inside the panel beside the width bar, separating two properties of the same kernel.
- [ADVISORY] Panel letters embedded in running title text in regular weight rather than set as bold standalone labels.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Move the panel (e) legend out of the data: replace it with inline labels — write "kernel" in red just to the right of the main lobe at about −15 dB, and "true spectrum (for scale)" in grey inside the grey fill near x ≈ +30, where the fill is broad and empty. Both labels then sit in their own color, requiring no swatch and no frame.
- In panel (a), replace the legend with "true PSD" in black placed along the smooth black curve near f ≈ 0.35 (above it, where the red floor is below) and "estimate" in red near f ≈ 0.45 just above the noise band, staggered vertically so the two labels do not align at the same y.
- Drop the x-axis label from all but one panel per row (or center a single label under each row) and delete y tick labels from panels b, c, d and f, g, h, keeping the shared scale implicit.
- Remove top and right spines on all eight panels; keep left and bottom only.
- Move ν inside each top panel, set in the same blue as the width bar and placed immediately beneath it, so kernel width and degrees of freedom read as one annotation: "width 8.2/N, ν = 8.9". Titles then reduce to one line, recovering vertical space.
- Lengthen the (a) width bar to a minimum of ~8 px or render it as a horizontal error-bar-style span with end caps, so a 0.8/N width is still visible after reduction.
- Collapse the blue shading to a single tone and mark |f|≤W with two thin vertical rules at ±W plus a small inline "|f| ≤ W" label in (g) only; the reader transfers the convention to (h). This removes the three-tone overlap ambiguity where blue crosses grey.
- Set panel letters as bold standalone labels at the top-left outside each axes frame, with the descriptive phrase following in regular weight, so caption cross-references land immediately.
- Optionally add one labeled χ² 95% interval bar in a blank corner of panel (a) (e.g., near f = 0.45, y = −35) and repeat unlabeled in (b)–(d), giving the variance reduction across the sequence a directly measurable visual length rather than only the numeric ν.

### figure3.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — Both panels are enclosed in complete four-sided frames; the top and right spines carry no information and can be erased (keep only the L-shaped axes, or use Tufte range-frames). Panel (a) has gridlines in both directions that are heavier than needed for a dense oscillatory trace, and the horizontal gridlines compete with the record itself; a single light zero reference line would do more with less ink. In panel (b) the marker density on the solid black curve (markers plus line plus dashed twin) is high — the markers are redundant with the line except where they mark actually-computed W values; if every plotted point is computed, thin the markers or drop them. The Hann taper is drawn twice (upper and lower envelope) — this is defensible as an envelope but is technically duplicated data ink.

READING FLOW:     Problematic — Both legends sit *below* their panels, so the reader must scan the plot, drop to the footer, decode three or four line styles, then jump back up. Two separate legend blocks means two lookup tables for a two-panel figure. Within panel (b) the flow is otherwise good (noise falls left→right, bias rises, minimum in the middle), and the "too noisy → just right → too smooth" annotations read left-to-right, which is excellent.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Detached legend boxes under both panels (replaceable by direct labels).
  - Full bounding boxes on both axes.
  - Dense horizontal gridlines in panel (a) that no reader will read a value off.
  - Redundant restatement of "(300 realizations)" in both the panel title and the caption.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate in panel (b); wasted in panel (a) — the upper-left, upper-right and lower-corner regions of panel (a) are empty and are the natural home for the three series labels, which would let the footer legend be deleted and the panels grow vertically.

AXES:             OK — Panel (b) y-axis (RMS error, dB) and x-axis (W) both begin at zero, correct for magnitude data. Panel (a) is symmetric about zero, appropriate for a zero-mean process. No inverted axes. Minor: panel (a) x-axis stops at ~1024 but the last tick is 1000; consider a tick or range-frame terminus at N = 1024 since that number appears in the title.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — and this is worth attention: the figure is built from 300 realizations, so there *is* a sampling distribution behind every plotted point, but only the RMS summary is shown. Not blocking (RMS over frequency is itself a summary statistic, not a mean±error), but the caption should state explicitly that no across-realization dispersion is displayed, or add a light shaded interquartile band around the two total-error curves.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — "(a)" and "(b)" are both embedded at the start of the panel title, same size and weight. Acceptable, though bold standalone labels at the top-left outside the axes are the more common journal requirement; check target journal style.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — Panel (a) is wide, correct for a 1024-point time series; panel (b) is near-square, correct for a trade-off/scatter-like relationship. The 2:1 width split between panels is sensible.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — The red callouts ("too noisy ν = 2", "just right ν = 9", "too smooth ν = 50") are unboxed, unfilled, unshadowed inline text tied to red circled points: this is close to textbook Tufte annotation. Two placement concerns: (i) "just right ν = 9" sits almost on top of the dotted "2W = peak width" guide line and only ~1 dB above the circled point, in the busiest part of the plot where the solid and dashed black curves converge — at final column width the text will crowd both the dotted line and the dashed curve; (ii) "too smooth ν = 50" is wedged in the narrow gap between the dashed curve above (~5.9 dB) and the solid curve below (~3.9 dB) and will clip one of them when scaled down. The "2W = peak width" / "2W = peak separation" labels on the dotted verticals are a good, box-free solution — keep them, but they currently hug the top frame; nudge them down slightly so they don't collide with the frame once the top spine is removed.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box — Both panels use detached legend boxes for 3 and 4 series respectively. Panel (b) especially invites direct labeling: "noise (std)" can be written in blue just above the blue curve near W ≈ 28–32 where it is flat and isolated; "bias (blur)" in orange along the orange curve near W ≈ 12–14 where nothing else is nearby; "whole band" and "peak region" can label the solid and dashed black curves at their right-hand endpoints (x ≈ 32), where they are cleanly separated by ~1.2 dB. Panel (a) can carry "record x_t" in gray in the upper left, "Hann-tapered record" in green in the upper middle, and "Hann taper (scaled)" in black just under the dashed envelope crest. Neither legend currently overlaps data, so this is advisory, not blocking.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Two detached legend boxes below the panels impose an avoidable lookup cost; all seven series are separable by colour/style and can be labeled inline.
  - [ADVISORY] "just right ν = 9" is crowded against the dotted "2W = peak width" guide and the converging black curves; risk of visual collision at publication width.
  - [ADVISORY] "too smooth ν = 50" sits in a narrow corridor between the dashed and solid black curves; will clip on downscaling.
  - [ADVISORY] Complete bounding boxes on both axes; non-data ink.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) horizontal gridlines are unnecessary and compete with a dense trace.
  - [ADVISORY] 300 realizations are summarized with no indication of across-realization spread; state this in the caption or add a light dispersion band.
  - [ADVISORY] The three red circles are referenced only by the caption ("panels (b), (c) and (d) of Fig. motif"); the reader must hold that mapping in memory. Label them on the figure.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Delete both legend boxes. Place coloured inline labels: panel (a) — gray "record x_t" upper-left, green "Hann-tapered record" upper-centre inside the envelope, black "Hann taper (scaled)" just beneath the dashed crest near t ≈ 500. Panel (b) — right-endpoint labels "total RMS error, whole band" (solid, black) and "total RMS error, peak region" (dashed, black) at x ≈ 32, staggered vertically; "noise (std), falls with W" in blue above the blue curve at W ≈ 27; "bias (blur), grows with W" in orange below the orange curve at W ≈ 13. Reclaim the footer space to make both panels taller.
  - Move "just right ν = 9" to the left of the dotted guide line (x ≈ 2–3.5) and add a short thin red leader line to the circled point, so the text clears both the guide and the dashed curve.
  - Move "too smooth ν = 50" above the dashed curve (y ≈ 6.3) with a thin red leader down to the circled point at (24, 3.3), or place it below the solid curve at y ≈ 2.6 where the region is empty.
  - Remove the top and right spines on both panels; drop panel (a)'s horizontal gridlines and keep a single hairline at x = 0 (y-value zero).
  - Append the Fig.-motif panel letters directly to the red callouts, e.g. "too noisy — ν = 2 (b)", "just right — ν = 9 (c)", "too smooth — ν = 50 (d)", so the cross-reference is resolved in the graphic rather than in the caption.
  - Thin or subsample the markers on the solid black curve so the line, not the marker field, carries the shape; keep markers only at the computed W values you want the reader to count.
  - In the caption, state explicitly what is and is not shown about variability, e.g. "curves show the RMS error of the ensemble mean over 300 realizations; across-realization dispersion is not displayed" — or add a shaded interquartile band and define it.
  - Drop "(300 realizations)" from the panel (b) title since the caption already carries it; the title then reads "(b) Hann taper, then box: error vs box width".

### figure4.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — four complete bounding boxes (all 4 spines on every panel) add non-data ink; top/right spines can be erased. Three detached legend boxes (6, 8, and 6 entries) carry opaque white fills and frames that duplicate information available by direct labeling. The top-right panel's gray band fill is heavy relative to the thin curves it qualifies; two light vertical reference lines at ±4 (or a thin bracket under the axis) would encode the same fact with far less ink. Bottom-right gridlines are appropriately subordinate — good. Top-left panel correctly suppresses a meaningless y-axis (offset traces) — good.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — 2×2 grid read left→right, top→bottom, and the caption ("Top left… Top right… Bottom left… Bottom right") matches that order exactly.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Redundant legend: top-left (k=0–5) and top-right (k=0–7) use the identical color→k mapping, so the same key is printed twice. One key (or direct labeling) suffices for both.
- Interpretive titles ("tapers beyond 2NW carry only leakage", "fills the box [-W, W]") restate the caption verbatim; ink spent twice on the same sentence.
- Bottom-right legend box for two reference lines that could be labeled in place.
- Legend frames/white fills on all three legends.

**WHITE SPACE:** Crowded in top-right (8 overlapping curves plus an 8-entry two-column legend in the same panel); Excessive in bottom-right, where the y-axis runs to 10⁻¹⁶ while the smallest plotted value is ≈2×10⁻¹⁰ — roughly the lower 40% of that panel is empty.

**AXES:** Issues —
- Bottom-right y-limit (10⁻¹⁶) is unjustified by the data; tighten to ~10⁻¹¹ or 10⁻¹². Log scale itself is appropriate for a leakage fraction and needs no zero origin.
- Top-right and bottom-left y-axes begin at zero — correct for power quantities.
- Top-left y-axis unlabeled/untick-marked is defensible (arbitrary vertical offsets) but the reader has no amplitude reference; a short vertical scale bar would restore quantitative meaning.
- Bottom-left and top-right share identical x-axes but are in different rows, so the duplicate axis label "frequency offset (1/N)" is unavoidable; no action needed.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — appropriate for deterministic analytic quantities.

**PANEL LABELS:** Not present — no A/B/C/D on any of the four panels; the caption relies on positional wording ("Top left"), which is fragile if panels are rearranged at typesetting.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — panels are near 4:3, suitable for these spectral shapes; the bottom-right ladder would read slightly better if the vertical range were trimmed (see AXES), which effectively improves its aspect.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Replaceable — the dashed black rectangle in bottom-left (the ideal box) is unlabeled and appears in no legend; it should carry an inline tag such as "exact box, |f| < W" placed just above its top edge. The gray band in top-right is an unlabeled annotation whose meaning lives only in the caption ("shaded"); label it "[-W, W]" in the panel.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — three detached legend boxes on line charts. The top-left legend is positioned inside the plotting area over the k=2/k=3 trace region (roughly sample 150–256); its opaque fill appears to occlude the k=2 (green) trace as it exits the right edge and crowds the k=3 (red) peak near sample 200. That is an overlap of legend on data. The bottom-left legend sits in the near-zero right shoulder region and touches the outer ripple of the red/purple curves. The top-right legend is over genuinely empty space and is the least harmful, but its 8 entries are the most costly lookup in the figure.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Top-left legend box overlaps plotted curves (k=2 green exiting right edge; adjacent to k=3 red maximum). Legend fill must not sit on data.
- [ADVISORY] Bottom-left legend box encroaches on the outer sidelobe ripple of the 7- and 9-taper curves; at final publication width the frame will clip those features.
- [ADVISORY] Duplicate k→color key printed in both top panels.
- [ADVISORY] Bottom-right y-axis extends six decades below the smallest datum, wasting ~40% of the panel.
- [ADVISORY] No panel letters; caption depends on spatial wording.
- [ADVISORY] Dashed reference box (bottom left) and shaded band (top right) are unlabeled within their panels.
- [ADVISORY] Panel titles duplicate caption sentences, including the interpretation.
- [ADVISORY] Full four-sided frames and heavy gray band fill lower the data-ink ratio.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Replace the top-left legend with inline labels: place "k=0" … "k=5" in the matching trace color immediately to the left of each curve at sample 0 (or to the right at sample 256), staggered to follow each offset baseline. This removes the overlap and the lookup cost at once. Widen the x-limit slightly (e.g., −25 to 280) to make room without touching the traces.
- In top-right, drop the legend entirely and label only the curves the argument needs — "k=0" at its central peak and "k=7" at its outer shoulder near f≈±3.5, each in series color with a short thin leader line. If all eight must be identified, keep one shared key outside the panel (figure-level, below the top row) rather than two per-panel keys.
- In bottom-left, move the legend outside the axes (to the right of the panel) or convert to inline endpoint labels: tag each curve at its central plateau height near f=0, where the curves are well separated vertically (0.06, 0.085, 0.10, 0.115, 0.12) — that is the natural reading position and requires no box.
- In bottom-right, replace the legend with rotated inline text set alongside each vertical line: "2NW" in red beside the dashed line at k=8, "K = 2NW−1" in gray beside the dotted line at k=7, staggered vertically so the two do not collide.
- Set bottom-right y-limits to (1e-11, 2) and drop the unused decades; keep the log scale.
- Add bold panel letters **A**–**D** at the top-left of each axes, same font size, consistent offset, and switch the caption to "(A)… (B)… (C)… (D)…".
- Replace the gray band in top-right with two thin dashed vertical lines at f = ±4 labeled "±W" in the panel, or keep a much lighter fill (alpha ≈ 0.08) and add the "[-W, W]" text label inside it.
- Label the dashed ideal box in bottom-left directly, e.g., "exact box" in gray just above its top edge near f = −6.
- Shorten panel titles to the object shown ("Slepian tapers, N=256, NW=4"; "spectral windows |U_k(f)|²"; "running sum of λ_k|U_k|²"; "leakage fraction 1 − λ_k") and let the caption carry the interpretation.
- Remove top and right spines on all four panels; retain only the two axes that carry ticks.
- Add a short vertical scale bar with numeric value to the top-left panel so the offset traces retain an amplitude reference.

### figure5.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — All eight panels carry a complete four-sided frame; the top and right spines encode nothing and can be erased (the curves and bars already delimit themselves). The top-row panels have no y-axis at all yet retain a left spine and full box, which is pure non-data ink. Redundant axis text: "sample t" is repeated four times and "taper index k" four times for identically scaled axes — one label per row suffices. Bottom-row x-ticks are rendered as 0.0/2.5/5.0/…/15.0 for a *discrete integer* index, adding decimal ink that cannot correspond to any datum. Faint horizontal gridlines appear only in the bottom row (acceptable weight, but inconsistent with the top row).

READING FLOW:     Problematic — Columns read left→right and the two rows pair correctly by vertical alignment, but only the *top* panels carry (a)–(d). The reader must jump back up to the top row to learn which method each bar chart belongs to, and the caption's "Bottom:" clause forces a second pass. Additionally, the three- and four-line panel titles push the data down and are read as prose before the graphics, inverting the intended see-then-read order.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Top/right spines on all eight panels.
  - Decimal tick labels on an integer-valued axis (bottom row).
  - Four repetitions of each axis label.
  - Titles duplicating caption content (e.g., "Hann segments, L = N/4, 50% overlap", "K = 2NW-1 = 7 Slepian tapers, equal weights") — these belong in the caption, not as 3–4 line headers.
  - The suptitle's restatement of $\hat S(f)=\sum_k c_k|v_k^Tx_f|^2$ duplicates the caption/body equation.

WHITE SPACE:      Crowded above, Excessive within panels — The band between the suptitle and the top panels is choked with stacked title text, while the bottom-row panels are ~50% empty: in (a) and (d) all weights vanish beyond k = 6, and in every panel the y-axis extends to 0.45 though only (b) exceeds 0.30. Roughly half the bottom-row plotting area holds no ink.

AXES:             Issues — Bottom row correctly begins at zero. Top row has *no* y-axis and no statement that curves are vertically offset by an arbitrary constant; a reader cannot tell that the offsets are not data. Bottom-row x-range (0–16) is set by N/16 rather than by the data, producing large empty regions; if the common range is deliberate (to make ladder length comparable across methods) that intent should be stated in the caption. Non-integer ticks on a discrete index axis are a convention violation.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — appropriate here (deterministic tapers and weights). Note that $\nu$ is itself an effective-DOF quantity; no uncertainty claim is made, so nothing is required.

PANEL LABELS:     Inconsistent — (a)–(d) appear on the top row only; the four bottom panels are unlabelled, so the caption's "Bottom:" items cannot be referenced individually (e.g., you cannot cite "the ladder of (c)" unambiguously in text). Style is also inconsistent: the letters are embedded in centred multi-line titles rather than set bold at a fixed top-left position.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — 1950×884 for a 4×2 grid gives roughly square-ish cells; the top-row waveform panels are suitably wide for the t-axis and the bar panels are near-square. Trimming the empty right half of the bottom panels would improve the effective ratio further.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Replaceable — No boxed callouts, which is good. However the four 3–4 line panel titles function as unbordered annotation blocks carrying method parameters; each could be reduced to a short label (e.g., "(a) Welch, Hann, 50% overlap") with the parameters moved to the caption. The $\nu = 13.4/11.1/17.2/14.0$ values in the bottom titles would read better as a single inline annotation placed inside each panel's empty upper-right region, where there is ample unused space.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box — none present, and none needed, but the *absence* of any labeling is a defect: the coloured curves in the top row are unidentified on the graphic. In (b) they are modulation frequencies f' = 0, 1/N, 2/N, 3/N; in (d) they are Slepian orders k = 0,1,2,3; in (a) they are seven segment offsets; in (c) four sliding offsets. The reader must consult the title text and infer top-to-bottom ordering. These are textbook candidates for direct inline labels at the right end of each curve, coloured to match the series. Note also that colour carries *different* meanings in different columns (blue = first offset in (a), k = 0 in (d)), which invites false cross-panel comparison.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Bottom-row panels have no panel letters; four of eight panels are unlabelled, defeating unambiguous cross-reference from caption and body text.
  - [BLOCKING] Top-row curves are vertically offset by an undocumented arbitrary constant with no y-axis and no note; a reader may read the offsets as amplitude data.
  - [ADVISORY] Coloured curves in the top row are unlabelled; series identity (offset index, modulation frequency, or Slepian order) must be inferred from title prose.
  - [ADVISORY] Colour encodes different variables in different columns, inviting spurious cross-panel matching.
  - [ADVISORY] Full bounding boxes on all panels; top/right spines are non-data ink.
  - [ADVISORY] Decimal tick labels (0.0, 2.5, …) on the discrete integer taper index k.
  - [ADVISORY] Axis labels "sample t" and "taper index k" repeated four times each.
  - [ADVISORY] Bottom panels ~50% empty: x-range to k ≈ 16 and y-range to 0.45 exceed the data in most panels.
  - [ADVISORY] Multi-line panel titles duplicate caption content and crowd the top of the figure.
  - [ADVISORY] Gridlines present in bottom row only — inconsistent treatment across rows.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption says "(c) … weighted by $\lambda_k$" but the bottom-row ordinate is labelled "weight (normalized)"; the relation between plotted $c_k$ and $\lambda_k$ (normalisation) is not stated.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add bold panel letters (e)–(h) at the top-left of each bottom panel (or relabel the grid as a1–d1 / a2–d2) and update the caption to reference them explicitly.
  - Label the top row's y-axis "taper amplitude (offset for clarity)" or add a short note; remove the left spine's ticks but keep the statement so offsets are not misread as data.
  - Replace the absent legend with inline right-end labels coloured to match each curve: in (b) "f' = 0, 1/N, 2/N, 3/N"; in (d) "k = 0…3"; in (a)/(c) "offset 1…7" / "offset 1…4". Reserve ~8% of the panel width at the right for the labels so no text clips a curve.
  - Consider greying all curves within a column except the one being emphasised, or use a single perceptually ordered colour ramp per column, so colour ordering (not hue identity) is the cue and cross-panel confusion is avoided.
  - Strip the top and right spines from all eight panels; keep only left and bottom.
  - Set bottom-row x-ticks to integers (0, 2, 4, …) with MaxNLocator(integer=True); shorten x-range to k = 0–12 (or to the largest nonzero k across panels, stated in the caption) and y-range to 0–0.32 to recover the empty half of each panel.
  - Keep one x-label per row ("sample t" under the top row, "taper index k" under the bottom row), centred across the four columns.
  - Compress panel titles to "(a) Welch", "(b) smoothed periodogram", "(c) sliding sinc", "(d) Thomson multitaper" and move all parameter detail (L = N/4, 50% overlap, σ = 3/N, K = 2NW−1 = 7) into the caption, which already carries most of it.
  - Move each $\nu$ value inside its bottom panel as inline text in the now-empty upper right, e.g. "ν = 13.4", instead of a separate title line.
  - Drop the suptitle equation (it duplicates \eqref{eq:ladder}'s neighbourhood in the text) or reduce it to the short phrase "three ways to build one bank"; state N = 256 and W = 4/N once, in the caption.
  - Apply gridlines consistently: either faint horizontal grids in the bottom row only with an explicit note, or remove them and rely on the four y-ticks.
  - In the caption, state the normalisation used for $c_k$ (e.g., $\sum_k c_k = 1$) so the "weight (normalized)" ordinate connects to the $\lambda_k$ weighting described for (c).

### figure6.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — Overall lean (light gridlines, shared y-labels, no 3D/shadows), but there is repeated non-data ink: the x-axis title is printed four times per row ("frequency (cycles/sample)" ×4, "frequency offset (1/N)" ×4), and every panel carries a full black bounding box (all four spines) plus duplicate tick labels on identical shared scales. Two boxed legends add frame ink that inline labels would eliminate.

READING FLOW:     Natural — Left→right across methods (a–d), top→bottom from estimate to kernel, with the kernel panel sitting directly beneath its estimate. The column pairing is the figure's strongest design feature. One friction point: the legends live in the leftmost panels, so a reader parsing panel (d) or (h) must jump back to column 1.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Boxed legend in (a) and in (e) — both replaceable by direct labeling.
  - Small blue square marker + "width 8.2/N" text acting as a mini-legend inside each top panel; a marker glyph is used where a drawn half-power bar would encode the width directly.
  - Top and right spines on all eight panels.
  - Redundant per-panel axis titles (8 instances of 2 unique strings).

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate — Panels are not crowded; the empty lower-right of the top panels is data-determined (the true PSD rolls off), not wasted. Inter-panel gutters are generous enough that column labels read cleanly.

AXES:             OK — dB (log) ordinate needs no zero origin; frequency abscissa starts at 0 in the top row and is symmetric about 0 in the bottom row, both field-standard. Limits are identical across each row, which is essential here and correctly done. Minor: the −40 dB floor in the top row is never approached by any curve, so ~10 dB of vertical range in four panels carries no data; tightening to −30 would enlarge the informative region.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — Acceptable for spectral estimates, but the figure reports ν (equivalent degrees of freedom) in every top title without stating anywhere in figure or caption that ν defines the χ²_ν sampling distribution from which a confidence interval follows. Since ν is the only uncertainty statement in the figure, its meaning must be defined in the caption.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a)–(h) present on all eight panels, all in the same position (leading the centered title) and same style. They are not bold and not set at the top-left of the axes; both are acceptable, but bold letters would separate the label from the descriptive title text.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — Each panel is modestly wider than tall (~400×330), suitable for these single-hump/decay traces, and the 4×2 grid fills a two-column page width sensibly.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Replaceable — The "width X/N" annotations are unboxed (good, no competing frame) and sit above the spectral peaks with clearance in all four panels. However, they communicate a width using a point marker, which is a category mismatch: a short horizontal bar of exactly that width drawn at the −3 dB level on the peak would show the quantity instead of asserting it.

LEGEND STYLE:     Inline — overlapping / Box — Two boxed legends on line charts. The (a) legend (bottom-left, "true PSD" / "estimate") sits in clear space and is safe, but with only two series it is pure lookup cost. The (e) legend is the real problem: it is placed bottom-left inside the axes and lies entirely on top of the gray "true spectrum near its peaks" fill, which is plotted data, and its top edge sits within a few dB of the red kernel trace (~−35 dB at f = −40, legend top ~−66 dB — close at final scale but the gray overlap is unambiguous).

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Legend box in panel (e) overlaps plotted data: the semi-transparent white frame is drawn over the gray true-spectrum fill, obscuring the very reference surface the panel exists to compare against. Legend boxes must not overlie data.
  - [BLOCKING] Undefined uncertainty quantity: ν appears in four titles with no definition (equivalent degrees of freedom of the χ²_ν distribution) anywhere in the caption. Journals treat an undefined dispersion/precision statistic the same as an undefined error bar.
  - [ADVISORY] The blue "|f| <= W" band is visible in (e), (f) and (g) but appears absent (or fully hidden) in (h). If it is genuinely omitted, the four kernels are no longer judged against the same reference interval; if it is merely buried under the wide Welch main lobe, it needs to be drawn on top or outlined so the comparison is preserved.
  - [ADVISORY] Legends appear only in column 1, forcing a leftward jump when reading (d) and (h); this is the standard cost of a detached legend.
  - [ADVISORY] Eight axis titles for two unique quantities; eight complete axis boxes.
  - [ADVISORY] The caption's most informative numbers — median errors 11.6, 4.6, 1.3, 0.8 dB, the figure's actual ranking of the four methods — exist only in prose. The reader must map four numbers onto four panels by counting.
  - [ADVISORY] Top-row y-range extends to −40 dB, roughly 10 dB below the lowest plotted value in any panel.
  - [ADVISORY] The "width X/N" marker glyph encodes a width as a point.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Delete the legend box in (e). Label the gray region inline in dark gray ("true spectrum, for scale") in the upper-right of the panel where the fill is broad and the red kernel is far away, and label the red trace "kernel" directly beside it at its left endpoint (f ≈ −38, y ≈ −35) in red. Annotate the blue band once with a small "W" at its upper edge. This clears the data and removes the frame.
  - Delete the legend box in (a). Write "true PSD" in black beside the black curve around f ≈ 0.40 (where it runs flat near −18 dB) and "estimate" in red beside the red trace around f ≈ 0.40 (where it sits near +2 dB); the two are ~20 dB apart there, so no leader lines are needed and no crowding occurs.
  - Define ν in the caption: add "ν is the equivalent number of χ² degrees of freedom of each estimate" after the method list.
  - Add each panel's median error into its own title, e.g. "(a) raw periodogram, then box W = 4/N — ν = 17.2, median error 11.6 dB", and reduce the caption sentence to a pointer. This puts the figure's key comparison where the reader is already looking.
  - Verify the "|f| <= W" band is drawn in (h); if it is occluded, plot it above the fill or replace it with two thin vertical rules at ±W in all four kernel panels so the reference is identical across the row.
  - Replace the square marker + "width X/N" text with a short horizontal bar spanning the measured half-power width, drawn at the −3 dB level across the peak, with the numeral set immediately above it in the same blue. The annotation then measures rather than asserts.
  - Print the x-axis title once per row, centered beneath the four panels; keep tick labels on all panels but drop the top and right spines everywhere. Keep y tick labels only on columns 1 (already done for the axis label — extend to the ticks).
  - Raise the top-row lower limit from −40 to −30 dB to recover vertical resolution in the region where the four methods actually differ.

### figure7.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — (1) Full four-sided box spines on both panels; top/right spines carry no information and their weight is comparable to the thinner blue trace. (2) Two opaque, bordered legend boxes hold long descriptive sentences (up to 55 characters) that duplicate caption text. (3) Left panel title packs six numbers ($\nu$ and three half-power widths) that are repeated verbatim in the caption; same for the right panel legend's median/95% values. (4) Gridlines are unobtrusive — acceptable. (5) Redundant "frequency (Hz)" label repeated on two panels sharing an identical x-scale.

READING FLOW:     Problematic — Legend order in the left panel is (a), (c), (b), forcing the reader to scan up and down to map labels onto curves; the right panel repeats the inverted order ((c) minus (a), then (b) minus (a)). Alphabetical/plotting order should govern. Panels otherwise read left→right correctly.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Opaque white legend patches with borders sitting on top of the plotting region in both panels.
  - Top and right spines on both axes.
  - Numeric summaries embedded in the legend/title rather than placed at the data or in the caption.

WHITE SPACE:      Crowded — the upper third of the left panel is entirely consumed by a three-line legend that competes with the tallest spectral peaks; the right panel's upper-right corner is likewise filled where the largest blue excursions occur.

AXES:             OK — power in dB is a log measure, so a non-zero origin is justified; the difference panel is centred on zero with an explicit zero reference line (good). Minor: right-panel y-limits (−17 to +13) are asymmetric about zero, which visually exaggerates the negative orange excursion at low frequency; consider symmetric limits (±13) with the low-frequency spike clipped and annotated, or state the asymmetry is data-driven.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — the caption's central claim is that the estimators "agree to within their own noise" ($4.3\sqrt{2/\nu}$ = 1.6, 2.1, 1.5 dB), but no uncertainty band is drawn. The right panel would support the argument far more directly with horizontal reference lines or a light shaded band at the predicted ±1.6 and ±1.5 dB noise levels.

PANEL LABELS:     Inconsistent — there are no panel identifiers (A/B) on either axes; the caption refers to "Right:". Compounding this, (a), (b), (c) are used for *series*, so adding A/B panel letters would collide. Recommend numbering panels 1/2 or labelling them "spectra" / "differences", or explicitly using "left"/"right" in caption and keeping (a)–(c) for curves only.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — wide-and-short suits 0–40 Hz spectra; panels are near-equal width, which is correct since both share the frequency axis.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Overlapping — the left legend box (spanning roughly 6.5–40 Hz, 37–48 dB) sits directly over the four tallest harmonic peaks (≈46–47 dB at 5.5, 8, 10.7 and 13.5 Hz), occluding the peak tops that are the primary comparison point of the figure. The right legend box (≈10–40 Hz, 8–13 dB) sits over the largest blue spike (≈12.5 dB near 11 Hz) and several 6–10 dB excursions.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box — Inline — overlapping risk if naively converted. Both panels are 2–3 series line charts, the canonical case for direct labelling. Left panel: place "(a) multitaper" beside the black trace in the flat 30–40 Hz region where curves separate least ambiguously is not ideal — better, label all three at the right edge (35–40 Hz) staggered vertically, since (c) orange runs ~2 dB above (a) and (b) there; or label (c) at the 6 Hz valley where orange is clearly lowest (~23 dB) and (b) at the 3.5 Hz shoulder where blue is clearly highest. Right panel: label the orange curve below zero around 27–30 Hz and the blue curve above zero around 30–33 Hz; the sign separation makes inline labels unambiguous with no leader lines needed.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Left-panel legend box occludes the tops of the four highest spectral peaks (~46–47 dB, 5–14 Hz) — data hidden behind non-data ink.
  - [BLOCKING] Right-panel legend box occludes the largest blue difference spikes (8–12.5 dB, ~9–12 Hz), precisely the excursions the caption discusses as "largest differences."
  - [BLOCKING] Undefined dispersion statistic: "95% 4.9 dB" / "95% 5.9 dB" is not identified as a percentile of |difference|, a CI, or an interval; state the definition explicitly in the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend entry order (a), (c), (b) does not match alphabetical or plotting order in either panel.
  - [ADVISORY] Detached legend boxes on 2–3 series line charts; direct labelling would remove the lookup cost entirely.
  - [ADVISORY] Left-panel title duplicates six numeric values already in the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Top and right spines and the duplicated x-axis label are non-data ink.
  - [ADVISORY] The caption's noise prediction (1.6/2.1/1.5 dB) is stated only in text; nothing in the graphic lets the reader verify agreement "within noise."
  - [ADVISORY] Line-weight hierarchy is inconsistent between panels: (a) is heaviest at left, (c) is heaviest at right, which invites misreading the emphasis.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Delete both legend boxes. In the left panel, place coloured inline labels at the right edge (~36–40 Hz), staggered vertically: "(c) Hann + box" above, "(a) multitaper" middle, "(b) Welch" below, each in its series colour; add thin same-colour leader lines only if a label would clip its trace at final column width.
  - In the right panel, label "(c) − (a)" in orange in the negative region near 28 Hz and "(b) − (a)" in blue in the positive region near 32 Hz; move the median and 95% numbers to the caption or to small inline text at the plot's left margin.
  - Add light horizontal reference lines (or a faint grey band) at ±1.6 dB and ±1.5 dB labelled "predicted estimator noise" in the right panel, so the figure demonstrates the caption's claim rather than asserting it.
  - Define the 95% statistic explicitly, e.g. "95th percentile of |difference| over 0.5–40 Hz."
  - Shorten the left panel title to a plain descriptor ("everyday estimators, same design bandwidth") and let the caption carry $\nu$ and the half-power widths.
  - Remove top and right spines on both panels; keep only the left and bottom. Suppress the left panel's x-axis label and tick labels only if panels are stacked; for side-by-side, retain both but reduce to a single shared label centred beneath the pair if the layout permits.
  - Make symmetric right-panel y-limits (±13 dB), clip the sub-1 Hz orange excursion, and annotate it inline ("−18 dB at 0.7 Hz") rather than stretching the axis for one outlier.
  - Fix legend/label ordering to (a), (b), (c) throughout, and use one consistent line weight convention across panels (e.g. reference estimator (a) always heaviest, or all equal).
  - Replace "Right:" in the caption with explicit panel identifiers that do not collide with the (a)–(c) series letters.

### figure8.png

---
**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — mostly economical (shared axes across (c)–(e), no 3D, no shadows), but several small drains: every panel carries a full four-sided bounding box; the light gridlines in (f)/(g) are close in weight to the thin grey periodogram trace and compete with it; the histogram bars in (h) carry black outlines on every bin, adding ink without information; the colorbar is unlabelled so its ink is under-used.

**READING FLOW:** Problematic — the layout mixes two reading orders. The left column runs (a)→(c)→(d)→(e) top-to-bottom while the right column runs (b)→(f)→(g)→(h); the reader must jump from (a) across to (b), back left to (c)–(e), then right again for (f)/(g), which reference the dashed lines in (a). Also, the time axes of (a) and (c)–(e) are not pixel-aligned (panel (a)'s frame is slightly wider and starts at t=0 while the spectrograms start at the first window centre), so vertical comparison of the seizure onset across panels requires eye-estimation rather than a straight vertical read.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Full rectangular frames on all eight panels (top/right spines carry no information).
- Bin outlines on all histogram bars in (h).
- Redundant title text: "seizure shaded" in (a) restates the caption; "(h) (d) minus (e)" nests parentheses awkwardly and reads as two panel labels.
- Rainbow (jet) colormap in (c)–(e) creates false luminance banding — decorative rather than informative gradation.

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate overall, but unbalanced: a large empty band sits between (b) and (f) in the right column and to the right of (c) where no colorbar is drawn, while (f)'s plotting area is crowded by the legend.

**AXES:** Issues — (1) dB axes in (f), (g), (h) do not start at zero, which is defensible for log-power and for a signed difference distribution, but should be stated; (2) **panel (g) has a duplicated y-axis label, "dB" printed twice**; (3) the colorbar in (d) has no unit label and no indication that it is shared by (c) and (e) — as drawn it appears to belong to (d) alone; (4) frequency axes of (c)–(e) are labelled but their x-tick labels are suppressed on (c) and (d) without an explicit note that the axis is shared (acceptable, but the x-limits must then match exactly); (5) (b) y-axis shows no +500 tick although (a) is described as clipped at ±500, so the clipping level is invisible in (b).

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — appropriate, but note that (f)/(g) show spectral estimates whose variability is the entire point of the figure; the effective degrees of freedom ν are given in the legend yet no confidence interval is drawn. A single vertical ν-based CI bar per estimator would make the smoothing/variance trade-off visible rather than asserted.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent in style (all lower-case letters in parentheses, same size, embedded in centred titles) but unconventional — labels are not at the top-left of each panel, so the reader must scan each centred title to locate the letter. (h)'s label collides semantically with the "(d) minus (e)" reference.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — wide panels for the time series and spectrograms, near-square for the spectra and histogram. (b) is somewhat tall for a 6-second waveform; widening it would let the individual ictal cycles be counted.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — the dashed instant markers and the shaded seizure interval are minimal and directly interpretable. Minor: the two dashed lines in (a) are not labelled with their t-values or with "f"/"g", so the link to the right-hand spectra depends entirely on the caption; the pink shading is a second encoding of the same event and could be reduced to a thin horizontal bracket beneath the trace.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — a four-entry legend box sits inside panel (f) at upper left, in the region where the low-frequency ends of all four curves reach ~43–45 dB; the "Hann periodogram" and "multitaper" entries appear to abut or overlap the leftmost portion of the traces. The legend serves (g) as well but is placed only in (f), forcing a look-back from (g).

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Panel (g) y-axis label is duplicated ("dB" printed twice) — a rendering defect that will be caught in production.
- [BLOCKING] Legend box in (f) overlaps (or sits flush against) the 0–5 Hz portion of the plotted spectra; a legend must not obscure data.
- [BLOCKING] Colorbar is unlabelled. A shared dB colour scale must state its unit and be identified as common to (c)–(e); otherwise the three spectrograms are not verifiably on the same scale.
- [ADVISORY] Time axes of (a) and (c)–(e) are not aligned, defeating the vertical comparison the layout implies.
- [ADVISORY] Legend appears only in (f) but is needed for (g); the reader must backtrack.
- [ADVISORY] Rainbow colormap in (c)–(e) is perceptually non-uniform and not colour-vision safe; the apparent "ridge" structure during the seizure may be partly a colormap artifact.
- [ADVISORY] No indication of estimator variability in (f)/(g) despite ν being the figure's subject.
- [ADVISORY] Panel letters buried in centred titles; (h)'s title nests "(d) minus (e)" inside its own label.
- [ADVISORY] Non-zero baselines on dB axes undocumented in the caption.
- [ADVISORY] Panel frames, gridlines, and histogram bar outlines add ink without information.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Delete the duplicated "dB" on (g); set a single shared y-label, and consider a common dB range for (f) and (g) so the pre-ictal/ictal difference is read as a shift rather than a rescaling.
- Remove the legend box entirely and label the four estimators inline at their right-hand endpoints in (g), where the curves separate most cleanly (multitaper ≈38 dB, Welch ≈37, Hann+box ≈36, raw periodogram ≈29 at 30 Hz), each label in its series colour and staggered vertically. Then (f) needs no legend at all and its upper-left data is freed.
- Move the colorbar to a single horizontal bar spanning the full width beneath (e), or place a vertical bar centred on the (c)–(e) block, labelled "power (dB)", with a note "common scale, (c)–(e)".
- Force identical x-limits and identical axes positions for (a) and (c)–(e) (shared-x with a common left/right margin), and extend the two dashed instant markers as thin vertical lines through (c)–(e) — as already done — while annotating them in (a) directly as "247 s (f)" and "318 s (g)" instead of relying on the caption.
- Replace jet with a perceptually uniform sequential map (viridis or magma); the ictal ridge will then be a genuine luminance feature.
- Add a single vertical bar in (f) and (g) showing the ±1 CI half-width implied by each ν (e.g. three short bars at 28 Hz, colour-matched), turning the ν values in the caption into visible information.
- Move panel letters to bold, top-left, outside each axes frame; shorten titles to the estimator description only ("Hann periodogram, 2-s windows, ν = 2"), and retitle (h) as "difference, (d) − (e); median |diff| = 1.5 dB".
- Drop top and right spines on all panels; lighten or remove gridlines in (f)/(g), or keep only a faint horizontal grid at 10-dB intervals drawn beneath the traces; remove bin outlines in (h) and use a single mid-grey fill.
- Replace the pink seizure shading in (a) with a thin horizontal bracket or bar below the trace, and add a +500 tick to (b) so the stated ±500 µV clipping is visible where it matters.
- In the caption, state explicitly that dB axes are truncated (not zero-based) and that the colour scale is common to (c)–(e).

### figure9.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — (1) all four panels carry full four-sided bounding boxes; top/right spines on the spectrograms carry no information and compete with the dense image data. (2) The y-axis label "frequency (Hz)" plus its full tick set is repeated three times for three identical axes; one label (or a single label centered on the b–d stack) would suffice. (3) The `jet`-family rainbow colormap is a data-ink liability, not an asset: its non-monotonic luminance manufactures apparent contour bands (notably the cyan→yellow transition near 25–30 dB) that are artifacts of the palette, not of the spectrum. A perceptually uniform map (viridis/magma) would show the harmonic stacks with less false structure.

READING FLOW:     Natural — top→bottom stack with a single shared time axis at the bottom is the correct arrangement; the eye moves from raw trace to progressively smoothed estimates as the caption narrates.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Top and right spines on all four panels.
  - Duplicate y-axis labels/ticks on (b), (c), (d).
  - Colorbar outline box (the color ramp itself defines its extent).

WHITE SPACE:      Crowded — the four panels are pressed together with the panel titles sitting directly on the axis line of the panel above (the "(b) Hann periodogram…" title nearly touches the bottom spine of (a); same for (c) and (d)). Add ~4–6 pt of vertical padding, or move the titles inside each panel as small top-left labels. Conversely there is a wasted vertical gutter to the right of panel (a) where the colorbar column is empty.

AXES:             Issues — **panel (a) is not aligned with panels (b)–(d)**. Because the colorbar is attached only to the spectrogram axes, panels (b)–(d) are ~30 px narrower on the left and ~20 px narrower on the right than panel (a). The entire analytical claim of this figure is temporal correspondence between the shaded seizures in (a) and the descending harmonic stacks in (b)–(d), and that correspondence cannot be read by dropping a vertical line. Otherwise: time and frequency both begin at zero; the ±500 µV clip is documented in the caption; no inverted axes.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — appropriate for spectrogram images. The equivalent degrees of freedom (ν = 2, 6, 4.8) are stated in each panel title, which is the right uncertainty disclosure for this figure type; consider noting in the caption that ν is the chi-square equivalent dof so the reader knows what it quantifies.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a)–(d) present on every panel, uniformly styled and centered above each axes. Centered titles are less scannable than top-left labels, but consistency is satisfied.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — wide-and-short panels suit a 600-s record; the ~1:4 panel aspect keeps the 20→3 Hz descent visible without vertical exaggeration. Panel (a) is somewhat thin relative to the spectrograms; a modest height increase would make the two seizure envelopes more legible.
