ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — no callouts, text boxes, or brackets; parameter annotations live in the titles where they belong.

LEGEND STYLE:     Inline — clean (no detached legend; the shared colorbar is correctly labeled "dB" and serves all three spectrograms with a common scale).

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (a) x-extent does not match panels (b)–(d), so the shaded seizure intervals do not align vertically with the corresponding spectrogram features. This defeats the figure's central comparison.
  - [ADVISORY] Seizure intervals are shaded only in (a). The reader must estimate their horizontal position in (b)–(d) by eye.
  - [ADVISORY] Rainbow colormap introduces false banding and is not colorblind-safe; it also makes the "renders it identically" claim for (c) vs (d) harder to verify than a uniform map would.
  - [ADVISORY] Triplicated y-axis labels and tick sets; four-sided bounding boxes on all panels.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel titles collide with the axis line of the panel above.
  - [ADVISORY] Channel name is "FP1" on the y-axis label but "Fp1" in the caption — pick one (Fp1 is the 10–20 standard).
  - [ADVISORY] Colorbar tick sequence begins at 15 dB with no round anchor; the reader cannot tell whether the color limits are shared-but-clipped or data-driven.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Force a common x-axis geometry: build the stack with `sharex=True` and reserve the colorbar gutter for *all four* rows (e.g., `gridspec` with a dedicated colorbar column, or `make_axes_locatable` on panel (a) with an invisible cax). Then every vertical position corresponds to the same instant across all panels.
  - Repeat the seizure intervals in (b)–(d) as two thin vertical rules (or a 2-px shaded band along the top edge of each spectrogram) so onset/offset can be read against the harmonic descent without leaving the panel.
  - Switch to `viridis` or `magma`, state the vmin/vmax explicitly in the caption, and confirm the three spectrograms share identical limits.
  - Delete top and right spines everywhere; keep "frequency (Hz)" on panel (c) only (or as a single rotated label centered on rows b–d) and retain tick marks without labels on (b) and (d) — or retain all ticks and drop only the duplicate text.
  - Add vertical padding between panels (`hspace≈0.35`) or convert titles to small bold top-left in-panel labels, e.g. "**b** Hann periodogram, 2-s windows, ν = 2".
  - Increase panel (a) height by ~30% so the two seizure envelopes and the interictal baseline are distinguishable.
  - Harmonize "Fp1" between axis label and caption; define ν in the caption as the chi-square equivalent degrees of freedom per spectral estimate.

## Color & Accessibility Auditor

---
PER-FIGURE:

**efigure1.png** (kernels at equal design bandwidth)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — each hue = one estimator (multitaper, raw+box, Hann+box, Bohman+box) plus black dotted ideal box. No decorative fill.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Borderline — 4 hues + black. Acceptable in count, but hue is the *only* channel distinguishing four overlapping curves in the right panel.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    At risk — the primary categorical contrast in the right panel is red (multitaper) vs green (Hann+box); these are the two curves that must be compared against each other and they converge/cross near the band edge. Under deuteranopia/protanopia they become near-identical mustard tones. Green vs orange (Hann vs Bohman) is a second at-risk pair. Under achromatopsia all four are mid-grey and unresolvable.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Needs work — this is unmodified matplotlib `tab10` in default draw order, i.e. aesthetic-by-default rather than chosen. Replace with Okabe-Ito (blue #0072B2, orange #E69F00, bluish-green #009E73, vermillion #D55E00) and add dash patterns.

**efigure2.png** (AR(4), dropping the leakiest taper)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — grey = periodogram, black = truth, then one hue per estimator.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Excessive — 4 saturated hues + black + grey = 6 encoded levels in one busy panel.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    At risk — red (K=7) vs green (Hann+box) again carries the paper's key claim (the 10 dB noise floor vs the correct floor); they sit within 10 dB of each other at high frequency. Orange (K=6) vs red (K=7) is also a low-contrast pair and is exactly the comparison the caption asks the reader to make.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Needs work — `tab10` default. Since the message is "two of these floor high, two do not," consider 2 accents (one for the leaky pair, one for the correct pair) with linestyle separating members.

**efigure3.png** (resolution / variance / leakage sweep)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — 8 methods, 8 hues.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Excessive — 8 distinct hues (blue, orange, green, red, purple, brown, pink, grey) is double the recommended ceiling, and the same 8-way legend must be decoded three times.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    At risk (severe) — red (raw+box) vs green (sine tapers) vs brown (Bohman+box) collapse into one family under deuteranopia; purple (Hann+box) vs blue (DPSS K=2NW−1) and pink (Hann+Gaussian) vs grey (Welch) also merge. Markers are identical circles everywhere, so there is no redundant encoding.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Needs work — `tab10` beyond its usable size. Either (i) group methods into 3–4 families (multitaper / tapered+smoothed / untapered / Welch), one Okabe-Ito hue per family, distinguished within family by marker and dash; or (ii) small-multiple the panels by family with all other curves in light grey.

**efigure4.png** (four constructions in full)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Partly decorative — in rows 1–2 the curves are vertically offset and identified by position/ordering, so the blue/orange/green/red cycle is redundant with the offset; it is harmless but not encoding anything a reader retrieves. Kernel row (single red) and weight bars (single grey) are appropriately neutral.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Good — 4 cycled hues + 1 accent + grey, and the 4 are not compared against each other.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    Yes (low stakes) — no distinction in this figure is lost if red/green merge, because taper identity is carried by vertical position. Would recommend switching to a sequential ramp anyway since k is an *ordered* index.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Needs work (minor) — taper index k = 0..3 is ordinal; a 4-step sample of `viridis` or `cividis` would encode order, which `tab10` does not.

**figure1.png** (three routes, one estimate)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — black = reference (a), orange = (c), blue = (b); difference panel reuses the same two hues.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Good — exactly 2 accents plus black.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    Yes — orange/blue is the canonical safe pair and survives deuteranopia, protanopia and (via luminance offset) grayscale. Model figure for the rest of the paper.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Good — effectively Okabe-Ito blue/orange. Keep.

**figure2.png** (kernel motif)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — red = estimate/kernel, black = truth, grey = true spectrum for scale, blue = bandwidth annotation (bar and shading). Four roles, four treatments.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Good — 2 accents (red, blue) + neutrals.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    Yes — red vs black differ strongly in luminance and weight; red vs blue vs grey all separable under all three simulations.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Good — principled role-based assignment. Only nit: the two blue shading levels in (h) are close in value; nudge the alpha apart.

**figure3.png** (choosing the bandwidth)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — (a) grey record vs green tapered record vs black dashed taper; (b) black = total, blue = noise, orange = bias, red = annotation callouts.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Borderline — 4 hues across the two panels (green, blue, orange, red) where 2 accents plus red annotation would do.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    At risk (mild) — the green tapered record over the grey raw record in (a) is a green-vs-neutral contrast that loses most of its distinction under deuteranopia and nearly all of it in grayscale (similar mid-luminance); the green trace also lies inside the grey trace, so there is no positional cue. The red circled callouts against a black curve are fine.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Needs work (minor) — green appears only here and nowhere else in the paper. Recolor the tapered record to the paper's orange accent (or make the raw record much lighter grey) and keep red strictly for annotation.

**figure4.png** (why the multitaper kernel is a box)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — hue = taper index k in all four panels; red dashed = 2NW, grey dotted = K=2NW−1.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Excessive — top-right legends 8 hues (k=0..7), top-left 6, bottom-left 5 + black; the figure carries ~8 distinct hues total.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    At risk — in the top-right panel the overlapping spectral windows are identified *only* by hue, and k=2 (green) vs k=3 (red) vs k=5 (brown), and k=4 (purple) vs k=6 (pink), are the pairs a reader must separate to see "each filling the gaps left by the previous ones." This is the figure most damaged by red-green deficiency. Bottom-left "first 5 (green) vs first 7 (red)" is the second instance.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Needs work — k is strictly ordered, so a categorical palette is the wrong family. Use `viridis` (or `cividis`, which is CVD-optimised) sampled at k = 0..7; order then reads directly as dark→light, and the "filling the gaps" narrative becomes visible in grayscale. Also change the red 2NW line to black dashed and keep grey dotted, so the annotation layer is neutral.

**figure5.png** (three ways to build a bank)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Partly decorative — as in efigure4, the top-row curves are offset and ordered, so hue is redundant; bars are neutral grey (good).
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Good — cycled 4 hues + grey.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    Yes — no load-bearing distinction depends on hue.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Needs work (minor) — same recommendation as efigure4: sequential ramp for an ordered index, and keep it identical to efigure4 (this figure and efigure4 are the same construction at two levels of detail).

**figure6.png** (everyday estimators on the AR record)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — identical role scheme to figure2 (red estimate/kernel, black truth, grey spectrum, blue |f|≤W shading).
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Good — 2 accents + neutrals.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    Yes.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Good — and commendably consistent with figure2.

**figure7.png** (everyday versions on EEG)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — black (a) multitaper, orange (c) Hann+box, blue (b) Welch; difference panel reuses the same two hues.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Good — 2 accents + black.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    Yes — blue/orange/black.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Good — matches figure1's scheme, which is the right instinct (see cross-figure note on what the letters mean, though).

**figure8.png** (three estimators of a seizure)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Mixed — spectrogram colour encodes dB (meaningful); the red trace in (b) encodes nothing (it is the same channel as the black trace in (a), recoloured for emphasis only) and the red/blue dashed markers in (a) are a third colour language not reused in (f)/(g). Line colours in (f)/(g) are meaningful and match figure7.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Excessive overall — jet spectrograms (full rainbow) + red trace + pink shading + blue/red dashed markers + grey/black/orange/blue line set in one figure.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    At risk (severe, in the spectrograms) — `jet` is the principal problem: it is non-monotonic in luminance, so under achromatopsia the colorbar folds (yellow and cyan map to similar greys as the extremes do not), and under deuteranopia the red-to-green half of the ramp compresses, destroying the ictal/pre-ictal contrast that panels (c)–(e) exist to show. The cyan-vs-green and orange-vs-red bands, where the harmonic stack lives, are the worst affected. Additionally the blue dashed (pre-ictal) vs red dashed (ictal) marker pair in (a) is a red-green-family pair used as the only categorical cue.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Needs work (blocking) — replace `jet` with `viridis`, `magma` or `cividis` for all three spectrograms; jet also manufactures false edges (apparent contours at the cyan/green and yellow/orange transitions) that could be misread as spectral structure. Make (b)'s trace black like (a)'s, or if emphasis is wanted, keep it black and shade its extent in (a). Note also the duplicated "dB dB" axis label near panel (g)/the colorbar.

**figure9.png** (two seizures in one clip)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — spectrogram colour = dB; pink shading = seizure extent.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Excessive — full rainbow ramp again.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    At risk (severe) — same `jet` problems as figure8. The caption's central claim ("the two smoothed estimators render it identically, the unsmoothed one renders it through noise") depends on fine texture discrimination in the blue-cyan region, which is where jet's luminance is flattest and where deuteranopes lose the most.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Needs work (blocking) — `viridis`/`magma`/`cividis`.

CROSS-FIGURE:
  - **Same estimator, different colour.** The multitaper estimate is **red** in efigure1 and efigure2 but **black** in figure1, figure7 and figure8(f,g). The Hann-periodogram-then-box estimate is **green** in efigure1/efigure2 but **orange** in figure7 and figure8(f,g). The untapered-periodogram-then-box estimate is **blue** in efigure1/efigure2 but **orange** in figure1. Welch's method is **grey** in efigure3 but **blue** in figure7 and figure8(f,g). A reader tracking "Hann + box" across the paper sees it change hue three times.
  - **Letter labels are consistent but the underlying method is not.** figure1 and figure7 both use black=(a), orange=(c), blue=(b), which is good discipline — but (b) means "sliding sinc average" in figure1 and "Welch, 9 Hann segments" in figure7, and (c) means "raw periodogram + box" in figure1 and "Hann periodogram + box" in figure7. Colour is therefore keyed to the *route* (multitaper / average / smooth) rather than to the estimator; that is defensible but must be stated, or else it reads as an inconsistency against efigure1/efigure2 where colour is keyed to the estimator.
  - **Red has three incompatible jobs.** Red = "the estimate under discussion" (figure2, figure6), = "multitaper" (efigure1, efigure2), = "annotation/callout" (figure3's circles, figure4's 2NW line, figure8's dashed marker and (b) trace). Reserve red for exactly one role.
  - **Two palette styles coexist.** figures 1, 2, 6, 7 are a disciplined black/grey + orange/blue + red-accent family; efigures 1–3 and figure4 are raw `tab10` rainbow; figures 8–9 are `jet`. The set does not read as one paper — the jet panels in particular look like they come from a different decade of software than figure1.
  - **Spectrogram colour scales are not comparable.** figure8's colorbar spans roughly 10–45 dB (ticks at 20, 40) while figure9's spans roughly 13–43 dB (ticks 15–40). Since both are jet, the same colour denotes different dB in the two figures, and the two seizure clips cannot be compared by eye. Either fix a common vmin/vmax or state explicitly that scales differ.
  - **Green appears only twice** (figure3's tapered record, and as a method colour in efigures 1–3), with no shared meaning; it can be eliminated entirely.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] `jet` colormap used for all five spectrogram panels (figure8 c–e, figure9 b–d). Non-monotonic luminance makes the panels unreadable under achromatopsia, compresses the red-green half under deuteranopia/protanopia, and introduces false contour artefacts that can be mistaken for spectral structure. These panels carry the paper's empirical claim.
  - [BLOCKING] Red-vs-green is the primary categorical contrast for the paper's key method comparison in efigure1 (multitaper vs Hann+box), efigure2 (K=7 vs Hann+box), efigure3 (raw+box vs sine tapers vs Bohman) and figure4 top-right (k=2 vs k=3 vs k=5). Hue is the only encoding channel; these distinctions vanish for ~8% of male readers and in any grayscale reproduction.
  - [BLOCKING] Cross-figure colour inconsistency: multitaper (red↔black), Hann+box (green↔orange), raw+box (blue↔orange), Welch (grey↔blue) each change hue between the supplementary and main figures.
  - [ADVISORY] efigure3 uses 8 distinct hues with identical markers in three panels — beyond any palette's discriminable capacity.
  - [ADVISORY] figure4 and efigure4/figure5 use a categorical palette (`tab10`) for the ordered taper index k, discarding the ordering information that the figures are trying to convey.
  - [ADVISORY] Spectrogram dB limits differ between figure8 and figure9, so identical colours mean different powers.
  - [ADVISORY] Red is overloaded across the set (estimate / multitaper / annotation).
  - [ADVISORY] figure3(a): green tapered record over grey raw record is low-contrast in grayscale and under deuteranopia; figure8(b): red trace is purely decorative recolouring of the same signal shown in black in (a).
  - [ADVISORY] Duplicated "dB dB" axis label adjacent to figure8(g)/colorbar.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Adopt one Okabe-Ito mapping, fixed for the whole paper, keyed to the *estimator* and stated once in the first figure caption. Suggested: multitaper = black (thick); untapered periodogram / raw+box = vermillion #D55E00; Hann periodogram (± box) = orange #E69F00; Welch / sliding-window average = blue #0072B2; Bohman = bluish-green #009E73; sine tapers = reddish-purple #CC79A7; raw periodogram (single realisation) = light grey; true PSD = black dashed. Apply identically in efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, figure1, figure7, figure8(f,g).
  - Replace `jet` with `viridis` (or `cividis` for maximum CVD safety, or `magma` if a dark-background feel is wanted) in figure8(c–e) and figure9(b–d), and set a single shared `vmin`/`vmax` across both figures so the two clips are directly comparable. State the shared range in both captions.
  - Add a redundant non-colour channel wherever more than two series overlap: dash patterns in efigure1's right panel and efigure2, and distinct marker shapes (circle/square/triangle/diamond/×) in efigure3 so every curve is identifiable in grayscale.
  - Restructure efigure3 from 8 equal curves to 3–4 method families (multitaper, tapered+smoothed, untapered+smoothed, Welch), one hue per family with within-family variation by dash/marker; or split into small multiples in which the highlighted family is coloured and all others are light grey.
  - Recolour the ordered taper index k in figure4 (top-left, top-right, bottom-left) and in efigure4/figure5 (top rows) with a sequential `viridis`/`cividis` sample, so k reads as dark→light and the "each taper fills the previous gaps" argument survives grayscale. Change figure4's red 2NW line to black dashed.
  - Reserve red/vermillion for exactly one meaning. Recommend: red = "the estimator being illustrated / its kernel" (as in figure2 and figure6). Convert all annotation reds to black or dark grey: figure3's circled callouts, figure4's 2NW line, figure8(a)'s dashed instant markers (use two distinguishable neutral dash patterns, or label them "pre-ictal"/"ictal" directly on the axis).
  - In figure3(a), drop green: lighten the raw record to ~20% grey and draw the tapered record in the paper's orange. In figure8(b), draw the zoomed trace in black to match (a).
  - Clarify in the figure1/figure7 captions that (a)/(b)/(c) and their colours denote the three *routes* (multitaper / average / smooth), not fixed estimators — or relabel so that colour tracks the estimator consistently with efigure1–2.
  - Run the final PDF through a CVD simulator (e.g. `daltonize`, Coblis) and a desaturate-to-grayscale pass; every legend entry should remain resolvable in all four renderings.
  - Increase the alpha separation of the two blue shading levels in figure2(h) so the W and 2W regions are distinguishable when printed.

## Typography & Annotation Inspector

---
PER-FIGURE:

**efigure1.png** (1430×520 px)
    FONT:               Consistent — single sans-serif throughout (appears to be matplotlib's default DejaVu Sans, not Helvetica/Arial); mathtext elements match the body sans face, so no true mixing.
    SIZE:               Too small — legend entries in the left panel are the smallest text (~10 pt nominal at ~100 dpi export ⇒ ≈4.8 pt when placed at 6.875 in double-column width; ≈2.3 pt at single column). Tick labels ≈4.8 pt, suptitle ≈5.8 pt. All below the 6 pt floor.
    ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: NW (appears in the in-figure title but not expanded/related to N and W in the caption), K (only implicitly "K=2NW-1=7"), W (used in "ideal box, half-width W" — defined in caption as W=4/N, acceptable), "dB" (conventional, acceptable).
    AXIS LABELS:        Missing/ambiguous units — "frequency (1/N)" is not a unit; should read "frequency (units of 1/N)" or "frequency ×N (cycles/sample)". "kernel (linear, × N)" is cryptic; "kernel (dB)" lacks a reference level (dB relative to what? cf. figure2's "dB re peak").
    STAT ANNOTATIONS:   Not present.
    TITLE PLACEMENT:    Incorrect (inside figure) — the suptitle "Expected-value smoothing kernels at equal design bandwidth (N=256, NW=4)" is a figure title and duplicates caption content. Also note: the draft caption text supplied for this figure is the **band-power table caption**; the actual kernel caption appears only at the end of the pasted block (caption/figure mismatch).

**efigure2.png** (1040×546 px)
    FONT:               Consistent — same sans face as the rest of the set.
    SIZE:               Borderline adequate — tick labels ≈6.6 pt at 6.875 in (smallest: legend text, ≈6.6 pt). Would be ≈3.1 pt if reduced to single-column; must be placed full width.

    TITLE PLACEMENT:    Acceptable (panel headers only).

CROSS-FIGURE:
  - **Typeface**: all 13 figures use the same sans-serif face, but it appears to be matplotlib's default DejaVu Sans rather than Helvetica/Arial. Consistent, but not the preferred publication face.
  - **Effective font size varies widely between figures.** All figures appear to use the same nominal point sizes (≈10 pt ticks / 12 pt titles) but are exported at very different pixel widths, so after scaling to a fixed column width the rendered sizes differ by nearly a factor of two: efigure2 ≈6.6 pt (largest) vs efigure4 and figure5 ≈3.5 pt (smallest). Equivalent elements will not match on the printed page.
  - **Axis-label naming for the same quantity is inconsistent**: y-axis for spectral power is variously "dB" (efigure2, figure2, figure6), "power (dB)" (figure1, figure7), "kernel (dB)" (efigure1), "kernel (dB re peak)" (figure2, figure6). Only two figures state a dB reference.
  - **Unit-in-parentheses convention broken inconsistently**: "difference from (a), dB" (figure1, figure7) uses a comma, whereas every other axis uses parentheses.
  - **Normalised-frequency unit is written three different ways**: "frequency (1/N)", "frequency offset (1/N)", "box half-width W (units of 1/N)". Only the last is unambiguous.
  - **Math notation register is mixed across figures**: proper symbols (λ_k, ν, Ŝ(f), σ, ≤ implied) in efigure1/efigure4/figure2/figure5/figure7, but ASCII in figure4 ("1 - lambda_k", "|U_k(f)|^2") and figure6 ("|f| <= W").
  - **Spacing around "=" is inconsistent**: "N=256, NW=4", "K=2NW-1=7" (efigure1, efigure2, figure4) vs "N = 256, W = 4/N", "K = 7" (efigure4, figure5, figure6, figure8, figure9).
  - **Capitalization of in-figure titles is mixed**: lower-case sentence fragments ("main lobe", "sidelobes = leakage", "variance: higher is better", "(a) periodogram, no taper: leaky and noisy") vs capitalised sentences ("Three routes, one estimate: …", "The everyday versions: …", "Expected-value smoothing kernels …", "Resolution / variance / leakage per method …", "AR(4) process, …"). Axis labels are uniformly lower case (good) except "RMS error (dB)".
  - **Figure titles inside the figure** in efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, efigure4, figure1, figure3, figure4, figure5, figure7 — 9 of 13 figures. figure2, figure6, figure8, figure9 use panel headers only and are the correct model.
  - **Terminology drift for the same object**: "sidelobes" (efigure1 title) vs "peak sidelobe" (efigure3) vs "side lobe" (captions); "raw periodogram" (efigure1, efigure2, figure1, figure6) vs "untapered periodogram" (captions, figure6 panel a title says "raw"); channel "FP1" (figure9) vs "Fp1" (caption); "AR(4)" vs "AR4" (efigure2 legend).
  - **Duplicate content**: figure5 is a strict subset of efigure4 with an identical suptitle; ensure the two are labelled so readers can tell them apart without reading both captions.
  - **Caption/source mismatch**: the LaTeX supplied for efigure1 and for figure7 is table caption text (band-power table; tab:dof) with the true figure caption appended. Verify caption-to-figure mapping before submission.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Font sizes fall below the 6 pt absolute floor in 11 of 13 figures at double-column width (6.875 in). Estimated smallest text: ≈3.5 pt (efigure4, figure5), ≈3.8 pt (efigure3, figure2, figure6), ≈4.1 pt (figure1, figure3, figure7), ≈4.4 pt (figure4), ≈4.8 pt (efigure1), ≈5.0–5.1 pt (figure8, figure9). Only efigure2 (≈6.6 pt) clears the floor, and only if placed full width.
  - [BLOCKING] figure8 panel (g): the y-axis label "dB" is printed twice, overlapping — a plotting bug (duplicate `set_ylabel`/twin axis) that must be fixed.
  - [BLOCKING] Overall figure titles are embedded inside 9 figures (efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, efigure4, figure1, figure3, figure4, figure5, figure7), duplicating caption text. Journal style requires the bold title in the legend only.
  - [BLOCKING] efigure3's in-figure title contains the informal phrase "(the 2015 whiteboard plan)" — internal lab shorthand that cannot appear in a published figure.
  - [BLOCKING] Undefined abbreviations used in figures and not expanded in any caption: EEG, PSD, DPSS, dof, AR(4)/AR4, std, NW (as a compound parameter), C4/FP1 (electrode positions), ELROND.
  - [BLOCKING] Missing y-axis labels: figure4 top-left and bottom-left panels, figure5 top row (taper amplitude); bare "dB" y-axes with no quantity in efigure2, figure2, figure6, and no dB reference level anywhere except figure2/figure6 bottom rows.
  - [ADVISORY] Font family is matplotlib's default DejaVu Sans rather than Helvetica/Arial.
  - [ADVISORY] "frequency (1/N)" and "frequency offset (1/N)" state a scaling, not a unit; inconsistent with figure3's correct "(units of 1/N)".
  - [ADVISORY] ASCII math ("1 - lambda_k", "|U_k(f)|^2", "|f| <= W", "2x") mixed with proper mathtext elsewhere in the set.
  - [ADVISORY] Inconsistent capitalisation of in-figure titles and inconsistent spacing around "=" across figures.
  - [ADVISORY] Channel-name capitalisation mismatch: "FP1" in figure9 vs "Fp1" in its caption (10–20 convention is "Fp1").
  - [ADVISORY] figure8: a single colorbar adjacent to panel (d) serves panels (c)–(e) without saying so; panels (c)/(d) lack an x-axis title while retaining tick labels.
  - [ADVISORY] figure9: panel (a) horizontal extent is not aligned with the spectrogram panels below it.
  - [ADVISORY] efigure1 and figure7 caption sources supplied are table captions; verify caption-to-figure assignment.
  - [ADVISORY] figure7 legend uses an undefined "95%" summary statistic.
  - [ADVISORY] No statistical significance markers appear anywhere in the set — nothing to define, but confirm none were intended.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Set a single global style for all 13 figures and re-export: `rcParams` with `font.family='sans-serif'`, `font.sans-serif=['Helvetica','Arial']`, `font.size=8`, `axes.labelsize=8.5`, `axes.titlesize=9`, `xtick.labelsize=7.5`, `ytick.labelsize=7.5`, `legend.fontsize=7.5`, and set `figsize` to the **final printed inches** (6.875 in wide for double column, 3.25 in for single) with `dpi=600`, rather than exporting large pixel canvases and scaling them down. This fixes both the sub-6 pt problem and the cross-figure size mismatch in one step.
  - Split or re-lay-out the two densest figures: efigure4 (4×4 grid at ≈3.5 pt) should become two stacked figures (banks + eigen-tapers; ladders + kernels), and efigure3's three panels should be stacked vertically or given a shared external legend, so that 7–8 pt type fits.
  - Delete every suptitle and convert the informative content into the bold caption title: e.g. efigure4/figure5 → "**Three ways to build a bank of tapers from one window.**"; figure1 → "**Three routes, one estimate.**"; efigure3 → "**Variance, leakage and side-lobe level versus measured resolution.**" Retain only short panel headers, and remove "(the 2015 whiteboard plan)".
  - Expand on first use, in the caption of the first figure in which each appears: EEG (electroencephalogram), PSD (power spectral density), AR(4) (fourth-order autoregressive), DPSS (discrete prolate spheroidal sequence, i.e. Slepian), RMS (root mean square), ν (equivalent degrees of freedom), NW (time–bandwidth product), K (number of tapers), L (segment length in samples), Q (quadratic-form matrix), U_k (spectral window of taper k), λ_k (eigenvalue / concentration). Replace "dof" with "ν" and "std" with "standard deviation".
  - Standardise axis labels across the set, sentence case with units in parentheses: "frequency (Hz)" / "frequency (cycles per sample)" / "frequency offset (units of 1/N)"; "power spectral density (dB re µV² Hz⁻¹)" for all EEG panels; "power (dB re unit variance)" for simulated panels; "kernel (dB re peak)" for all kernel panels; "difference from (a) (dB)"; "amplitude (µV)"; "count (pixels)". Add the missing taper-amplitude labels as "taper amplitude (a.u., offset for display)".
  - Replace ASCII math with mathtext everywhere: `$1-\lambda_k$`, `$|U_k(f)|^2$`, `$|f|\leq W$`, `$2\times$`; and adopt one spacing convention for equations in titles (recommend spaces: "N = 256, W = 4/N, K = 7").
  - Fix the duplicated "dB" y-label in figure8(g); label the shared colorbar "power spectral density (dB re µV² Hz⁻¹)" and add a note in the caption that it applies to panels (c)–(e); add "time (s)" to the bottom-most spectrogram only and remove intermediate tick labels for a clean shared axis.
  - Harmonise names: use "side lobe" (two words) everywhere, "untapered periodogram" everywhere (not "raw"), "AR(4)" everywhere, and "Fp1"/"C4" in both figures and captions.
  - Add a short "Abbreviations" sentence at the end of each caption, or define all symbols once in the first figure caption and cross-reference ("Symbols as in Fig. 1").
  - Re-check the caption-to-figure mapping for efigure1 and figure7; the supplied LaTeX contains table captions.

## Technical Format Checker

### efigure1.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     1430 x 520 px (aspect 2.75:1) — recommended **double** column (181 mm / 7.16 in) — **200 DPI** at that width. (At single column 88 mm / 3.5 in it would be 409 DPI; using the alternate 3.25 in / 6.875 in conventions: 440 DPI and 208 DPI respectively.) Physical height at double column: ~66 mm — well within the 230 mm limit.
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (two line graphs with grids, legend, tick labels). 200 DPI at the required double-column width vs. 300 DPI minimum / 600 DPI preferred. Only a single-column placement would clear 300 DPI, but a 2.75:1 two-panel figure at 88 mm gives a panel height of ~32 mm and unreadable axis text, so single-column is not a viable rescue.
FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG (acceptable for review only) → vector PDF or EPS for final submission; TIFF at ≥600 DPI if raster is unavoidable.
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.13 MB (limit 10 MB).
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick labels, titles and legend entries are bitmap text from a PNG export; pixel edges will be visible at print size, and the smallest text (legend entries, tick labels) is the most affected.
COLOR MODE:     OK for IEEE (RGB assumed for PNG; no color_mode field was supplied — confirm it is RGB, not indexed/palette). Note that the saturated green (#2ca02c-like), orange and red traces are the classes most prone to shift if the production workflow converts to CMYK; the red/green pair is also a concern for color-deficient readers since the traces are distinguished by hue only.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution is 200 DPI at double-column width — below the 300 DPI minimum and far below the 600 DPI preferred for line art.
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text and rasterized line work in a line-art figure; re-export as vector.
  - [BLOCKING] An overall title is typeset inside the figure ("Expected-value smoothing kernels at equal design bandwidth (N=256, NW=4)"). IEEE TBME requires no in-figure titles; this information belongs in the caption.
  - [BLOCKING] Caption/figure mismatch: the draft caption supplied for efigure1.png describes band powers from seizure clips and is followed by a `tabular` environment (it is the caption for Table `tab:bandpower`). The kernel figure's actual caption text appears later, attached to `\includegraphics{fig1_kernels.png}`. Filenames also disagree (efigure1.png vs fig1_kernels.png). Resolve before submission.
  - [ADVISORY] No lowercase panel labels. The two panels carry in-figure subtitles ("main lobe", "sidelobes = leakage") instead of (a) and (b).
  - [ADVISORY] Parameter notation is inconsistent between the in-figure title (NW=4) and the caption (W=4/N); with N=256, NW=4 implies W=4/256, so the caption's "W = 4/N" is the correct reading — unify on one form.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting: these curves are expected-value (deterministic, analytic) kernels, so no error bars are needed — but this must be stated explicitly, otherwise a reader may assume they are estimated from realizations. Where the text compares noise levels (Table `tab:dof`, ν = (tr Q_f)²/tr Q_f²), the degrees of freedom per estimator and the number of Monte-Carlo realizations used for the stated agreement should be given in the caption or table note.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend/tick text is likely to fall below the 6 pt floor once the figure is scaled to 181 mm; measure at final size. Legend is also placed over the lower-left data region of the left panel and partially crowds the rising edge of the Bohman/Hann traces.
  - [ADVISORY] Right panel y-axis is truncated at 0 dB, so the main-lobe peaks of the red and green/orange traces run off the top of the axes; the vertical dotted band-edge lines at ±4/N are clipped by the frame.
  - [ADVISORY] Traces are distinguished by color only; no line-style or marker redundancy.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from matplotlib as vector PDF or EPS at a figure width of 7.16 in (`figsize=(7.16, 2.6)`), with `rcParams['pdf.fonttype']=42` / `ps.fonttype=42` so fonts embed as vector Type 42. If a raster deliverable is required, export TIFF at ≥600 DPI for the 7.16 in width (≥4300 px wide, LZW compression).
  - Delete the suptitle from the figure; move "Expected-value smoothing kernels at equal design bandwidth (N = 256, W = 4/N)" into the caption.
  - Replace the two subplot titles with lowercase panel labels (a) and (b) placed at the upper-left outside the axes, and describe "main lobe, linear scale" / "sidelobes (leakage), dB scale" in the caption text.
  - Set all text to a sans-serif face (Arial/Helvetica) at 8–9 pt for axis labels and 7–8 pt for tick and legend text, measured at 181 mm final width; verify nothing renders below 6 pt.
  - Move the legend outside the plotting area (e.g., a single horizontal legend below both panels, or to the right of panel b) so it does not overlap the kernel skirts; consider one shared legend for both panels.
  - Extend the right panel's y-limits to about +5 dB (or annotate that the peaks are clipped at 0 dB) so the main-lobe tops and the full ±4/N band-edge markers are visible.
  - Add line styles (solid / dashed / dash-dot / long-dash) on top of color, and consider swapping the red/green pair for a colorblind-safe set; this also insulates the figure against any CMYK conversion shift in the green and orange traces.
  - Fix the caption/filename bookkeeping: give efigure1.png the kernel caption, and add a sentence stating that the plotted kernels are exact expected-value (deterministic) quantities with no sampling variability, together with the K = 2NW − 1 = 7 taper count and the degrees of freedom of each estimator being compared.
  - Confirm the PNG is true RGB (not palette-indexed) and export the final file in RGB.
---

### efigure2.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     1040 x 546 px (aspect 1.90:1) — recommended **double column** — 151 DPI at 6.875 in (145 DPI at the 181 mm / 7.16 in IEEE double-column width). At single column: 320 DPI at 3.25 in (297 DPI at the 88 mm / 3.5 in IEEE spec).
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (dense spectral traces, text, gridlines); 145–151 DPI at the recommended double-column width vs. 300 DPI minimum / 600 DPI preferred. Even the single-column number (297–320 DPI) fails the 600 DPI line-art preference and is unusable here because the type would fall to ~3–4 pt.
FORMAT:         Needs conversion — PNG is accepted for review only → supply vector PDF or EPS (or 600 DPI TIFF) for production.
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.11 MB (limit 10 MB); there is ample headroom to re-export at full resolution.
TEXT RENDERING: Rasterized — all axis labels, tick labels, legend and title are bitmap glyphs with visible pixel edges; no embedded font data.
COLOR MODE:     OK for submission (RGB required). Potential shift if the publisher converts to CMYK: the saturated matplotlib orange (#ff7f0e), green (#2ca02c) and red (#d62728) will desaturate, and red vs. orange may become nearly indistinguishable where the two multitaper floors overlap.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution at double-column width is ~145–151 DPI, less than half the 300 DPI floor for color line art and ~25% of the 600 DPI preference. Dense high-frequency wiggles in the periodogram trace will alias/smear.
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure; label and legend glyphs will look soft against the journal's vector body text.
  - [BLOCKING] Format: PNG is not a production format for line art; vector (PDF/EPS) is required.
  - [BLOCKING] A title is set inside the plot canvas ("AR(4) process, N=1024, design bandwidth W=4/N"). IEEE TBME prohibits in-figure titles; this information must move into the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Font size at final size: at 7.16 in the current ~10–12 pt matplotlib type scales to roughly 6.5–7.5 pt — legal but at the bottom of the 8–10 pt preferred band. At single-column width it would be ~3–4 pt, well below the 6 pt absolute minimum, so single-column placement is not viable without re-typesetting.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is incomplete. The caption compares noise floors and variability between estimators but gives no degrees of freedom: the multitaper estimates have ≈2K dof (14 for K=7, 12 for K=6), and the two box-smoothed periodograms have ≈2M dof set by the smoothing half-width, which is never stated. The number of realizations is also implicit ("another realization" = 1) rather than explicit.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend occupies a large fraction of the upper-right data area and its frame overlaps the high-frequency portion of the curves; the gray periodogram is also drawn over/under the estimates inconsistently, making the 0 dB region hard to read.
  - [ADVISORY] Red (K=7) and orange (K=6) floors overlap in the 0.25–0.5 band and are separated by hue alone — problematic for CMYK conversion, grayscale printing, and red-green color vision deficiency.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export from the plotting source as vector PDF or EPS sized to 181 mm x ~95 mm (7.16 x 3.75 in) with fonts embedded; if a raster is unavoidable, render at 600 DPI (≈4300 x 2250 px) TIFF with LZW compression.
  - Delete the in-figure title and fold it into the caption, e.g. "AR(4) process, N = 1024, design bandwidth W = 4/N (single realization)."
  - Set all text in a sans-serif face (Arial/Helvetica) at 8 pt for tick labels and legend and 9–10 pt for axis labels, specified at the final 7.16 in width.
  - Add the estimator degrees of freedom to the caption: state 2K = 14 (K = 7) and 2K = 12 (K = 6) for the multitaper estimates, the box-smoothing half-width and resulting equivalent dof for the two smoothed periodograms, and that a single realization is shown.
  - Move the legend outside the axes (right-hand strip or a two-column legend above the plot) so it does not mask the noise-floor region being discussed.
  - Distinguish K = 7 from K = 6 by line style or dash pattern in addition to color, and consider a colorblind-safe palette (e.g. Okabe–Ito) to survive CMYK/grayscale reproduction.
  - Label the y axis "PSD (dB)" (or dB re unit variance) rather than bare "dB" so the quantity is defined on the axis.

### efigure3.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     1820 x 546 px — recommended **double column** (three side-by-side panels, 3.33:1 aspect) — **254 DPI** at 181 mm (7.16 in); 265 DPI at the 6.875 in text-block width. For reference, at single column (88 mm / 3.5 in) it would be 520 DPI, but the three-panel layout would make the panels ~28 mm wide each and is not viable.
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (vector-style line plots with markers, text-heavy); 254 DPI at recommended width vs. 300 DPI minimum / 600 DPI preferred. Height at double column = 546/254 = 2.15 in (55 mm), well within the 230 mm limit.
FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG (review-acceptable only) → TIFF (LZW, ≥600 DPI) or, preferably for line art, vector PDF/EPS.
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.16 MB (limit 10 MB); there is ample headroom to re-export at 600 DPI or as vector.
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all labels, tick numbers and legend entries are bitmap (matplotlib PNG raster output); no vector text present.
COLOR MODE:     Presumed RGB (not reported in metadata) — OK for IEEE, which requests RGB. No CMYK conversion planned; if the publisher converts, the saturated default blue (#1f77b4), green (#2ca02c) and orange (#ff7f0e) are the most likely to shift.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Resolution insufficient at double-column width: 254 DPI for a line-art figure (needs ≥300, ideally 600).
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure; strokes and glyphs will show pixel edges in print.
  - [BLOCKING] Effective font size at final width is far below the 6 pt floor. The canvas is 18.2 in wide at 100 DPI; scaling to 7.16 in is a 0.39× reduction, so nominal 10–12 pt labels render at ~3.9–4.7 pt, and the 8-entry legend is smaller still.
  - [BLOCKING] A figure title is embedded in the image ("Resolution / variance / leakage per method, N=256 …"). IEEE prohibits in-figure titles; all of this belongs in the caption. The informal parenthetical "(the 2015 whiteboard plan)" must also be deleted.
  - [BLOCKING] No panel labels. Panels must carry lowercase (a), (b), (c), and the caption must refer to them as such rather than "Left / Center / Right".
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting incomplete. The left panel reports equivalent degrees of freedom, but the caption does not state how the plotted quantities were obtained: whether ν, leakage and side-lobe level are analytic kernel properties or Monte-Carlo estimates, and if the latter, the number of realizations. For Welch, the number of 50 %-overlap segments (and hence windows averaged) at each bandwidth must be stated, since ν depends on it.
  - [ADVISORY] Eight series are distinguished by colour alone. Red/brown and red/green pairs are hard to separate for colour-vision-deficient readers and in greyscale reproduction; several curves also overlap closely in the left panel.
  - [ADVISORY] The three axes-level texts ("variance: higher is better", "broadband leakage: lower is better", "peak sidelobe: lower is better") read as titles/editorial commentary. Retain only short axis quantities in the figure and move the "higher/lower is better" guidance to the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] The centre-panel y-label ("kernel mass beyond 2x half-power half-width") is long and will crowd at final size; the log axis spans 7 decades with unlabeled minor grid, which may be hard to read at 55 mm height.
  - [ADVISORY] The x-axis label "half-power bandwidth (1/N)" is repeated on all three panels; consider a single shared label to free space for larger fonts.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-generate from source at the final physical size: `fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 3, figsize=(7.16, 2.5))`, then save with `fig.savefig('efigure3.pdf')` (vector) and, if a raster is required, `savefig('efigure3.tif', dpi=600)`. This resolves the resolution, rasterized-text and font-size issues simultaneously.
  - Set typography explicitly for the new canvas: `rcParams.update({'font.family':'sans-serif','font.sans-serif':['Arial'],'font.size':8,'axes.labelsize':8,'xtick.labelsize':7,'ytick.labelsize':7,'legend.fontsize':6.5})`; verify no glyph falls below 6 pt after export.
  - Remove `fig.suptitle(...)` entirely and delete the three `ax.set_title(...)` calls; add `ax.text(0.02, 0.95, '(a)', transform=ax.transAxes, fontweight='bold')` etc. for panels (a)–(c).
  - Move the legend outside the axes (e.g. `fig.legend(loc='lower center', ncol=4, bbox_to_anchor=(0.5,-0.02))`) so it does not overlay data and can use a consistent 6.5–7 pt size across a wider box.
  - Differentiate series by linestyle and marker as well as colour (e.g. solid/dashed/dotted with o/s/^/v markers) and consider a colour-blind-safe palette (tab10 minus the red/brown pair, or `matplotlib` 'tab10' reordered / Okabe–Ito).
  - Shorten the centre y-label to "leakage: mass beyond 2×HPHW" and add a note in the caption defining it; keep only decade major ticks with labels.
  - Rewrite the caption opening as a brief descriptive sentence without bold title-style phrasing, use (a)/(b)/(c), and add the missing statistics sentence, e.g.: "Quantities are computed analytically from each method's spectral window; equivalent degrees of freedom for Welch's method assume M = <n> 50 %-overlap Hann segments of length <L> at each bandwidth setting. (a) ν; (b) kernel mass beyond twice the half-power half-width (lower is better); (c) peak side-lobe level (lower is better); for the Gaussian and Welch kernels, which have no flat top, this reports the main-lobe skirt."
  - Confirm the export is RGB (not CMYK or indexed-palette PNG) and embed all fonts if submitting PDF/EPS.

### efigure4.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     1950 x 1690 px — recommended **double** column (4 x 4 panel grid; single column is not viable) — **284 DPI** at 6.875 in / 181 mm. (At single-column 3.25 in it would be 600 DPI, but the 16-panel layout cannot be read at 88 mm.) Printed height at double column ≈ 151 mm — within the 230 mm limit. Aspect ratio (1.15:1) is acceptable for a full-width block figure.
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — figure type: line art (plots, bar charts, axes) — actual 284 DPI at the recommended width vs. 600 DPI preferred / 300 DPI minimum.
FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG → PDF or EPS (vector) for final submission; PNG is acceptable only for the review upload.
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.32 MB (limit 10 MB); substantial headroom for a 600-DPI raster or vector re-export.
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all labels are baked into the bitmap; pixel edges will be visible at print size. BLOCKING for line art.
COLOR MODE:     OK (RGB assumed, as required). If the production house converts to CMYK, the default matplotlib blue (#1f77b4), orange (#ff7f0e) and green (#2ca02c) traces will desaturate/shift; the four overlaid traces remain distinguishable by vertical offset, so the risk is cosmetic only.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Resolution 284 DPI at 181 mm is below the 300 DPI minimum and far below the 600 DPI preference for line art.
  - [BLOCKING] Text is rasterized rather than vector. Line-art figures with bitmapped text are rejected for print quality.
  - [BLOCKING] Effective font size at final width. The image appears to have been rendered at ~19.5 in wide with ~10 pt default fonts; scaled to 7.16 in this yields roughly **3.5–4 pt** for tick labels, axis labels and panel titles — well under the 6 pt absolute floor.
  - [BLOCKING] A figure title is set inside the image ("Every estimator is a bank of tapers with weights, …: three ways to build the bank from one window (N = 256, W = 4/N)"). IEEE TBME prohibits in-figure titles; this text must move to the caption.
  - [BLOCKING] Caption/figure mismatch. The caption assigns the Slepian sequences to (c) and the equal-weight/arbitrary-orthonormal-basis case to (d), but in the figure the second-row title "hidden tapers: the Slepians (with equal weights; any orthonormal basis of their span would do)" sits over column **(d)** (Thomson multitaper), while column (c) is the sliding-sinc construction with "eigenvectors of Q". Also, the title says "three ways to build the bank" while four columns (a)–(d) are shown.
  - [ADVISORY] Only the four column headers carry (a)–(d) labels; the 16 individual axes are unlabelled. A reader cannot cite "the eigen-weight ladder of column b" unambiguously.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel headings carry 2–3 lines of explanatory prose each (method description, parameter values, the bank composition). This is caption material, not in-figure annotation, and it is what forces the tiny type.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is only partially satisfied. The estimator degrees of freedom ν = 2(Σc)²/Σc² are given per column (13.4, 11.1, 17.2, 14.0) — good — but the figure/caption never state that these are deterministic/analytic quantities computed from a single window of N = 256 with no Monte-Carlo realizations, and no error bars or distributions are shown. Readers may assume an ensemble.
  - [ADVISORY] The bottom-row bar charts and kernel panels repeat identical y-axis ranges and tick labels four times; redundant labelling consumes space that the type size needs.
  - [ADVISORY] Row 3 y-label "weight (normalized)" and row 4 "dB" appear only on the leftmost panel — correct practice, but confirm the shared axis is obvious after the panels are re-labelled.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export from the plotting script as **vector PDF or EPS** with `figsize=(7.16, 6.2)` (inches) so that the figure is authored at final size; set `rcParams['font.size']=8`, `axes.labelsize=8`, `xtick/ytick.labelsize=7`, `axes.titlesize=8` with a sans-serif family (Arial/Helvetica). This resolves the resolution, rasterization and font-size blockers in one pass.
  - If a raster deliverable is required, render at ≥ 600 DPI for 181 mm width, i.e. ≥ 4280 px wide, and save as TIFF (LZW) — still well under 10 MB for this content.
  - Delete the suptitle from the image and open the caption with it, e.g. "Every estimator is a bank of tapers with weights Ŝ(f)=Σ_k c_k|v_kᵗx_f|²: four constructions of the bank from one window (N = 256, W = 4/N)."
  - Correct the (c)/(d) attribution in the caption to match the figure, and change "three ways" to "four ways" (or drop one column if only three constructions are intended).
  - Add lowercase panel labels to every axes — (a)–(d) for row 1, (e)–(h) row 2, (i)–(l) row 3, (m)–(p) row 4 — placed at the upper-left outside the axes, and reference them in the caption.
  - Move the multi-line method descriptions out of the panel titles into the caption; keep at most a short identifier per column (e.g. "Welch (Hann, L = N/4, 50 %)", "smoothed periodogram", "sliding sinc", "Thomson MT, K = 7").
  - Given the density, consider splitting into two double-column figures — (i) banks + hidden eigen-tapers, (ii) eigen-weight ladders + kernels — which would roughly double the available type size at the same print width.
  - Add to the caption: the number of realizations behind each quantity (state explicitly that all curves are analytic/deterministic for a single N = 256 window, no averaging over realizations), and restate that ν = 2(Σ_k c_k)²/Σ_k c_k² is the equivalent degrees of freedom used to compare noise levels across the four estimators.
  - Retain RGB; if the publisher requests CMYK, pre-convert and verify the blue and green traces, and consider adding line-style differentiation (solid/dashed/dash-dot) so the four overlaid tapers survive greyscale reproduction.

### figure1.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     1690 × 546 px (aspect 3.10:1) — recommended **double column** (181 mm / 7.16 in) — **236 DPI** at that width (246 DPI if the narrower 6.875 in text block is used). At single-column width (88 mm / 3.5 in) it would be 483 DPI, but a 3.1:1 two-panel figure compressed to 88 mm would render axis text illegibly small; single column is not viable.
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — figure type: line art (2-D line plots, no photographic content); actual 236 DPI at double-column width vs. 300 DPI minimum and 600 DPI preferred for line art. Rendered height at 7.16 in width is only 2.31 in (59 mm), well inside the 230 mm limit.
FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG (review-acceptable only) → TIFF (LZW, ≥600 DPI) or, preferably for line art, vector **PDF/EPS**.
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.15 MB (limit 10 MB); there is ample headroom to raise resolution substantially.
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick labels, legends and titles are pixel-bound (consequence of PNG export); no vector glyph outlines available.
COLOR MODE:     OK (RGB assumed for PNG) — palette is the matplotlib default black / orange (#ff7f0e) / steel blue (#1f77b4). Keep RGB per IEEE; if a CMYK proof is ever generated, the saturated orange and the blue are the two traces most at risk of shifting and becoming harder to separate. The blue and black traces overlap heavily in the left panel and are distinguished only by hue/width — add a linestyle difference for grayscale/print safety.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Resolution at double-column width is 236 DPI, below the 300 DPI floor and far below the 600 DPI line-art preference.
  - [BLOCKING] Text is rasterized in a line-art figure; at the required 3.1:1 reduction the pixel edges on tick labels and legend text will be visible in print.
  - [BLOCKING] Effective font size after scaling to 181 mm is roughly 4–5 pt (the figure appears authored at ~16.9 in wide with ~10–12 pt type; the 0.42× reduction drops it below the 6 pt absolute minimum).
  - [BLOCKING] Both panels carry in-figure titles ("Three routes, one estimate: 8 s of EEG…" and "(c) equals (a) to machine precision…"). IEEE TBME prohibits titles inside the figure; this content belongs in the caption (it is already largely duplicated there).
  - [BLOCKING] File format: PNG is not an accepted final submission format.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel labels are embedded in legend entries rather than placed as lowercase (a)/(b) identifiers on the panels. The left panel currently contains curves labelled (a), (b), (c) while the panel itself is unlabelled — a reader following the caption's "Left:" / "Right:" cannot map "(a)" to a panel. Relabel the two panels (a) and (b), and rename the three curves (e.g., MT, SMOOTH, SLID or i/ii/iii) to remove the collision.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting incomplete: the number of realizations behind the sliding-window average is not stated. For a length-$16N$ record with unit-sample hop this is $L = 16N - N + 1 = 15361$ windows — state it. Likewise state the estimator degrees of freedom being compared (all $N=1024$ eigenvalue-weighted Slepian tapers; effective dof $\approx 2\sum_k\lambda_k$, and the equivalent smoothing bandwidth of the box, $2W = 1$ Hz) since the panels compare noise/bias levels of three estimators.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend strings in the left panel are very long (up to ~60 characters) and occupy ~30% of the axes width; at 8 pt final size they will crowd the 8–14 Hz peaks.
  - [ADVISORY] Right-panel y-axis spans ±0.20 dB while the data occupy roughly −0.06 to +0.01 dB; ~70% of the panel is empty white space.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption states "(c) equals (a) to $3\times10^{-13}$ dB" while the in-figure legend says "max 2.7e-13 dB". Harmonise the rounding and use a consistent scientific-notation style ($2.7\times10^{-13}$) in both places.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Regenerate from source at the final physical size: `fig, ax = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(7.16, 2.6))`, then set all font sizes to 8 pt (tick labels 8 pt, axis labels 8–9 pt, legend 7–8 pt) so no text falls below 6 pt after placement. Verify by measuring a tick label in the output at 100% scale.
  - Export as vector PDF or EPS (`savefig('figure1.pdf')` with `rcParams['pdf.fonttype']=42`) to satisfy both the 600 DPI line-art expectation and vector text. If a raster is required, export TIFF at `dpi=600` for a 7.16 in width (≈4300 × 1560 px, LZW-compressed, still well under 10 MB).
  - Delete both in-figure `set_title` calls; move that text into the caption. Add lowercase panel labels "(a)" and "(b)" in the upper-left corner of each axes (8–9 pt, bold optional).
  - Rename the three curves to avoid clashing with panel letters, e.g. legend entries "multitaper ($N$ Slepian tapers, weights $\lambda_k$)", "smoothed periodogram (box, half-width $W$)", "slid sinc window, $16N$ samples", and shorten them so the legend occupies <20% of axes width; consider moving the left-panel legend outside/above the axes or to the lower-left where the spectrum has decayed.
  - Give the black multitaper trace a dashed or dotted overlay (or reduce its width and plot it last) so the three coincident curves remain distinguishable in grayscale and after any CMYK conversion.
  - Tighten the right-panel y-limits to about −0.07 to +0.02 dB (or plot on a symmetric log/absolute-difference axis) so the $10^{-13}$ dB trace and the $0.056$ dB trace are both interpretable.
  - Add to the caption: the number of sliding windows ($L = 15361$), the number of tapers / effective degrees of freedom of the multitaper estimate, the equivalent smoothing bandwidth $2W$ of the box filter, and the EEG channel/subject provenance.
  - Confirm RGB color mode is retained on export; do not convert to CMYK.

### figure2.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     1820 x 884 px — recommended **double column** (8-panel 2×4 grid; unusable at 88 mm) — **254 DPI** at 181 mm (7.16 in); for reference, 520 DPI if forced to 88 mm single column, and 265 DPI at the 6.875 in legacy text width
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (axis plots, kernels, line traces, flat fills): 254 DPI at double-column width vs. 300 DPI minimum / 600 DPI preferred
FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG → PDF or EPS (vector strongly preferred for this figure type); TIFF at ≥600 DPI acceptable as a raster fallback
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.21 MB (limit 10 MB); ample headroom for a 600 DPI TIFF or vector PDF
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all labels are baked into the PNG raster; pixel edges will be visible on tick labels and titles at print size
COLOR MODE:     OK (assumed RGB for PNG) — journal requires RGB, so no conversion needed. Note the saturated blue kernel-width bars and light-blue |f|≤W shading are the elements most at risk if any downstream CMYK conversion occurs; the pale blue band in (g)/(h) is also close in value to the grey spectrum fill and may become indistinguishable at 181 mm.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Resolution at the recommended double-column width is 254 DPI, below the 300 DPI floor and far below the 600 DPI preference for line art.
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. Combined with the sub-300 DPI scale factor, tick labels and legends will show pixel edges.
  - [BLOCKING] Effective font size at final width. The figure appears authored at roughly 18 in wide with ~10 pt type; scaled to 7.16 in, label text renders at approximately 4 pt — below the 6 pt absolute minimum and well below the 8–10 pt target. Affected: all axis tick labels, axis titles ("frequency (cycles/sample)", "kernel (dB re peak)"), panel titles, legend entries, and the in-panel "width 0.8/N … 48.2/N" annotations.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel titles function as titles inside the figure ("(a) periodogram, no taper: leaky and noisy", "(e) kernel: mass beyond 2W 1.3%", etc.), which IEEE asks to be moved to the caption. The numeric content (ν values, mass-beyond-2W percentages) is substantive, however, and is better retained in-panel than lost.
  - [ADVISORY] Two-line panel headers plus an 8-panel grid leave little vertical margin; at 181 mm × 88 mm the top row's titles will crowd the axes once fonts are enlarged to 8 pt.
  - [ADVISORY] Red-on-black and red-on-grey are the only encodings distinguishing estimate from truth and kernel from spectrum; this is not robust to grayscale reproduction or to red–green color vision deficiency.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is largely compliant — the caption states a single record (n = 1 realization, N = 1024) and reports equivalent degrees of freedom ν per panel — but ν appears only as an in-figure annotation with no axis or legend gloss, and the caption should state explicitly that no averaging over realizations was performed (i.e., the visible roughness is the ν = 2 sampling variability of one periodogram, not an error bar).
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Regenerate from source with the figure canvas set to exactly 7.16 in × ~3.5 in and font sizes specified in points (8 pt ticks/legend, 9 pt axis labels, 9 pt panel headers) so that no post-hoc scaling is required. Do not export a large canvas and shrink it.
  - Save as vector PDF or EPS with fonts embedded (Type 1/TrueType, not Type 3); this simultaneously clears the resolution, rasterized-text, and file-size items. In matplotlib: `rcParams['pdf.fonttype']=42`, `savefig('figure2.pdf')`.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandated, export TIFF at 600 DPI for a 7.16 in width (4296 × ~2096 px) with LZW compression; this will remain well under 10 MB.
  - Confirm the sans-serif family is Arial or Helvetica (matplotlib's DejaVu Sans is acceptable in appearance but substitute Helvetica/Arial for consistency with IEEE house style).
  - Shorten in-panel headers to bare labels plus the numeric result, e.g. "(a) ν = 2.0", "(e) mass beyond 2W = 1.3%", and move the prose descriptors ("no taper: leaky and noisy") into the caption, which already carries them.
  - Increase the value separation between the pale-blue |f|≤W band and the grey true-spectrum fill (e.g., replace the blue fill with a hatched band or a pair of vertical dashed rules at ±W), and verify legibility of the 8.2/N and 48.2/N bars at 181 mm.
  - Add a redundant non-color cue to the estimate/truth pair (e.g., truth as a heavy solid black line, estimate as a thin line — already close — or estimate with a distinct dash pattern in panels c/d where the two overlap).
  - Amend the caption to state: "All estimates are from a single realization (n = 1 record, N = 1024); no averaging across realizations was performed. ν denotes the equivalent degrees of freedom of each estimator's kernel and is the basis for the noise-level comparison between panels (a)–(d)."
  - Add lowercase panel labels (a)–(h) as standalone bold labels at the upper-left of each axes if the descriptive titles are moved to the caption, so panel identity survives independently of the header text.

### figure3.png

---
**DIMENSIONS:** 1676 × 530 px — recommended **double** column (3.16:1 aspect, two side-by-side panels) — **244 DPI** at 6.875 in (234 DPI at the full 181 mm / 7.16 in trim). For reference: 516 DPI at 3.25 in single column, but the 3.16:1 aspect ratio makes single-column placement impractical (panel height would fall to ~26 mm and text would be illegible).

**RESOLUTION:** Below threshold — figure type: **line art** (vector-style plots, no photographic content or gradients); actual 244 DPI at recommended width vs. 300 DPI minimum / 600 DPI preferred.

**FORMAT:** Needs conversion — PNG (acceptable for review only) → **PDF or EPS** (preferred, vector) or 600-DPI TIFF for final submission.

**FILE SIZE:** OK — 0.21 MB (limit 10 MB).

**TEXT RENDERING:** Rasterized — all axis labels, tick labels, legends, and the red annotations are baked into the PNG raster; pixel edges will be visible at print size.

**COLOR MODE:** OK (assumed RGB for PNG; metadata did not report color_mode — confirm before upload). Journal requires RGB, so no CMYK conversion is needed. Note that the saturated orange (bias curve), medium blue (noise curve), and green (tapered record) are exactly the hues most at risk if a production house re-separates to CMYK; keep RGB and verify proofs.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Effective resolution of 244 DPI at double-column width is below the 300 DPI floor and far below the 600 DPI preference for line art.
- [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure; strokes and glyphs will appear soft/aliased in print.
- [BLOCKING] Estimated final font size is ~4–5 pt. The figure was evidently authored at ~16 in wide with ~10 pt type; scaled to 181 mm this becomes roughly 4.5 pt for axis labels and ~4 pt for tick labels — below the 6 pt absolute minimum and well below the 8–10 pt target.
- [ADVISORY] Panel titles are set inside the figure ("(a) the record: N = 1024 samples of an AR(4) process", "(b) Hann taper, then box: error vs box width (300 realizations)"). IEEE TBME does not permit titles inside the figure; this descriptive text belongs in the caption. Retain only the lowercase panel labels (a)/(b).
- [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is partially complete: the 300 realizations are stated (in-figure and in caption) and the equivalent degrees of freedom (ν = 2, 9, 50) are annotated at the circled points, which satisfies the DOF requirement for the noise comparison. However, the caption does not state (i) whether the RMS is taken over frequency only, then averaged over realizations, or pooled over both, (ii) the number of frequency bins entering the RMS, or (iii) how ν relates to W (e.g. ν ≈ 2·(2W·N·something)) so readers can reproduce the annotated values. No error bars or shaded intervals are shown, so the 300-realization spread is not visualized.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a) is a dense oscillatory trace at ~1024 samples across ~85 mm; at 244 DPI adjacent zero crossings will merge into a gray mass. This panel benefits disproportionately from vector output.
- [ADVISORY] The dotted vertical guides in (b) are labeled "2W = peak width" and "2W = peak separation" in small gray type that will be near-illegible at 4 pt; consider larger type or moving these to the caption.
- [ADVISORY] Legends sit below each panel, which is fine, but the three/four-entry legends consume vertical space that could go to the axes; consider in-axes legends or direct line labeling to increase plot height.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Regenerate from source as **vector PDF or EPS** at the final physical size — set `figsize=(7.16, 2.6)` inches (181 × 66 mm) rather than exporting a large raster and downscaling. This resolves the resolution, text-rasterization, and font-size issues simultaneously.
- Set an explicit font stack and sizes for the final size: sans-serif (Arial/Helvetica/DejaVu Sans), 9 pt axis labels, 8 pt tick labels and legend, 8 pt for the red annotations. Verify by measuring a rendered PDF at 100 % zoom.
- If a raster is unavoidable, export at ≥ 600 DPI for 181 mm width, i.e. ≥ 4280 px wide, as TIFF with LZW compression. At that width the file will still sit well under 10 MB.
- Delete both in-figure titles. Place the lowercase labels "(a)" and "(b)" at the upper-left of each panel (outside the axes or in the corner) and move all descriptive text into the caption, which already carries it.
- Consider a 2×1 stacked layout (each panel 181 mm wide, ~55 mm tall) instead of 1×2. This nearly doubles the horizontal space per panel, letting fonts reach 9–10 pt comfortably and giving the dense AR(4) trace in (a) room to resolve. Total height would be ~120 mm, well within the 230 mm limit.
- Expand the caption's statistics sentence, e.g.: "…root-mean-square error over the M = 512 Fourier frequencies in the band, averaged across 300 independent realizations; the equivalent degrees of freedom of the smoothed estimator, ν, is annotated at the three circled bandwidths." If the 300-realization spread is informative, add a shaded ±1 s.d. band around the two total-error curves and state in the caption that the band is ±1 s.d. over 300 realizations.
- Confirm the exported file is RGB (not CMYK or indexed-palette) and embed all fonts (`pdftex` Type 42/TrueType or Type 1) so no font substitution occurs at the publisher.

### figure4.png

---

**DIMENSIONS:** 1560 × 1040 px (aspect 1.50:1) — recommended **double** column — **218 DPI** at 181 mm (7.16 in); 446 DPI if forced to 88 mm (3.46 in) single column. At double-column width the rendered size is 181 × 121 mm, well within the 230 mm height cap, so the aspect ratio itself is fine.

**RESOLUTION:** **Below threshold** — figure type: **line art** (vector-style plots, no photographic or continuous-tone content). 218 DPI at the recommended double-column width fails both the 300 DPI minimum and the 600 DPI line-art preference. The single-column option nominally clears 300 DPI but is unusable for a 2 × 2 panel grid (see text rendering).

**FORMAT:** **Needs conversion** — PNG → **PDF or EPS** (vector) for final submission; PNG is accepted for review only. Since the source is evidently a matplotlib figure, re-export vector rather than upsampling the raster.

**FILE SIZE:** OK — 0.29 MB, far below the 10 MB limit.

**TEXT RENDERING:** **Rasterized** — all labels are baked into the PNG bitmap; tick labels show pixel-edge antialiasing at 1:1 zoom. This is a BLOCKING defect for a line-art figure.

**COLOR MODE:** **OK (assumed RGB)** — no `color_mode` field was supplied in the metadata block; confirm the export is RGB, not CMYK or indexed/palette PNG. If the publisher later converts to CMYK, the saturated matplotlib default blue (#1f77b4), green (#2ca02c) and orange (#ff7f0e) used in all four panels are the most likely to shift/dull; the red dashed "2NW" marker and the magenta k=6 trace are also at risk.

**ISSUES:**
- **[BLOCKING]** Resolution: 218 DPI at 181 mm double-column width, below the 300 DPI minimum for color line art (600 DPI preferred). Upsampling the existing PNG will not recover the deficit.
- **[BLOCKING]** Rasterized text and curves in a pure line-art figure. Thin traces (the ripple crests in the bottom-left running-sum curves, the black "all N tapers" box edges) and the 1-px dotted/dashed reference lines will alias badly in print.
- **[BLOCKING]** Font size at final size. The figure was drawn at roughly 15.6 in wide with default ~10 pt type; scaled to 7.16 in this becomes ≈4.6 pt for tick labels and axis labels, and ≈5.5 pt for the panel titles — both below the 6 pt absolute floor. At single-column width it falls to ≈2 pt.
- **[BLOCKING]** Format: PNG is not acceptable for final submission (TIFF/EPS/PDF/PS required).
- **[ADVISORY]** Titles are set inside each panel ("first six Slepian tapers…", "their spectral windows…", "running sum…", "eigenvalue ladder: tapers beyond 2NW carry only leakage"). IEEE TBME does not permit in-figure titles; this descriptive content belongs in the caption.
- **[ADVISORY]** No panel labels. The figure needs lowercase (a), (b), (c), (d), and the caption should refer to them instead of "Top left / Top right / Bottom left / Bottom right".
- **[ADVISORY]** Eight-category legend in panel (b) relies on the matplotlib default cycle; k=3 (red) vs k=5 (brown) and k=1 (orange) vs k=5 (brown) are hard to separate, and the set is not colorblind-safe. The same applies to the red dashed 2NW line vs the grey dotted K=2NW−1 line at small size.
- **[ADVISORY]** In panel (d) the y-axis spans 10⁻¹⁶ to 10⁰ but no datum falls below ~2×10⁻¹⁰; roughly 40 % of the panel is empty, which shrinks the informative region unnecessarily.
- **[ADVISORY]** Axis and legend text uses ASCII pseudo-math ("|U_k(f)|^2", "1 - lambda_k (leakage fraction)", "2NW"). Should be typeset math consistent with the caption: $|U_k(f)|^2$, $1-\lambda_k$, $2NW$.
- **[ADVISORY]** The legend in panel (a) is placed over the k=3 and k=4 traces; in panel (c) it overlaps the right shoulder of the box. Both reduce legibility once the figure is reduced to 181 mm.
- **[ADVISORY]** Statistical reporting: this figure is deterministic (analytic Slepian sequences and eigenvalues), so no error bars or distributions appear and no realization count is required. State explicitly in the caption that the curves are exact/deterministic for $N=256$, $NW=4$, so a reader does not look for missing uncertainty information. If panel (d) eigenvalues were obtained numerically, name the solver/tolerance, since values below ~10⁻¹⁰ approach double-precision limits.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Re-export from the plotting script at the final physical size: `fig, axes = plt.subplots(2, 2, figsize=(7.16, 4.8))`, then `fig.savefig('figure4.pdf')`. This produces true vector output and eliminates the resolution and rasterization blockers simultaneously.
- Set fonts explicitly for the new canvas: `plt.rcParams.update({'font.family':'sans-serif','font.sans-serif':['Arial'],'font.size':8,'axes.labelsize':8,'xtick.labelsize':7,'ytick.labelsize':7,'legend.fontsize':7})`. Verify nothing renders below 6 pt after export.
- If a raster is required as well, render at 600 DPI for 7.16 in → 4296 × 2864 px TIFF with LZW compression; check the result stays under 10 MB (it should, given the sparse line content).
- Delete all four `ax.set_title()` calls and move that text into the caption. Add panel labels with `ax.text(-0.12, 1.05, '(a)', transform=ax.transAxes, fontsize=9, fontweight='bold')` and equivalents for (b)–(d).
- Recast the caption along these lines: "**Why the multitaper kernel is a box.** (a) First six Slepian tapers for $N=256$, $W=4/N$, vertically offset for display. (b) Corresponding spectral windows $|U_k(f)|^2$, $k=0..7$; each fills the gaps left by the previous ones inside the band $|f|<W$ (shaded). (c) Running sum $\sum_{k<K}\lambda_k|U_k(f)|^2$ as tapers are added; with all $N$ tapers it is exactly the box of identity (\ref{eq:identity}), while the usual $K=2NW-1$ gives the box with rounded shoulders and small ripple. (d) Leakage fraction $1-\lambda_k$ per taper on a log scale; tapers beyond $2NW$ carry only leakage. All curves are deterministic for the stated $N$ and $NW$; no averaging over realizations is involved."
- Replace the default color cycle in (b) with a perceptually ordered sequence (e.g. `viridis` sampled at 8 points) so the taper index reads as a progression, and distinguish the 2NW and K=2NW−1 markers by both color and dash pattern with a slightly heavier linewidth (1.2 pt).
- Set `axes[1,1].set_ylim(1e-11, 2)` in panel (d) to remove the empty decades, and reduce y-tick density to every second decade so labels do not crowd at 7 pt.
- Move the legends outside the data area (`loc='upper left', bbox_to_anchor=(1.02, 1)`) or into clear whitespace — in (a) the lower right below the k=0 trace, in (c) the far left near $f=-8/N$.
- Convert ASCII pseudo-math in all axis labels and legends to mathtext so the figure matches caption notation.
- Confirm the exported PDF/EPS embeds fonts (Type 42/TrueType: `plt.rcParams['pdf.fonttype']=42`, `plt.rcParams['ps.fonttype']=42`) and that the color space is RGB.

### figure5.png
