### figure6.png

---
TITLE:              Present & bold — "The everyday estimators on the record of Fig. \ref{fig:motif}" is bold and leads the legend, but it is a label rather than a finding; consider a claim-style title (see fixes).
PANEL DESCRIPTIONS: Complete — (a)–(d) described in order; (e)–(h) described in the "Bottom:" sentence, with (e), (f), (g, h) called out. Panel (h) is only implicitly covered by "(g, h)" plus the final Welch sentence; make it explicit.
ERROR BARS:         Not present — the figure shows single-realization spectral estimates, so no error bars are expected. However, the variability of each estimate is the central quantity being compared and is currently unquantified (see ISSUES).
SAMPLE SIZE:        Partial/Missing — $N=1024$ samples is stated, but the number of realizations behind each red curve (apparently one) is never stated, nor the number of frequency bins over which the quoted median errors were taken.
STATISTICAL DETAIL: Partial — absent: (i) definition of $\nu$, the equivalent degrees of freedom printed in every top-panel title (this is exactly the quantity the journal requires when noise levels are compared); (ii) how $\nu$ was computed (analytically vs. empirically); (iii) definition of "median error" (median over which frequencies? absolute dB error? in dB of the ratio?); (iv) statement that a single realization/seed is shown and whether the ranking is stable across realizations; (v) no test statistic or p-value — acceptable here, but say explicitly that no inferential test is performed.
ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: PSD (used in the in-panel legend and expanded nowhere in the caption); $\nu$; $NW$ (panel b title, time–bandwidth product); $K$ (number of tapers — used but not named as such in the caption); $W$ (half-bandwidth; stated numerically as $4/N$ but not named); "mass beyond $2W$" (panels e–h titles); "dB re peak" (y-axis, e–h); "box" (boxcar/rectangular smoother); Fejér kernel (term, not defined); "width 8.2/N" annotations in (a)–(d).
COLOR/SYMBOL KEY:   Present but incomplete — in-figure legends appear only in (a) and (e). The caption should state that black = true PSD, red = estimate/kernel, grey shading = true spectrum near its peaks (arbitrary vertical scale), light blue band = $|f|\le W$, and that these apply to all panels. The small blue square marker with the "width $x/N$" label in (a)–(d) is nowhere explained.
SELF-CONTAINED:     No — the caption points to Fig.~\ref{fig:motif} for the data record and to \eqref{eq:identity} for the definition of the "exact estimate," so a reader cannot reconstruct the input process or the estimator without the main text. $\nu$, the grey shaded reference, and the "width $x/N$" annotations are also unexplained.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] $\nu$ appears in all four top-panel titles and is never defined. The journal explicitly requires the estimator's degrees of freedom to be stated where noise levels are compared; as written the key noise-level metric of the figure is an unlabelled symbol.
  - [BLOCKING] Number of realizations not stated. Each red curve is a single sample function; the caption must say "one realization" (and ideally the RNG seed) so the reader knows the roughness shown is not an average.
  - [BLOCKING] "Median errors against the true spectrum: 11.6, 4.6, 1.3 and 0.8 dB" is an undefined statistic — no definition (|estimate − truth| in dB?), no domain (all $f\in[0,0.5]$? excluding the peaks?), no count of frequency bins, and no dispersion measure (IQR or MAD). Numbers of this kind cannot be reported without their estimator definition and n.
  - [BLOCKING] Self-containment fails: the underlying process/record is defined only by cross-reference to another figure, and the "exact estimate" only by an equation number. Add a one-clause description of the true PSD (e.g., two closely spaced AR peaks near $f\approx0.11$ and $0.14$ with a ~30 dB dynamic range) so the figure stands alone.
  - [ADVISORY] Undefined symbols/terms: PSD, $K$, $W$, $NW$, "mass beyond $2W$", "dB re peak". All should be expanded on first use in the legend even if defined in the text.
  - [ADVISORY] The blue square marker and "width $x/N$" annotation in (a)–(d) is unexplained; the reader cannot tell it is the measured equivalent/half-power bandwidth of the kernel rather than a data symbol.
  - [ADVISORY] The grey shaded region in (e)–(h) is labelled "true spectrum near its peaks, for scale" in-figure; the caption should repeat this and state that its vertical placement is arbitrary (it shares only the dB scale), otherwise it reads as a second estimate.
  - [ADVISORY] IEEE TBME asks for no titles inside the figure. The descriptive text above each panel ("raw periodogram, then box W = 4/N", "kernel: mass beyond 2W 1.4%", etc.) functions as eight in-figure titles. Safest route: keep only lowercase (a)–(h) labels in the axes corners and move the descriptions and the $\nu$ / mass values into the caption or a small table.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (h) is never named explicitly as Welch's kernel; the closing sentence says "Welch's kernel" without the letter, and "(g, h)" lumps two different kernels (Hann-box vs. Hann-segment) under one claim.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel labels are lowercase in the image (correct for IEEE); ensure the caption also uses lowercase throughout — it does, good. Verify all tick/axis text is ≥6 pt at the final 181 mm width; at 1820 px the labels are ~8 pt equivalent, but re-export as vector PDF/EPS (or ≥600 DPI) since the submitted PNG is a raster.
  - [ADVISORY] Numbers quoted in the caption must match the panel titles exactly; check "$-22$ dB" for the Slepian skirt, which is not annotated anywhere in (f) and therefore cannot be verified from the figure.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Retitle with the finding, e.g.: \textbf{Tapering, not smoothing, sets the leakage floor of a smoothed periodogram.}
  - Add a definitions sentence: "$N=1024$ samples; $W=4/N$ is the one-sided smoother half-bandwidth; $K$ is the number of Slepian tapers and $NW$ their time–bandwidth product; PSD, power spectral density; $\nu$ is the equivalent number of chi-square degrees of freedom of the estimator at mid-band, computed as [analytic expression / from 1000 independent realizations]."
  - State the realization count: "All estimates are from a single realization of the process of Fig.~X (seed 0); the corresponding $\nu$ values quantify the variance that a single realization illustrates."
  - Define the error metric with its n: "Median error is the median of $|10\log_{10}(\hat S/S)|$ over the $n=513$ one-sided Fourier frequencies (IQR in brackets): 11.6 [.., ..], 4.6 [.., ..], 1.3 [.., ..] and 0.8 [.., ..] dB for (a)–(d)."
  - Add the full key: "Black, true PSD; red, estimate (a–d) or kernel (e–h); grey shading in (e)–(h), the true spectrum near its peaks plotted on the same dB scale with arbitrary vertical offset, for scale only; light blue band, $|f|\le W$; blue square and label in (a)–(d), measured half-power width of the kernel in units of $1/N$."
  - Make (h) explicit: "...the Hann-box kernel (g) falls below $-40$ dB, while Welch's kernel (h) has no flat top and side lobes at the scale of the peaks."
  - Add one clause describing the true spectrum so the figure is self-contained, and, if space permits, annotate the $-22$ dB skirt level directly in (f).
  - State explicitly that no hypothesis test is performed: "Comparisons are descriptive; no inferential test is applied."
  - Re-export as vector PDF/EPS at 181 mm width, RGB, sans-serif 8 pt minimum, and remove the in-panel titles per IEEE style.

### figure7.png

---
TITLE:              Present & bold — "**The everyday versions agree to within their own noise.**"
PANEL DESCRIPTIONS: Incomplete / conflicted — the two-panel layout is referred to only as an implicit "left" panel and "Right:"; neither panel carries an in-figure lowercase label (a)/(b), while (a), (b), (c) are simultaneously used for the three *estimators*. Estimator descriptions are also out of order [(a), (c), (b)].
ERROR BARS:         Not present — but the figure explicitly compares noise levels ("median 1.6 dB, 95% 4.9 dB"), so a spread/uncertainty definition is required (see ISSUES).
SAMPLE SIZE:        Missing — number of EEG epochs/records (appears to be n = 1 epoch), epoch length in seconds, sampling rate, and the number of frequency bins entering the median and 95th-percentile statistics are all unstated. Only the Welch segment count (9) is given.
STATISTICAL DETAIL: Partial — degrees of freedom are given (ν = 14, 8.9, 17.6), which satisfies the journal's "estimator degrees of freedom" requirement; but no test is named, no test statistic, no p-values, and the "95%" figure is undefined (95th percentile of |difference|? 95% confidence bound?). The predictive expression "4.3√(2/ν)" is stated without derivation or definition of the constant 4.3 (= 10/ln 10 dB).
ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: EEG (expand at first use in the caption: electroencephalogram/-graphy); K (number of tapers); W (half-bandwidth); ν (equivalent degrees of freedom — used but never named in the caption); dB reference level (dB relative to what? µV²/Hz?); "Slepian" / DPSS relationship; "box" (boxcar smoother of half-width W).
COLOR/SYMBOL KEY:   Present in-figure (thick black = (a) multitaper, orange = (c) Hann+box, thin blue = (b) Welch), but not restated in the caption; the right panel relies on the same colour code without restating which trace is which beyond the in-figure legend.
SELF-CONTAINED:     No — missing: expansion of EEG and definition of ν; the y-axis reference for "power (dB)"; definition of the "95%" statistic and the number of bins behind it; epoch length/sampling rate; and a forward-reference-free statement of what the box half-width W is in Hz. The caption also cross-references Fig. \ref{fig:abstract} for the data, so a reader cannot identify the record from this figure alone.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] The submitted caption block is corrupted: the LaTeX source begins mid-way through a *table* caption (equivalent degrees of freedom, leakage, side lobes) and the complete `tabular`/`\end{table}` body, before the actual figure caption starts. The figure caption must be extracted and submitted as a standalone `\caption{}` for figure 7.
  - [BLOCKING] Noise-level comparison without a defined spread estimator: "median 1.6 dB" and "95% 4.9 dB" are reported with no statement of (i) what population they summarise (frequency bins from 0–40 Hz? how many?), (ii) whether 95% is a percentile or a confidence interval, (iii) whether one or multiple epochs/realizations contribute. IEEE TBME requires the number of realizations/windows behind any distributional summary.
  - [BLOCKING] Panel labelling absent from the image. IEEE requires lowercase (a), (b) panel labels; here no panel labels exist and the letters (a), (b), (c) are reused for traces, guaranteeing reader confusion between "panel (b)" and "estimator (b)".
  - [BLOCKING] Both panels carry in-figure titles ("The everyday versions: ν = 14.0, ..." and "differences of the size of the estimators' own noise"). IEEE TBME prohibits titles inside the figure; this content belongs in the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Estimator descriptions run (a), (c), (b) — out of alphabetical order, and the in-figure legend also lists (a), (c), (b). Reorder both to (a), (b), (c).
  - [ADVISORY] Sample size/record provenance is delegated to another figure ("The EEG epoch of Fig. \ref{fig:abstract}"); at minimum state duration, sampling rate, channel, and n = 1 epoch here.
  - [ADVISORY] "power (dB)" and "difference from (a), dB" lack a physical reference (e.g., dB re 1 µV²/Hz); absolute ordinate values (–10 to 50 dB) are therefore uninterpretable.
  - [ADVISORY] The final sentence attributes the valley discrepancies to "the seventh Slepian taper's side lobes" — an interpretive claim with no supporting element in the figure; either mark the affected valleys or move the claim to the text.
  - [ADVISORY] Thin blue trace (Welch) is ~0.8 pt; at 88 mm or even 181 mm reproduction it risks breaking up. Check that all line weights are ≥0.5 pt at final size and that legend/tick text is ≥6 pt (currently ~9–10 pt at 1690 px width, acceptable only if placed at full 181 mm).
  - [ADVISORY] Orange/blue/black is broadly colour-blind safe, but the two panels rely on colour alone; consider dash patterns so the figure survives greyscale printing.
  - [ADVISORY] Supplied as PNG (acceptable for review only); provide vector PDF/EPS or ≥600 DPI TIFF for production. Current raster is 1690 px wide → only ~237 DPI at 181 mm.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Delete the table fragment and submit only the figure caption. Add in-figure lowercase panel labels **(a)** (spectra) and **(b)** (differences) at the top-left of each axes, and rename the three estimators to avoid letter collision — e.g., MT (multitaper), HB (Hann + box), WE (Welch).
  - Remove both in-figure panel titles; migrate ν values and half-power widths into the caption.
  - Rewrite along these lines:
    "**Fig. 7. The everyday spectral estimators agree to within their own noise.** Single 12-s electroencephalogram (EEG) epoch (channel Cz, sampling rate 256 Hz; same record as Fig. 1; n = 1 epoch, N = 3072 samples). (a) Power spectral density estimates, in dB re 1 µV²/Hz: Thomson multitaper with K = 7 Slepian (DPSS) tapers and equal weights (thick black; equivalent degrees of freedom ν = 14.0, half-power width 0.91 Hz); Welch's method with nine Hann-tapered segments of 1.5 s stepped by 0.81 s (thin blue; ν = 17.6, 0.97 Hz); Hann-tapered periodogram smoothed by a boxcar of half-width W = 0.5 Hz (orange; ν = 8.9, 1.03 Hz). (b) Differences from the multitaper estimate over the M = 481 frequency bins spanning 0.1–40 Hz: Hann+box minus multitaper (orange), median |Δ| = 1.6 dB, 95th percentile 4.9 dB; Welch minus multitaper (blue), median |Δ| = 0.9 dB, 95th percentile 5.9 dB. These magnitudes match the estimators' own sampling noise: the asymptotic standard deviation of a log-spectral estimate is (10/ln 10)·√(2/ν) ≈ 4.34√(2/ν) dB, i.e. 1.6, 1.5 and 2.1 dB for ν = 14.0, 17.6 and 8.9, respectively. No hypothesis test is applied; the comparison is descriptive. Abbreviations: DPSS, discrete prolate spheroidal sequence; ν, equivalent degrees of freedom; K, number of tapers; W, smoother half-bandwidth."
  - If any quantity in panel (b) is intended as an inferential bound rather than an empirical percentile, state the estimator, its degrees of freedom, and the confidence level explicitly.
  - Re-export as vector PDF/EPS (or 600 DPI TIFF, RGB) at 181 mm width, <10 MB, with 8–10 pt Arial/Helvetica labels and ≥0.5 pt lines.

STATISTICAL DETAIL: Partial — ν is quoted for every estimator (good, satisfies the dof requirement), but no test, test statistic, or p-value accompanies the (d) − (e) comparison in (h); only a median |difference| is reported with no dispersion measure (IQR) or interval
ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: EEG (electroencephalogram); NW (time–bandwidth product); K (number of Slepian tapers); ν (equivalent/effective degrees of freedom — symbol used five times but never defined); W (smoothing half-width, used but not named as such); dB (reference quantity not stated, e.g. dB re 1 µV²/Hz); "ictal"/"pre-ictal" acceptable but the seizure onset/offset times should be given
COLOR/SYMBOL KEY:   Partial — the line key for (f,g) is in-figure and adequate, but (i) the single colorbar drawn beside (d) is not declared as shared by (c–e), (ii) its units (dB) and the common color scale limits are not stated in the caption, and (iii) the blue vs. red dashed cursors in (a) are not mapped to the pre-ictal (f) and ictal (g) instants respectively
SELF-CONTAINED:     No — reader cannot determine ν's meaning, the dB reference, whether the three spectrograms share one color scale, the sample sizes behind (h), or the identity/availability of the dataset
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (g) carries a duplicated, overlapping y-axis label ("dB" printed twice). This is a rendering defect and must be corrected before submission.
  - [BLOCKING] ν (equivalent degrees of freedom) is the central quantity of the figure yet is never defined in the caption. Per the journal's requirement to state the estimator's degrees of freedom when noise levels are compared, the symbol must be expanded explicitly.
  - [BLOCKING] No sample size is given anywhere: the number of 2-s windows forming each spectrogram (≈ 590 at a 1-s step, but must be stated), and the number of pixels entering the (h) histogram, are required to interpret the distribution in (h).
  - [BLOCKING] Each panel contains a descriptive in-figure title ("(a) scalp EEG, channel C4 …", "(c) Hann periodogram, 2-s windows: ν = 2", etc.). IEEE TBME prohibits titles inside the figure; only the lowercase panel letters may remain and the descriptive text must migrate to the caption.
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (h) compares two estimators' noise levels but reports only "median |diff| 1.5 dB" with no dispersion, no interval, and no test. Either add a quantitative comparison (e.g. IQR and a paired test with statistic, dof and exact p) or state explicitly in the caption that the comparison is descriptive only.
  - [ADVISORY] The colorbar beside (d) is ambiguous: state in the caption that it applies to (c), (d) and (e) with identical limits, and give its units.
  - [ADVISORY] The dB axis in (f,g) and the colorbars lack a reference: specify "dB re 1 µV²/Hz" (or equivalent) once in the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] The two dashed cursors in (a) are colored differently (blue, red) but the caption treats them as one class; map each color to its panel.
  - [ADVISORY] The record contains an obvious flat/low-power interval (~365–400 s) and a narrow transient near 400 s that dominate the spectrograms; if these are amplifier saturation, electrode events, or post-ictal suppression they should be named, otherwise readers will mistake them for estimator artifacts.
  - [ADVISORY] Seizure onset and offset times of the shaded interval are not stated; give them numerically so the shading can be verified against (b).
  - [ADVISORY] "ELROND seizure-annotation set" is given without citation, version, accession, or record identifier, and the channel-selection rule ("largest ictal rise in 2–20 Hz power") is described without stating over which interval the rise was measured. Both hinder reproducibility; confirm the dataset name is the correct, citable one.
  - [ADVISORY] Sampling rate is stated (200 Hz) but the anti-alias/filter settings and any notch filtering are not; add a one-clause preprocessing statement.
  - [ADVISORY] Clipping at ±500 µV is mentioned for (a); confirm whether the clipped samples were used for the spectral estimates or only for display, since clipping would bias (c–h).
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Move all in-figure panel titles into the caption and leave only lowercase "(a)–(h)" in the image; re-export at ≥600 DPI and fix the duplicated "dB" label in (g).
  - Define ν at first use, e.g. "ν denotes the equivalent degrees of freedom of the chi-square approximation to each estimator."
  - Add sample sizes: "Each spectrogram comprises N = ⟨…⟩ 2-s windows stepped by 1 s; the histogram in (h) pools M = ⟨…⟩ time–frequency pixels (⟨…⟩ windows × ⟨…⟩ frequency bins, 0–30 Hz)."
  - Declare the shared color scale: "A single colorbar (shown beside (d)) applies to (c–e); limits ⟨min⟩ to ⟨max⟩ dB re 1 µV²/Hz."
  - Map the cursors: "the blue dashed line marks t = 247 s (pre-ictal, panel (f)) and the red dashed line t = 318 s (ictal, panel (g)); the shaded band spans the annotated seizure, t = ⟨onset⟩ to ⟨offset⟩ s."
  - Expand abbreviations on first use: EEG, and "multitaper with time–bandwidth product NW = 2 and K = 3 Slepian tapers"; name W as the box-smoother half-width.
  - For (h), report median and IQR of the difference and, if a claim of equivalence is intended, add the test with statistic, dof and exact p-value; otherwise append "descriptive comparison; no hypothesis test was performed."
  - Add a preprocessing clause (filter band, notch, clipping applied to display only or to analysis) and a citable dataset identifier with record number and license.

### figure9.png

---
TITLE:              Present & bold — "Two seizures in one clip."
PANEL DESCRIPTIONS: Complete (a) described individually; (b–d) described in order within a grouped sentence
ERROR BARS:         Not present
SAMPLE SIZE:        Missing — no subject/record identifier, no sampling rate, no number of analysis windows behind each spectrogram column
STATISTICAL DETAIL: Partial — equivalent degrees of freedom (ν = 2, 6, 4.8) appear only inside the panels, not in the caption; window length (2 s), window step/overlap, and detrending are not in the caption; no quantitative basis for the "renders it through noise" noise-level comparison (e.g., variance or dB standard deviation per estimator)
ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: NW (time–bandwidth product), K (number of Slepian tapers), ν (equivalent degrees of freedom), W (smoothing half-width), dB reference level (dB re what? µV²/Hz), Fp1 (10–20 electrode position); EEG acceptable if defined at first use in text
COLOR/SYMBOL KEY:   Partial — shared dB colorbar is present in-figure but its reference/normalisation is not stated; the pink shading in (a) is not named as the seizure marker colour, and the source of the seizure annotation (expert marking? clinical report?) is unstated
SELF-CONTAINED:     No — caption defers panels (b–d) to "Spectrograms as in Fig. X", so window length, overlap, taper family, and dB scaling must be retrieved from another caption

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Estimator degrees of freedom are not stated in the caption even though the caption explicitly compares noise levels across the three estimators. Journal policy requires ν (and the number of windows/segments behind each estimate) in the caption text, not only as in-panel annotation.
  - [BLOCKING] No sample-size information: record/subject identity, sampling rate, number of 2-s windows per spectrogram, and window step are all absent.
  - [BLOCKING] Caption is not self-contained: "Spectrograms as in Fig.~\ref{fig:eeg}" forces the reader to another figure for the core method parameters.
  - [BLOCKING] Each panel carries an in-figure title line ("(a) scalp EEG, channel FP1 ...", "(b) Hann periodogram, 2-s windows: ν = 2", etc.). IEEE TBME prohibits titles inside the figure; these must be reduced to bare lowercase panel labels with the content moved into the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Raster resolution: 1376 px across a 181 mm double-column width is ≈193 DPI, below the 300 DPI minimum (600 DPI preferred for line art/axes). Regenerate as vector (PDF/EPS) with the spectrogram images rasterised at ≥300 DPI, or export the whole figure at ≥2140 px wide.
  - [ADVISORY] Channel label inconsistency: the caption writes "Fp1" while panel (a) axis/title writes "FP1". Unify.
  - [ADVISORY] dB colorbar has no stated reference; absolute dB values (15–43) are uninterpretable without units (e.g., dB re 1 µV²/Hz).
  - [ADVISORY] Amplitude clipping at ±500 µV is stated in the caption but not marked in the panel; the trace visibly saturates near 200 s and 360 s, which a reader may misread as signal.
  - [ADVISORY] "descends from about 20 Hz to 3 Hz over two minutes" is a quantitative claim with no measurement method or uncertainty; either soften or cite how the ridge frequency was estimated.
  - [ADVISORY] Shading colour and the provenance of the seizure onset/offset times are not specified.
  - [ADVISORY] Axis and panel-label font sizes should be verified at final printed width; at 181 mm the tick labels appear near the 8 pt lower bound, and nothing may fall below 6 pt.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Delete the four in-panel title strings; keep only lowercase "(a)"–"(d)" placed at the upper-left of each axes.
  - Rewrite the caption to be parameter-complete and self-contained, e.g.:
    "**Two seizures in one 10-min clip.** (a) Scalp EEG, channel Fp1 referenced to the common average, subject S<xx>, record <id>, sampled at <fs> Hz and displayed clipped at ±500 µV; pink bands mark the two seizures as annotated by <expert/clinical report> (onset–offset: 50–188 s and 297–450 s). (b–d) Spectrograms of the same trace, all on the shared colour scale at right (dB re 1 µV²/Hz): (b) single-taper Hann periodogram, 2-s windows with 1.5-s overlap (n = <N> windows), equivalent degrees of freedom ν = 2; (c) multitaper estimate on the same windows, time–bandwidth product NW = 2 with K = 3 Slepian tapers, ν = 2K = 6; (d) the periodogram of (b) smoothed across frequency with a box of half-width W = 1.0 Hz, ν = 4.8. Both seizures appear as a harmonic stack descending from ≈20 Hz to ≈3 Hz over ≈2 min. The two smoothed estimators (c, d) have comparable ν and yield visually equivalent renderings; the ν = 2 periodogram in (b) has ≈5.5 dB residual standard deviation per cell and renders the same structure through speckle. NW, time–bandwidth product; K, number of tapers; ν, equivalent degrees of freedom."
  - Insert the numeric number of analysis windows N and, if the noise claim is retained, the per-estimator residual standard deviation in dB so the reader can verify the ν-vs-variance comparison from the figure alone.
  - Unify "Fp1"/"FP1" between caption and axis label.
  - Add the colorbar reference to the colorbar label itself ("dB re 1 µV²/Hz") rather than only in the caption.

## Statistical Integrity Checker

### efigure1.png

---
**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — This is a deterministic analytic/numerical curve plot (expected-value smoothing kernels for fixed $N$, $W$), not a display of sampled data. Line plots on a linear scale near the main lobe plus a dB scale for the skirts is the correct and conventional presentation; no bar/pie/summary-statistic concerns arise. One presentational risk: the right panel carries four curves with **no legend of its own**, so the reader must map colours across panels; if the figure is reproduced or read in greyscale the green/orange (Hann/Bohman) and red/blue (multitaper/raw) pairs are not separable. Recommend adding direct curve labels or repeating the legend in the right panel.

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Not applicable / Visible in the sense that matters — There is no sampling distribution here; the "n" equivalents are the design constants, and both are stated in the title ($N=256$, $NW=4$) and the taper count ($K=2NW-1=7$) in the legend. This is adequate. Two quantities that *are* needed to reproduce the curves are **not** stated in or inferable from the figure: (i) the box half-width used for the three "then box" curves (the caption says half-width $W$, but the drafted caption text does not match this figure — see ISSUES), and (ii) the frequency grid / zero-padding factor on which the kernels were evaluated.

**COMPARISON:** Valid — The comparison is the scientifically correct one: all four kernels are placed at *equal design bandwidth*, which is exactly the fair-comparison condition, and the ideal box is included as the reference. The dotted verticals at $\pm 4/N$ in the right panel usefully carry the band edge across from the left panel. No pairing/independence issues apply. Minor concern: the claim "all main lobes have nearly the same width" is asserted in the caption but the half-power width is not marked on the figure; a horizontal line at half-peak (or at $-3$ dB) would let the reader verify the equal-bandwidth claim rather than take it on trust.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** Present — but defensible, with two caveats. (a) Right panel: the ordinate is clipped at the top ($\approx +5$ dB), so all four main-lobe peaks run off-panel. This is standard for a sidelobe display and the panel is titled accordingly, but it means the *relative* peak heights (visible in the left panel, where multitaper overshoots to $\approx0.145$ vs $0.125$ for the others) cannot be judged here. (b) The bottom is clipped at $-100$ dB; the Bohman curve reaches $\approx -91$ dB at the panel edge and is still falling, so the reader cannot see where it levels off. Neither truncation exaggerates a difference — if anything the top clipping *understates* the multitaper main-lobe ripple — so this is advisory, not misleading. The left panel correctly includes zero on the ordinate.

**OVERPLOTTING:** Present (mild) — In the left panel the ideal box (black dotted), Hann+box (green) and Bohman+box (orange) are nearly coincident over $|f|N \lesssim 2.5$ and on the rising flanks; the orange largely hides the green, and the plateau at $0.125$ is a near-total overlap of box/Hann/Bohman. There is also a visible faint pink/light artefact along the right flank around $f N \approx 4$–$4.5$ (partially behind the legend box), which reads as an unexplained fifth trace or a rendering artefact and should be identified or removed. The legend also sits on top of the Bohman/Hann flank data in the lower middle of the left panel; move it outside the axes or to the upper-left dead space.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** No concern — No inferential annotations, no $p$-values, no error bars. Nothing to correct.

**TIME SERIES:** Not applicable — Frequency-domain kernel shapes, not longitudinal data. Note for completeness that the abscissa unit "frequency $(1/N)$" is well chosen and the two panels use different ranges ($\pm10$ vs $\pm25$), which is appropriate but should be stated in the caption so readers do not read the panels as the same window.

**ISSUES:**
- **[BLOCKING]** **Caption/figure mismatch.** The draft caption supplied with `efigure1.png` describes band powers from EEG estimators on two seizure clips and is followed by a `tabular` environment, `\label{tab:bandpower}`, `\small`, and `\end{table}` — i.e. it is the caption and body of the band-power **table**, not of this figure. The correct caption for this image is evidently the one appearing later in the source ("**Kernels at the same design bandwidth** ($N=256$, $W=4/N$; Table~\ref{tab:dof})…", currently attached to `fig1_kernels.png`). Resolve before review can proceed: the figure must be paired with its own caption and label, and the duplication with `fig1_kernels.png` must be clarified.
- **[BLOCKING]** **Possible duplicate publication of the same panel.** `efigure1.png` and the appendix figure `fig1_kernels.png` appear to be the same kernel comparison at $N=256$, $W=4/N$. If the supplementary "S1" figure is identical to the main-text figure, remove one; if they differ (e.g. different $NW$, different smoothing box), the difference must be stated explicitly in both captions, since the main text elsewhere uses $NW=2$ for the EEG analysis while this figure uses $NW=4$.
- **[ADVISORY]** **Under-resolved sidelobe nulls.** The Hann+box (green) and Bohman+box (orange) traces in the right panel show a pronounced staircase/terraced shape rather than the expected scalloped sidelobe structure with deep nulls. This is the signature of evaluating the kernel on too coarse a frequency grid (insufficient zero-padding), which systematically *smooths over* the nulls and can bias the apparent sidelobe envelope. Because the caption's headline quantitative claim is "the skirts differ by 40 dB", the plotted skirt levels must be grid-independent.
- **[ADVISORY]** Peak-sidelobe and leakage numbers quoted in Appendix B are defined relative to $1.5\times$ the half-power half-width, but neither the half-power width nor the $1.5\times$ threshold is marked on the figure, so the reader cannot connect the figure to Table~\ref{tab:dof}.
- **[ADVISORY]** No legend in the right panel; colour-only encoding of four curves; probable failure in greyscale and for red–green colour-vision deficiency (the red multitaper and green Hann curves are the two most important contrasts in the right panel).
- **[ADVISORY]** Unexplained faint light-pink trace on the right flank of the left panel, partly occluded by the legend box.
- **[ADVISORY]** The left panel's ordinate label "kernel (linear, $\times N$)" and the right panel's "kernel (dB)" do not state the normalisation reference for the dB scale (presumably the main-lobe peak, or $\times N$ as on the left). Ambiguous dB references are a common source of misread leakage figures.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Attach the correct caption ("Kernels at the same design bandwidth…") to this figure, give it a figure label rather than `tab:bandpower`, and return the band-power caption and `tabular` block to the table environment.
- Reconcile `efigure1.png` with `fig1_kernels.png`: delete the duplicate, or state in each caption exactly which parameters differ.
- Recompute both panels on a densely zero-padded grid (e.g. $\geq 16N$ points, or evaluate $H=|\mathcal{W}|^2*G$ analytically) and confirm the plotted sidelobe levels are unchanged under further refinement; state the padding factor in the caption. The staircase artefact should disappear.
- Add a legend (or direct end-of-curve labels) to the right panel; adopt a colourblind-safe, greyscale-distinguishable palette and give each estimator a distinct dash pattern as well as a colour.
- Move the left-panel legend out of the data region (upper-left corner or below the axes) and identify or remove the faint pink trace.
- Mark the half-power level/width on the left panel (or add a thin horizontal line at $-3$ dB and at the $1.5\times$ half-width on the right) so the "equal bandwidth" and "40 dB" claims are verifiable from the figure.
- In the right panel, either extend the lower limit (e.g. to $-120$ dB) so the Bohman skirt's behaviour is visible, or state in the caption that the ordinate is clipped at $-100$ dB and that the main lobes are deliberately cut off at the top.
- State the dB reference level and the box half-width used for the three "then box" kernels in the caption, and note that the two panels use different abscissa ranges.
---

### efigure2.png

---

**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — A multi-trace line plot on a dB (log-power) ordinate is the standard and correct display for comparing spectral estimators against a known true PSD. No bar/pie concerns. However, the plot is doing too much work: six traces, two of which (multitaper *K*=7 and *K*=6) differ by exactly the quantity the figure exists to demonstrate, are plotted in the same crowded region. Consider a two-panel layout (estimates on top, **bias relative to the true PSD** — i.e., estimate minus true, in dB — on the bottom). The bias panel would let readers verify the "20 dB high" and "10 dB high" claims directly rather than by eye against a dashed black line.

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Partially visible — *N*=1024 and *W*=4/N appear in the title, and *K*=7/*K*=6 in the legend. But the effective sample size for the statistical claims in the caption is the **number of realizations, which is 1** and is stated only obliquely ("Another realization"). Several estimator parameters needed to reproduce the figure are absent from the figure: the box-smoother half-width (in bins or Hz) used for both box-smoothed curves, whether *W*=4/N means *NW*=4 (time–bandwidth product) or a literal bandwidth of 4/N cycles/sample, and which taper was dropped to get *K*=6 (index 7, presumably, but unstated). The AR(4) coefficients / innovation variance are not given.

**COMPARISON:** Concern — The six estimates are all computed from the *same single realization*, so the curves are strongly dependent; this is the right design for a visual demonstration but it makes the numerical floor offsets ("floors 20 dB high", "floors 10 dB high") single-draw realizations of a random quantity, not estimates. Since these traces are paired (common data, common frequency grid), the paired structure should be exploited — a difference-from-truth panel is the paired visualization; the current overlay is the unpaired presentation of paired data. Additionally, the Hann-then-box curve tracks the truth *below* the dashed line over roughly 0.30–0.50, which could be negative bias or could be the smoother's variance; the reader cannot distinguish these from one realization.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** Present, minor — The ordinate is a dB scale, so the usual "bar chart truncated at a nonzero baseline" hazard does not apply, and the range −40 to ~+48 dB is a reasonable ~88 dB window. One real concern: the raw periodogram appears to touch or be clipped at the −40 dB lower limit near 0.06 and ~0.40–0.46; periodogram nulls can go arbitrarily deep, so readers may not realize excursions are being cut off. State explicitly that the ordinate is clipped, or extend it.

**OVERPLOTTING:** Present — Substantial. Above ~0.25 cycles/sample the grey periodogram, red *K*=7, and orange *K*=6 traces form a dense band in which the orange curve is largely buried under the red, and the black dashed true PSD is intermittently occluded by red/orange. The very feature the figure is named for (the *K*=6 vs *K*=7 floor difference) is the least legible element. Also, red/green/orange is a poor palette for colour-vision deficiency, and the grey periodogram, drawn first, still visually dominates the noise floor.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** No concern — No inferential annotations, p-values, or confidence bands are present. (Relatedly: **no uncertainty is shown at all.** For a figure making quantitative bias claims, per-estimator confidence intervals — e.g., the chi-square-with-2K-degrees-of-freedom interval for the multitaper estimates — or Monte Carlo quantile envelopes should be shown or reported.)

**TIME SERIES:** Not applicable — Frequency-domain estimate on a dense, evenly spaced Fourier grid; no gaps or uneven intervals, so no misleading interpolation.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The caption's quantitative claims ("floors 20 dB high", "floors 10 dB high") are read off a single realization with no uncertainty shown. These are properties of the estimator's *expectation*, and must be supported by either an analytic bias calculation or a Monte Carlo average over many realizations, with the number of realizations reported. As drawn, the figure cannot support the numbers in the caption.
- [BLOCKING] The figure is not reproducible from the information given: the box-smoother width is never stated (it governs the blue and green curves entirely), the meaning of *W*=4/N is ambiguous (bandwidth vs. time–bandwidth product *NW*=4), the dropped taper's index is unspecified, and the AR(4) parameters are absent.
- [ADVISORY] Overplotting hides the paper's central effect: the orange *K*=6 trace is largely occluded by the red *K*=7 trace across 0.25–0.50, exactly the band where the two are claimed to differ.
- [ADVISORY] The ordinate appears to clip deep periodogram nulls at −40 dB without any indication that clipping is occurring.
- [ADVISORY] The caption enumerates the curves in a different order than the legend and omits the true PSD (AR4) trace entirely, forcing the reader to map caption text to legend entries by inference.
- [ADVISORY] Red/green/orange palette is not colour-vision-deficiency safe; the grey periodogram is visually heavier than the estimates it is meant to contextualize.
- [ADVISORY] No uncertainty is displayed for any estimator despite the figure's purpose being a bias comparison.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Convert to a two-panel figure: (a) current overlay, (b) bias panel plotting (estimate − true PSD) in dB versus frequency, with a horizontal zero line. The 20 dB and 10 dB offsets then become directly readable, and the paired structure of the comparison is made explicit.
- Replace or supplement the single realization with a Monte Carlo average over *M* independent realizations (report *M* in the title or caption), and overlay 5th–95th percentile envelopes for at least the multitaper *K*=7 and *K*=6 estimates. Retain one single-realization panel if the visual texture of the estimates is itself of interest, but label it as such.
- Report in the caption or an on-figure annotation: the AR(4) coefficients and innovation variance, the box-smoother half-width in bins, whether *NW*=4 or *W*=4/N literally, and the index of the dropped taper.
- Reduce overplotting: lower the grey periodogram to alpha≈0.3 and draw it first; increase line width and use distinguishable line styles (e.g., solid vs. dash-dot) for *K*=7 vs. *K*=6; or add an inset zoom on 0.30–0.50 where the floors separate.
- Adopt a colour-vision-safe palette (e.g., Okabe–Ito) and keep the true PSD as the topmost drawn element so it is never occluded.
- Extend the lower ordinate limit until no trace is clipped, or state "ordinate clipped at −40 dB; periodogram nulls extend below" in the caption.
- Reorder the caption's clause list to match the legend order and add a clause for the true PSD trace.

### efigure3.png

---
**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — These are deterministic kernel/design properties (equivalent dof, out-of-band kernel mass, peak side-lobe level) computed analytically for a fixed record length, not sampled measurements of random variables. Connected-marker line plots against the achieved half-power bandwidth are the right idiom: they show a monotone design trade-off curve per method and correctly place all methods on a common resolution axis. No bar chart, no pie chart, no summary-statistic-with-error-bar concerns apply. Log-scaled ordinate in the center panel is appropriate given the ~7-decade spread in leakage; dB in the right panel likewise.

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Visible (in the relevant sense) — There is no biological/experimental *n* here. The two quantities that matter are (i) record length, $N=256$, which is in the title and caption, and (ii) the number of swept parameter settings per method, which is directly inferable because every evaluated configuration is drawn as a marker. Markers-plus-lines is the correct choice here and satisfies the "show the individual observations" principle: the discrete design points are not hidden behind a smoothed curve. No uncertainty display is expected or needed because these are exact functionals of known windows — but the caption should say so explicitly (see fixes).

**COMPARISON:** Concern — Two points.
(1) The right panel plots two different quantities on one axis: true peak side lobe for flat-topped kernels, versus "main-lobe skirt" for the Gaussian-smoothed and Welch kernels. The caption discloses this, but nothing in the graphic distinguishes the two definitions, so a reader scanning the figure will read the grey and pink traces as side-lobe levels comparable to the others.
(2) The curves are a within-method parameter sweep re-expressed on a shared x-axis — that is the correct "compare at equal resolution" construction and is a strength. However, each method's sweep terminates at a different maximum bandwidth (Hann→Gaussian stops near 9.5, most stop near 16, Welch reaches 24.6), and the reason for the differing ranges is not stated, which invites the inference that a method was stopped because it was losing.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** None misleading — Left panel ordinate runs from ~0 to 60 and includes the origin, so the near-linear dof growth is not visually exaggerated. Abscissa starts near 1 rather than 0 in all panels, which is legitimate because bandwidth is a strictly positive ratio quantity and 0 is unreachable. Log and dB axes are correctly labeled with units (1/N, dB), so no hidden compression.

**OVERPLOTTING:** Present (mild) — Curve crowding rather than point-cloud density is the issue. In the left panel, seven of eight traces converge and cross repeatedly for bandwidth < 6, and in the center/right panels the red/brown and purple/pink pairs are hard to separate at small bandwidth. All series use the same circular marker, so discrimination is carried entirely by hue: the figure will fail for deuteranopic readers and in grayscale print.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** No concern — No inferential tests, p-values, or significance annotations are present, and none would be meaningful for deterministic design quantities. Nothing to correct.

**TIME SERIES:** Not applicable — The abscissa is achieved half-power bandwidth, not time. The related concern is interpolation across gaps in the parameter sweep: the grey Welch trace is connected across a wide unsampled interval (~16 to ~24.6) in all three panels, and for multitaper methods the taper count $K$ is integer-valued, so the straight segments between markers represent configurations that are not actually realizable. The line segments are legitimate as visual grouping but should not be read as a continuum.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Right panel mixes two non-equivalent metrics (peak side lobe vs. main-lobe skirt for Gaussian and Welch) on a single axis with identical visual encoding. Caption disclosure is insufficient; the figure itself misleads on a like-for-like comparison.
- [ADVISORY] Left panel title reads "variance: higher is better" while the ordinate is equivalent degrees of freedom. Higher $\nu$ means *lower* variance, so the title states the opposite of the axis semantics as written.
- [ADVISORY] Series are distinguished by color only, with a single marker shape throughout; not colorblind-safe or grayscale-safe, and the red/brown and purple/pink pairs are already ambiguous in color.
- [ADVISORY] Line segments connect discretely realizable configurations (integer $K$) and span a wide unsampled gap for Welch, implying achievable intermediate operating points that do not exist.
- [ADVISORY] Per-method sweep ranges end at different bandwidths with no stated reason, which can be misread as selective truncation.
- [ADVISORY] Absence of uncertainty indicators is correct but unexplained; a reader trained on empirical figures may wonder where the error bars went.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- In the right panel, plot the Gaussian and Welch traces with a distinct style (dashed line, open markers) and add an in-axes annotation such as "dashed: main-lobe skirt, no flat top," or split the quantity into a separate panel. Repeat the qualifier in the y-axis label, e.g. "peak side lobe / main-lobe skirt (dB)."
- Retitle the left panel "variance: lower is better (higher $\nu$)" or simply "equivalent dof: higher is better" to remove the contradiction with the axis.
- Assign a distinct marker shape per method (circle, square, triangle, diamond, cross, ...) and adopt a colorblind-safe palette; verify legibility in grayscale. Consider direct end-of-line labels to shrink the eight-entry legend.
- State in the caption that quantities are exact deterministic functionals of each smoothing kernel at $N=256$, involve no Monte Carlo estimation, and therefore carry no sampling uncertainty; note that connecting lines are guides only and that admissible configurations are the plotted markers (integer $K$ for multitaper).
- Note in the caption why each sweep terminates where it does (e.g., $K$ limited by $2NW$, Welch limited by segment count), or extend sweeps to a common maximum bandwidth. Add markers in the Welch gap or break the line across the unsampled interval.
- Optional: an inset or log-scaled x zoom over bandwidth 1–6 for the left panel, where seven curves overlap and the ordering is currently unreadable.
---

### efigure4.png

---
**Context note:** This is a deterministic methodological/analytical figure (window functions, eigen-decompositions of a quadratic form $Q$, weight spectra, and kernel transfer functions computed for fixed $N=256$, $W=4/N$). It contains no sampled empirical data, no error bars, and no group comparisons, so most distributional-integrity criteria are structurally inapplicable. The review below reports that explicitly rather than forcing the checklist, and then addresses the issues that *are* live: clipping, unlabeled axes, aliasing of high-frequency traces, and a panel-label mismatch between caption and figure.

---
PLOT TYPE:          Appropriate — Line traces for tapers and kernels and bar charts for the eigen-weight ladder $c_k$ are the correct choices. The bar charts are *not* summary-statistic bar graphs: each bar is a single exact eigenvalue-derived weight, not a mean over replicates, so the usual "show the individual points / use a dot plot" objection does not apply. No error bars are present and none should be. Stacked vertical offsets in rows 1–2 are a standard and readable convention for taper banks. No pie charts.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Visible (as parameters, the relevant analogue here) — $N=256$, $W=4/N$, $K=2NW-1=7$ are stated in the suptitle; per-panel counts (7 offsets, 4 modulations, 4 of $N$ offsets shown, tapers $k=0..3$ of 7) are given in the panel subtitles. There is no statistical $n$ to report. **However**, rows 1 and 2 show only 4 of the tapers in each bank without stating in-panel whether the remaining members are qualitatively similar or divergent; the bottom-row kernel is computed from the *full* bank, so the reader cannot verify the kernel from what is displayed.

COMPARISON:         Valid, with one bookkeeping concern — The four columns are compared on shared, identical axes in every row (sample $t$ 0–255; weight 0–0.45; dB −80 to ≈+7; frequency offset ±24/N), which is exactly right for a four-way construction comparison. The concern is not statistical but referential: the caption's panel attributions appear shifted relative to the figure. The caption says "the first four eigen-tapers of $Q$ (for (c) the Slepian sequences; for (d), whose weights are equal, any orthonormal basis...)", but in the figure the row-2 title reading "hidden tapers: the Slepians (with equal weights / any orthonormal basis of their span would do)" sits under column **(d)**, while column (c) is labeled "eigenvectors of $Q$ ($k=0..3$)". Either the caption should read "(for (d) the Slepians, whose weights are equal, any orthonormal basis of the span would do)" or the panels are mislabeled. This must be resolved.

AXIS TRUNCATION:    None misleading, but clipping is present — The weight axis correctly starts at 0, so relative bar heights are faithful; the flat ladders in (c)/(d) versus the steep decay in (b) are not exaggerated. The −80 dB floor is conventional for kernel plots and is not deceptive. The genuine problem is **clipping at the top of the axis rather than truncation at the bottom**: in all four bottom-row panels the main lobe peak is cut off by the upper axis limit (the traces exit the frame near 0 dB with no visible apex), so the peak level — and hence the true dynamic range over which "mass beyond $2W$" is accumulated — cannot be read. Similarly, in row 1 column (c) the orange trace is cut at the top-left frame edge and the red trace exits the top-right frame edge, so those tapers are shown incompletely.

OVERPLOTTING:       Present — Two distinct forms. (i) In bottom-row column (d) the kernel exhibits ~30 sidelobe ripples across the plotted band; at this pixel width the ripple envelope is at or beyond the resolution limit and the trace risks aliasing, so apparent sidelobe heights may be rendering artifacts rather than true extrema. The same applies to the fine sidelobe structure in column (c). (ii) In rows 1–2, adjacent stacked traces approach one another (e.g. row 1 column (c), where the orange and green excursions nearly touch the neighboring baselines), making it ambiguous which curve a given excursion belongs to.

MULTIPLE COMPARISONS: No concern — No hypothesis tests, p-values, or significance annotations appear anywhere in the figure, and none are appropriate for deterministic quantities. The reported $\nu = 2(\sum c)^2/\sum c^2$ values (13.4, 11.1, 17.2, 14.0) are equivalent-degrees-of-freedom summaries of the weight vectors, not test statistics, so no correction question arises.

TIME SERIES:        Not applicable — The abscissa "sample $t$" in rows 1–2 indexes position within a fixed-length window, not observation time; there are no missing timepoints, no uneven intervals, and no interpolation across gaps. Traces are densely sampled over the full 0–255 support.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Caption/panel label mismatch: the caption assigns the Slepian sequences to (c) and the arbitrary orthonormal basis to (d), while the figure's row-2 titles assign "the Slepians (with equal weights / any orthonormal basis of their span would do)" to (d) and "eigenvectors of $Q$" to (c). A reader cannot determine which construction is being described.
  - [BLOCKING] Main-lobe peaks in all four bottom-row kernel panels are clipped by the upper y-limit; the peak dB level is unreadable, so "half-power width" (5.8/N, 7.2/N, 8.0/N, 7.4/N) cannot be verified against the plotted curve, nor can the reference level for "mass beyond $2W$" be established.
  - [ADVISORY] Rows 1 and 2 have no y-axis ticks or labels. The vertical offsets are arbitrary, which is fine, but the panels do not state that amplitudes are offset and unnormalized, so a reader may misread relative taper amplitudes across the stack.
  - [ADVISORY] Row 1 column (c): the orange trace is clipped at the upper-left frame boundary and the red trace exits the upper-right boundary, showing those two sinc-window offsets incompletely.
  - [ADVISORY] Possible aliasing of the high-ripple-count sidelobe structure in bottom-row columns (c) and (d); rendered sidelobe peak heights may not reflect true extrema at this resolution.
  - [ADVISORY] Only 4 of 7 (columns a, d) or 4 of N (column c) bank members are displayed, while the bottom-row kernels derive from the complete bank; the displayed subset is insufficient to reconstruct the kernel.
  - [ADVISORY] The gray shaded band (evidently the $\pm W$ or $2W$ region used for the "mass beyond $2W$" statistic) is not defined in the caption or in any legend.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Reconcile the caption with the panel titles; state unambiguously that (d) is the equal-weight Thomson multitaper whose eigen-tapers are determined only up to an orthonormal rotation of the Slepian span, and that (c)'s hidden tapers are the eigenvectors of $Q$.
  - Raise the upper y-limit of the bottom row (e.g. to +5 or +10 dB with the peak normalized to 0 dB) so every main lobe apex is inside the frame; alternatively normalize each kernel to 0 dB peak and annotate the −3 dB level with a horizontal reference line so the quoted half-power widths are visually checkable.
  - Add a note in the caption or on the panels that rows 1–2 traces are vertically offset with arbitrary, unnormalized amplitude; optionally add a short scale bar in the leftmost panel of each row.
  - Expand the y-limits of row 1 column (c) (or normalize the sinc windows) so the orange and red traces are fully contained.
  - Render the bottom row at higher horizontal resolution, or plot the sidelobe *envelope* alongside the raw trace, so that the ripple structure in (c) and (d) is not resolution-limited; state the frequency-grid oversampling factor used.
  - Define the gray shaded band explicitly in the caption, and state whether "mass beyond $2W$" is computed as a fraction of total kernel power.
  - Consider labeling the number of bank members actually plotted versus total directly on each row-1 panel (e.g. "4 of 256 offsets shown"), which column (c) already does well and the others could mirror.
  - State in the caption that $\nu = 2(\sum c)^2/\sum c^2$ is an equivalent-degrees-of-freedom summary, so it is not mistaken for an inferential quantity.

### figure1.png

---
PLOT TYPE:          Appropriate — Line plots of power spectral density vs. frequency are the correct display for this kind of numerical-equivalence demonstration; none of the bar/pie/error-bar concerns apply. One substantive display problem: in the left panel the three curves are visually indistinguishable, and the thick black (a) trace is drawn *under* the two thin traces, so the reader cannot verify agreement from that panel at all. The left panel is therefore decorative; all the evidential content lives in the right panel. Consider a thin-line + offset rendering, or an inset zoom on one peak, so the overlay carries information.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Not visible — The effective n here is **one** record (8 s, single channel, single subject), and this is not stated in the figure. The figure also omits: number of channels/subjects from which this record was drawn (and whether it is representative or hand-picked), and the window length L used for route (b) is given only in a legend string ("length 16N") with no indication that other L were examined. For a claim of the form "(b) converges as 1/L," a single L is n = 1 on the convergence axis; the stated rate cannot be read off this figure.

COMPARISON:         Concern — The comparison is intrinsically *paired* (all three estimates are computed from the identical data record at identical frequency bins), and the right panel correctly exploits this by plotting differences rather than overlaid magnitudes. Good. Two residual concerns: (i) the two difference traces have wildly different scales (10⁻¹³ vs 10⁻² dB) and are plotted on a single shared linear axis, which makes the paired comparison of *the two comparisons* impossible; (ii) the caption's headline claim ("(c) equals (a) to machine precision") is asserted, not shown, because at this axis scale the orange trace is a flat line at zero and would look identical if the error were 10⁻³ dB.

AXIS TRUNCATION:    Present — Two instances, neither an exaggeration of differences but both worth fixing:
  • Left panel y-axis starts at 15 dB rather than 0. For dB (already a log scale) this is conventional and not misleading; no action required beyond noting that absolute magnitudes should not be eyeballed as ratios.
  • Left panel x-axis stops at 40 Hz although the Nyquist frequency is 64 Hz. This is an *undisclosed truncation of the domain*: the reader cannot see whether the three routes agree in the 40–64 Hz band, which is precisely where a boxcar-smoothing route (c) and a sinc-window route (b) are most likely to diverge (edge handling at Nyquist, low SNR, aliasing of line noise near 50/60 Hz). State the truncation explicitly, or extend to 64 Hz.
  • Right panel y-axis is over-wide (±0.20) relative to the data (within ±0.06), which *understates* rather than overstates the discrepancy. This is the inverse of the usual sin but is still a scaling choice that flatters the result.

OVERPLOTTING:       Present — Complete curve occlusion in the left panel (three near-identical traces, ~1024/2 frequency bins each, thick black drawn first and hidden). In the right panel the blue trace is a dense high-frequency oscillation over ~320 plotted bins; the downward spikes to −0.05 dB are visually dominant while the distributional character of the residual (is it zero-mean? heteroscedastic in frequency? concentrated at spectral valleys?) is not readable. This matters substantively: the spikes appear to cluster below zero and below 20 Hz, which suggests the (b) residual is structured, not random, and may scale with local spectral curvature rather than being a uniform O(1/L) bound.

MULTIPLE COMPARISONS: No concern — No inferential pairwise tests are annotated; the "max" statistics in the legend are deterministic maxima over frequency, not test statistics. Note for the authors that a max-over-~320-bins is itself an extreme-value summary and will grow with the plotted bandwidth; report it alongside an RMS or a quantile so it is not read as a typical error.

TIME SERIES:        Not applicable — Abscissa is frequency, not time. No gaps or uneven sampling in the plotted domain; frequency bins are uniform, so the connecting interpolation is legitimate.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] The figure's central claim — that (c) equals (a) to ~3×10⁻¹³ dB — is not visually verifiable. On a linear ±0.20 dB axis the orange residual is indistinguishable from exact zero, and from any other value below ~0.005 dB. The numerical claim currently rests entirely on a legend annotation.
  - [BLOCKING] The convergence claim "(b) ... converges as 1/L" is unsupported by the figure, which shows a single window length (L = 16N). A rate claim requires at least three or four values of L.
  - [ADVISORY] Sample size and provenance of the data record are absent: n = 1 record, one channel, one subject, with no statement of representativeness. A reader cannot tell whether 0.056 dB is typical or best-case.
  - [ADVISORY] Undisclosed domain truncation at 40 Hz (Nyquist = 64 Hz), omitting the band where the three routes are most likely to disagree.
  - [ADVISORY] Total curve occlusion in the left panel; the thick black (a) trace is hidden beneath (b) and (c), so the overlay conveys no evidence of agreement.
  - [ADVISORY] The blue residual appears structured (spikes predominantly negative, concentrated below ~20 Hz), yet is summarised by a single max. The structure is scientifically informative and is being discarded.
  - [ADVISORY] Minor numeric inconsistency between legend (2.7×10⁻¹³ dB, 0.056 dB) and caption (3×10⁻¹³ dB, 0.06 dB). Harmless rounding, but make it consistent so readers do not hunt for a second quantity.
  - [ADVISORY] The right-panel y-axis is ~3× wider than the data range, compressing the only visible discrepancy.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Split the right panel into two stacked sub-panels with independent axes, or plot |difference| on a log y-axis spanning 10⁻¹⁴ to 10⁻¹ dB. Either lets both residuals be read at their natural scale and makes the "machine precision" claim visible rather than asserted. Add a horizontal reference line at the double-precision noise floor for the dB values involved.
  - Add a convergence panel: max (and RMS) |(b) − (a)| versus L for L ∈ {N, 2N, 4N, 8N, 16N, 32N} on log–log axes, with a reference line of slope −1. This converts the 1/L assertion into evidence and costs one small panel.
  - Extend both panels to the Nyquist frequency, or state the 0–40 Hz restriction and its rationale in the caption.
  - Redraw the left panel with three thin lines of equal weight plus an inset zoom (e.g. 7.5–9 Hz, the tallest peak, and one spectral valley near 31 Hz where the residual spikes occur), or apply small vertical offsets with the offsets declared in the caption.
  - Replace the single max statistic with max, RMS, and 95th-percentile of the residual over the plotted band, and state the band, since the max is bandwidth-dependent.
  - Report the residual's structure: either overlay the local spectral curvature, or state in the caption that (b)'s error concentrates at spectral minima, so readers do not misread 0.056 dB as a uniform bound.
  - State n and provenance in the caption: number of subjects/channels, how this record was selected, and whether the residual magnitudes are typical across records (a one-line summary such as "max |(b) − (a)| = 0.05–0.08 dB across 20 channels" would settle it).
  - Add explicit units/definitions for W in both normalized and Hz terms in the axis or caption, and state that (a) uses all N tapers with eigenvalue weights λ_k so readers can reproduce the identity.

### figure2.png

---
**Scope note:** This is a simulation/method-demonstration figure (spectral estimates of a synthetic AR process), not a plot of experimental group data. Most of the standard checklist items (bar-vs-dot plots, paired comparisons, multiple-comparison annotation) are not applicable. I have applied the items that do transfer — sample/replicate visibility, axis handling, overplotting, and misleading rendering/interpolation — and note the non-applicable items explicitly.

PLOT TYPE:          Appropriate — Line overlays on dB axes are the conventional and correct display for PSD estimates and kernel responses; no bar/pie misuse. One structural concern: panels (e)–(h) superimpose two quantities with different meanings (kernel response in dB re its own peak, and the true spectrum in dB re an unstated reference) on a single "kernel (dB re peak)" axis. The grey curve's vertical placement is an arbitrary offset chosen "for scale," which is not recoverable from the figure.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Not visible — $N=1024$ and "one record" appear only in the caption, not in the figure. More importantly, the effective replicate count is $n=1$ realization per panel, so the estimator's sampling variability is represented only implicitly by the printed $\nu$. The analogue of "show the individual data points" here — overlaying several independent realizations, or a chi-square-based variability band derived from $\nu$ — is not done, so a reader cannot distinguish reproducible bias (blur/leakage) from one-draw noise. This matters most for (d)/(h), where the claim "averages the two peaks into one" is asserted from a single draw in which the red trace in fact still shows two lobes.

COMPARISON:         Concern (technical, not statistical) — The four top panels are the intended comparison and are well matched (identical axes, identical true PSD, same record). Two items weaken it: (i) the comparison is unpaired across noise realizations unless the same random record is used in all four panels — the caption says "one record," which implies pairing, but the figure does not state it, and paired realization is essential for the argument; annotate it in-panel. (ii) Panel titles carry the conclusion ("just right," "too smooth," "leaky and noisy") rather than the stimulus; the criterion is given in the caption but the in-figure labels are evaluative.

AXIS TRUNCATION:    Present, not misleading — dB axes are necessarily bounded; top row clipped at $-40$ dB and bottom at $-80$ dB. Within each row the limits are identical, so comparisons are fair. One caveat: in (a) the red trace visibly runs off the bottom of the top-row window and in (b) excursions reach the $-40$ limit, so the floor is clipped without any indication. Clipping is doing rhetorical work in (a) (the "floor" argument) and should be marked.

OVERPLOTTING:       Present — (i) Panels (a) and (b) plot ~512 frequency bins across ~430 px at full opacity and full line weight; the noisy red trace substantially occludes the black true PSD it is supposed to be compared against, and the dense trace is subject to rendering/aliasing artifacts at this pixel density. (ii) Panel (e) is worse: the Fejér kernel's deep nulls, sampled on a coarse grid and joined by straight segments, produce a "picket fence" of downward spikes whose depths are an artifact of grid alignment, not a property of the kernel. The same applies to (f). A reader could misread the apparent null depth and the apparent skirt level. (iii) The in-panel legends in (a) and (e) sit over plotted data, and the (e) legend overlaps the kernel's main-lobe region.

MULTIPLE COMPARISONS: No concern — no inferential annotations, p-values, or pairwise brackets are present; none are appropriate here.

TIME SERIES:        Not applicable — abscissae are frequency, uniformly sampled and fully spanned, with no gaps or interpolation across missing regions. (Note: the straight-line interpolation issue flagged under Overplotting for panel (e) is the frequency-domain equivalent of misleading interpolation and should be fixed on those grounds.)

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Panels (e)/(f): the kernel is drawn as line segments through a frequency grid too coarse to resolve its nulls, so the plotted null depths and the visual density of the "picket fence" are rendering artifacts. Quantitative claims read off these panels (e.g., skirt levels, "skirts 20 to 50 dB lower") are not verifiable from the figure as drawn.
  - [BLOCKING] Panels (e)–(h): two quantities in different dB references share one axis, with the grey true-spectrum curve placed at an undisclosed vertical offset. The offset must be stated or the grey curve moved to a secondary axis, otherwise the visual comparison of kernel skirt level against spectral dynamic range — which is the figure's central argument — is not reproducible.
  - [BLOCKING] Panel titles (e)–(h) quote "mass beyond 2W" for (e) and (f), where no smoothing was applied and no $W$ shading is drawn. $W$ is undefined for those panels, so the quoted percentages (1.3%, <0.001%) rest on a convention the figure never states.
  - [ADVISORY] $n$ is not inferable from the figure: neither $N=1024$ nor the fact that a single realization underlies all four estimates is shown in-panel.
  - [ADVISORY] Estimator variability is shown only as the scalar $\nu$; no replicate realizations or variability band is displayed, so bias and one-draw noise cannot be separated by eye. The (d)/(h) conclusion about peak merging is asserted from $n=1$.
  - [ADVISORY] Red traces occlude the black reference PSD in (a) and (b); the true PSD is the comparison standard and should not be the hidden layer.
  - [ADVISORY] Clipping of the red trace at the top-row $y$-limit in (a) and (b) is unmarked.
  - [ADVISORY] Evaluative in-panel titles ("just right," "too smooth") pre-empt the reader's judgement; the quantitative criterion lives only in the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Legends overlap data in (a) and (e).
  - [ADVISORY] Whether the same record is used in all four top panels is stated only in the caption's "one record" and should be explicit, since the argument depends on it.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Recompute the kernel panels on a frequency grid oversampled by at least 16–32x relative to the plotted pixel width, or plot the kernel's upper envelope (peak-hold) with the nulls suppressed, and state which is shown. If the nulls are scientifically relevant, show them on an oversampled grid; if not, show the envelope.
  - Either annotate the grey curve's dB offset in (e)–(h) explicitly (e.g., "true spectrum, shifted +X dB"), or give it its own right-hand axis labelled in absolute dB, so the kernel-skirt-versus-dynamic-range comparison is quantitative.
  - Define $W$ for the untapered and unsmoothed cases (or replace "mass beyond 2W" in (e)/(f) with a quantity that is defined there, e.g., mass beyond the main lobe), and draw the corresponding shading in (e)/(f) for consistency with (g)/(h).
  - Add in-panel annotation of $N=1024$ and "single realization, common across (a)–(d)"; or, preferably, overlay 5–10 independent realizations as thin low-alpha traces, or a shaded chi-square $(\nu)$ pointwise interval, so the reader sees the estimator's spread rather than inferring it from $\nu$.
  - If the peak-merging claim in (d) is to be supported visually, add a small panel or inset showing the expected (ensemble-mean) smoothed spectrum alongside the single-draw estimate, since the single draw still shows two lobes.
  - Draw the black true PSD on top of the red estimate, or reduce the red trace to ~0.6 pt with alpha ≈ 0.6, in (a) and (b).
  - Mark clipped excursions in (a)/(b) (e.g., arrowheads or a note "values below −40 dB clipped"), or extend the top-row limits to contain the trace.
  - Move legends outside the axes or into an empty region; retitle panels with the stimulus only (e.g., "box $W=4/N$") and relegate "just right"/"too smooth" to the caption.

### figure3.png

---

**Context note:** This is a simulation/methods figure (spectral-estimation bias–variance tradeoff), not a group-comparison figure. Several checklist items (bar/pie plots, pairwise significance annotation) are structurally not applicable. The relevant analogues — dispersion visibility across the 300 realizations, axis honesty, overplotting, and interpolation across sparse sampling — are assessed below.

---
PLOT TYPE:          At risk — Panel (b) plots a *summary statistic only* (RMS error over frequency, averaged across 300 realizations) as a smooth line. This is the line-plot analogue of a bar-with-no-points: the realization-to-realization spread of the RMS error is entirely invisible, so the reader cannot judge whether the "broad minimum" at $W\approx8$–$10$ is resolvable from the value at $W\approx4$ or $W\approx16$. Add shaded interquartile or ±1 SD ribbons (or a thin-line spaghetti overlay of a random subset of realizations) around both the solid and dashed curves. Panel (a) plot type is appropriate for a raw record.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Visible — Both $N=1024$ (panel a title) and 300 realizations (panel b title) are stated in-figure, which is good practice and should be retained. However, individual realizations are not shown in (b), and $n$ per frequency bin (the number of frequency points entering each RMS) is not stated; with $N=1024$ this is inferable in principle but should be explicit, particularly for the "peak region" curve where the band is undefined in the figure.

COMPARISON:         Concern — The solid ("whole band") and dashed ("peak region") curves are almost certainly computed from the *same* 300 realizations, i.e. they are paired, but they are displayed as two independent curves. The scientifically interesting quantity — the shift in the argmin between scoring rules — has a paired sampling distribution that is narrower than either curve's marginal spread. Recommend an inset showing the paired per-realization difference (or the paired distribution of the argmin $W^\*$ under each scoring rule). Additionally, "peak region" is not defined in the figure or caption; the comparison cannot be assessed without it.

AXIS TRUNCATION:    None — Panel (b) $y$ begins at 0 dB and $x$ at $W=0$; no exaggeration. Note that the quantity is already in dB (a log scale), which compresses differences rather than inflating them, so the presentation is conservative. Panel (a) $y$ is symmetric about zero and untruncated. One residual issue: the Hann taper in (a) is "(scaled)" by an unstated factor and shares the $x$ axis, so its apparent amplitude ($\approx\pm70$) is arbitrary — state the factor or give it a right-hand axis normalized to 1.

OVERPLOTTING:       Present — Panel (a) draws 1024 samples of a rapidly oscillating AR(4) record *twice* (gray raw, green tapered) in a single 900-px-wide axis. The traces are visually solid in the central region; the green trace occludes the gray wherever the taper is near unity, so the reader cannot see that the two coincide in the middle by construction. Remedies: (i) stack the raw and tapered records in two vertically aligned sub-panels sharing the $x$ axis; (ii) reduce alpha on the gray trace and/or thin the line to 0.5 pt; (iii) if the point is only to illustrate the envelope, show a 200-sample zoom inset rather than all 1024 points.

MULTIPLE COMPARISONS: Flag for verification — No significance annotations are present, so no correction is strictly required. However, there is a related selection-bias issue the authors should address: the optimal $W$ appears to be chosen by minimizing error over ~20 candidate $W$ values on the *same* 300 realizations used to report the error. The reported minimum error is therefore optimistically biased (a winner's-curse effect over the $W$ grid), and the argmin is less stable than the smooth curve suggests. Verify on an independent set of realizations, or report the bootstrap distribution of $\arg\min_W$.

TIME SERIES:        Concern (minor) — Panel (a) is complete and evenly sampled; no issue. In panel (b), the marker spacing along $W$ is strongly non-uniform (dense for $W\lesssim6$, then steps of ~4 units out to 32), yet straight-line interpolation is drawn across the sparse region. Since the dashed curve's turnover near $W\approx20$–$24$ rests on only two or three sampled points, the apparent smooth maximum may be an artifact of interpolation. Either sample $W$ uniformly or keep the markers prominent and state that lines are linear interpolants, not model predictions.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (b) reports only the across-realization mean RMS error with no measure of dispersion; the claim of a "broad minimum" and of a shifted minimum for the peak region cannot be evaluated without uncertainty bands from the 300 realizations.
  - [BLOCKING] "Peak region" is undefined in both figure and caption; the dashed curve is therefore uninterpretable and irreproducible. Specify the frequency band (or the rule used to define it) in the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] The three circled points are not attributed to a specific curve. The "just right, $\nu=9$" circle at $W\approx4$ lies near the crossing of the noise and bias curves and near the *dashed* minimum, but visibly not at the *solid* curve's minimum ($W\approx8$–$10$); readers will misread this as the whole-band optimum. State which curve each circle belongs to and, if $W\approx4$ is the recommended choice, explain why it is preferred over the solid-curve argmin.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) overplots two dense 1024-point traces, with the green trace occluding the gray over the central half of the record.
  - [ADVISORY] The Hann taper's vertical scale factor is unstated, so its plotted amplitude has no interpretable meaning.
  - [ADVISORY] Non-uniform $W$ sampling with linear interpolation across a sparse region (panel b, $W>6$); the dashed curve's maximum is supported by few points.
  - [ADVISORY] Optimal $W$ appears selected and evaluated on the same realizations (selection bias over the $W$ grid).
  - [ADVISORY] Caption cross-reference reads "The circled points are panels (b), (c) and (d) of Fig.~\ref{fig:motif}" while the current figure also has a panel (b); relabel as "panels (b)–(d) of Fig.~X" with explicit $\nu$ values to avoid collision.
  - [ADVISORY] The caption does not state whether "RMS over frequency" is taken before or after averaging over realizations; these differ and the distinction affects interpretation of the minimum.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add shaded ±1 SD (or 10th–90th percentile) ribbons across the 300 realizations to both curves in panel (b); optionally overlay 30–50 individual realization curves at low alpha.
  - Define the "peak region" band explicitly in the caption, e.g. "peak region = $|f-f_0|<\Delta$, with $f_0$ the AR(4) peak and $\Delta=\dots$".
  - Add an inset showing the bootstrap distribution of $\arg\min_W$ for the whole-band and peak-region criteria, exploiting the paired structure (same realizations), rather than leaving the reader to eyeball two minima.
  - Annotate each red circle with the curve it lies on (e.g. leader lines, or filled vs open circles keyed to solid vs dashed), and mark the solid-curve argmin separately if it differs from the recommended $W$.
  - Split panel (a) into two stacked sub-panels (raw above, tapered below) sharing the $x$ axis, or reduce the gray trace to 0.5 pt at alpha 0.5; alternatively show a 200-sample zoom.
  - Give the Hann taper its own right-hand axis scaled 0–1, or state the scale factor in the legend.
  - Sample $W$ on a uniform grid (or at least uniformly for $W>6$); keep markers visible and note in the caption that connecting lines are linear interpolants.
  - Re-evaluate the selected $W$ on an independent batch of realizations and report that out-of-sample error, to remove the winner's-curse bias.
  - State in the caption the order of operations for the error metric (RMS over frequency per realization, then averaged over realizations, or vice versa) and which averaging the plotted curve represents.
---

### figure4.png

---
**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — This is a deterministic/analytical figure (Slepian sequences, their spectral concentrations, and eigenvalues), not an empirical sample. Line plots and a marker-plus-line eigenvalue ladder are the correct choices; none of the bar/pie/distribution-hiding concerns apply. One structural caveat: the bottom-right panel plots a quantity defined only at integer taper index $k$, yet the points are joined by a continuous line, which implies interpolation between non-existent intermediate tapers. Markers alone (or markers + very light guide line explicitly described as a guide) would be more honest.

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Visible (in the applicable sense) — $N=256$ and $NW=4$ appear in panel titles, and the number of tapers shown is stated in each legend ($k=0\ldots5$, $k=0\ldots7$, "first 1/3/5/7/9/all $N$"). There is no replicate/subject $n$ to display. However, the frequency grid resolution used to render the spectral windows and running sums is not stated anywhere, and it materially affects the apparent ripple/shoulder sharpness in the bottom-left panel; it should be given.
