**COMPARISON:** Concern — Two issues. (1) In the bottom-left panel the "first 9 tapers" curve (purple) is almost entirely hidden beneath the "all $N$ tapers" black curve; a reader cannot verify the claimed convergence behaviour because one of the six compared curves is effectively invisible. (2) The caption asserts that with all $N$ tapers the running sum is *exactly* the box, but the plotted black curve plateaus near 0.120 while the dashed reference box sits at ~0.125, and it shows visible ripple and shoulder overshoot. The rendered curve therefore contradicts the word "exactly" as drawn, and the discrepancy needs explanation (normalisation convention, finite frequency grid, or plotting of a partial sum).

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** Present — Not misleading in the exaggerating direction, but two axis-range problems exist. (1) Bottom right: the log $y$-axis extends down to $10^{-16}$ while the smallest datum is ~$2\times10^{-10}$; roughly six decades of empty canvas compress the informative part of the eigenvalue ladder. (2) Top left: the $y$-axis has no ticks, no labels, and no scale bar, so taper amplitudes are entirely unquantified. This is defensible for offset traces and is disclosed in the title ("offset for display"), but a scale bar or explicit statement that all tapers are unit-norm would make the panel readable.

**OVERPLOTTING:** Present — The top-right panel superimposes eight oscillatory spectral windows in the same band; within $|f| < 4/N$ the curves cross repeatedly and individual $k$ cannot be traced reliably, which weakens the caption's central claim that each window "fills the gaps left by the previous ones." The bottom-left panel has the specific occlusion of the purple curve noted above.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** No concern — No inferential comparisons or significance annotations are present.

**TIME SERIES:** Not applicable — The top-left abscissa is sample index within a single deterministic taper, not longitudinal measurement; there are no gaps or uneven intervals.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Caption–figure contradiction in the bottom-left panel: the text states the all-$N$-taper sum is "exactly the box," but the drawn black curve has a plateau below the dashed box level (~0.120 vs ~0.125) plus ripple and shoulder overshoot. Either the figure does not show what the caption claims, or a normalisation/grid effect is unexplained.
- [BLOCKING] The "first 9 tapers" curve in the bottom-left panel is occluded by the "all $N$ tapers" curve and cannot be read, so a legended comparison is not actually verifiable from the figure.
- [ADVISORY] The bottom-right eigenvalue ladder joins integer-indexed discrete values with a continuous line, implying interpolation between tapers that do not exist.
- [ADVISORY] Bottom-right $y$-axis spans to $10^{-16}$ with no data below ~$10^{-10}$, compressing the informative region.
- [ADVISORY] Top-left $y$-axis is entirely unlabelled and unscaled; taper normalisation is not recoverable from the figure.
- [ADVISORY] Eight overlapping curves in the top-right panel are distinguished by colour alone; tracing individual $k$ within the band is not feasible, and the panel is not robust to colour-vision deficiency or greyscale printing.
- [ADVISORY] The frequency grid used to evaluate $|U_k(f)|^2$ and the running sums is not reported, yet it controls the apparent ripple and shoulder sharpness that the caption interprets.
- [ADVISORY] Panels 1 and 2 use the same colour cycle for $k$, which is good, but this is nowhere stated; a single shared colour legend would make the correspondence explicit.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Reconcile the bottom-left panel with the caption: either plot the exact identity so the black curve coincides with the dashed box, or amend the caption to describe the residual ripple/plateau offset and state its cause (e.g. finite frequency sampling), and label the dashed reference level numerically.
- Make the "first 9 tapers" curve visible by increasing its linewidth relative to the black curve, drawing the black curve last as a thin dashed overlay, or adding a zoomed inset of the shoulder region $3 \le |f| \cdot N \le 5$.
- In the bottom-right panel, use markers without a connecting line (or an explicitly labelled guide line), and tighten the $y$-limits to roughly $10^{-11}$–$10^{0}$ so the ladder occupies the full panel height.
- Add a $y$-axis scale bar or annotation to the top-left panel stating the taper normalisation (e.g. unit $\ell_2$ norm) and the constant vertical offset applied.
- Reduce overplotting in the top-right panel by splitting it into a small-multiples grid (one window per subpanel, shared axes) or by offsetting the windows vertically; alternatively plot on a log ordinate so the out-of-band sidelobes, which are the quantity the caption cares about, become legible.
- Use distinguishable line styles in addition to colour for the two multi-curve panels, and verify legibility in greyscale.
- Report the frequency grid size and the eigenvalue solver/tolerance in the caption or methods so the rendered ripple amplitudes are reproducible.

### figure5.png

---
**CONTEXT NOTE:** This is a deterministic/analytical illustration (window sequences and eigen-weights computed exactly from $N$ and $W$), not a display of sampled measurements. Criteria concerning distributional hiding, error bars, sample size and multiple comparisons are therefore largely inapplicable; the relevant integrity issues here are axis honesty, comparability across panels, and trace occlusion. Assessed on that basis.

PLOT TYPE:          Appropriate — Bar charts in the bottom row are the correct choice for a discrete, exact, ordered quantity ($c_k$ vs. integer taper index $k$); there is no underlying distribution to hide and no error bars are implied. Line traces in the top row are appropriate for sequences in $t$. No pie charts, no bar-with-error-bar constructions.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Not applicable / partially visible — There is no statistical $n$. The relevant "counts" are $N=256$ (in suptitle), $K$ per method (stated in panel titles for (a) and (d), *not* for (b) or (c)), and the number of curves actually drawn (7 in (a); 4 in (b)–(d), flagged only in (c)'s title as "4 shown"). Panel (b) and (d) titles imply subsets ("modulated to $f'=0,1/N,2/N,3/N$"; "$k=0,1,2,3$ (of 7)") but the mismatch with (a)'s full set of 7 invites a false visual impression that (a) has more tapers than (d), when both have $K=7$.

COMPARISON:         Concern — Two cross-panel comparability problems:
 (i) **Inconsistent baseline convention.** In (a) all seven windows are drawn on a common zero baseline; in (b), (c), (d) the four curves are vertically offset. A reader scanning left to right may read (a) as non-negative and the others as oscillating about different DC levels.
 (ii) **No amplitude scale.** The top row has no y ticks, no y label, and no statement of normalization, so relative taper amplitude across methods is uninterpretable — yet the whole point of the figure is that the same estimator identity $\hat S(f)=\sum_k c_k|v_k^Tx_f|^2$ holds, which depends on normalization of $v_k$.
 (iii) **Unequal number of curves shown** (7 vs. 4) as noted above.

AXIS TRUNCATION:    None (bottom row) / Undetermined (top row) — The eigen-weight bar charts correctly begin at $c_k=0$ and share an identical 0–0.45 range across all four panels, so the flatness comparison that carries the argument is honest. The $k$ axis is also shared (0–16), correctly showing the trailing zeros of (a) and (d) rather than cropping to the support. Top-row y limits are unlabeled and appear to be set per panel, so truncation there cannot be assessed; panel (c)'s red trace appears to run into/through the top of its allotted band near $t\approx255$, suggesting clipping.

OVERPLOTTING:       Present (mild, panel (c)) — The offset traces in (c) cross into one another's bands (blue/orange near $t\lesssim20$; red/green near $t\approx200\text{–}255$), and the red curve appears clipped at the axis top. Ambiguity about which curve is which in the crossing regions. Panel (a)'s seven windows are cleanly separated; (b) and (d) are clean.

MULTIPLE COMPARISONS: No concern — No inferential annotations, no p-values, no pairwise tests.

TIME SERIES:        OK — $t$ is uniformly sampled $0\ldots255$ with no gaps; lines interpolate between adjacent integer samples only, which is not misleading at this density. One semantic point: in (a) the Hann segments of length $N/4$ are shown as exactly zero outside their support, which is correct for the zero-padded taper interpretation but should be stated so readers do not read it as missing data.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Top row lacks any y-axis scale, label, or normalization statement; vertical offsets are arbitrary and undeclared, so amplitudes are not comparable within or across panels.
  - [ADVISORY] Baseline convention is inconsistent: (a) uses a common zero baseline, (b)–(d) use stacked offsets. Same-figure convention switching is a known source of misreading.
  - [ADVISORY] Number of tapers displayed differs by panel (7 in (a), 4 in (b)–(d)) while $K=7$ in both (a) and (d). Only (c) explicitly says "4 shown". Risk of the reader inferring different bank sizes from the visual.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (c): traces overlap across offset bands and the red trace appears clipped at the upper axis limit; data may be lost at the right edge.
  - [ADVISORY] $K$ is not stated in the panel titles of (b) and (c); for (c) it is $N=256$ (all tapers), a fact central to the claim and currently only recoverable from the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption says "weight (normalized)" only via the axis label; the normalization convention ($\sum_k c_k = 1$, presumably) is never written, yet $\nu = 2(\sum c)^2/\sum c^2$ is normalization-invariant while the plotted heights are not.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add a y-axis label to the top row such as "taper amplitude (arbitrary vertical offset)" and either (i) a short scale bar in one panel, or (ii) an explicit note that each $v_k$ is unit-norm and traces are offset for display only.
  - Adopt one convention across (a)–(d): either offset all panels (including (a)) or draw all on common baselines with faint zero lines per trace.
  - Show the same number of tapers in every panel (4 is sufficient) and append "(of $K=7$)", "(of $K=7$)", "(of $K=256$)", "(of $K=7$)" to each title so bank size is readable without the caption.
  - Colour-code the first four bars of each bottom panel to match the four curves drawn above, making the taper↔weight correspondence explicit.
  - Fix panel (c)'s y limits so the red trace is not clipped, and/or add a small legend or per-trace $k$ label at the left margin to disambiguate the crossing regions.
  - State the weight normalization in the caption, e.g. "weights are normalized so that $\sum_k c_k = 1$; $\nu$ is invariant to this choice."
  - In the caption, note that (a)'s tapers are zero outside their $N/4$ support by construction (zero-padded), not missing.
  - Consider reporting $\nu$ to one decimal as now but adding the reference value $2K$ for (d) ($=14.0$) in text, since the exact agreement is a useful sanity check for the reader.
---

### figure6.png

---
**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — Overlaid line plots of spectral estimates against a reference PSD, plus kernel-response panels on a dB scale, are the standard and correct idiom for this material. No bar/pie chart concerns arise. One structural note: the caption reports four scalar summary statistics (median errors 11.6, 4.6, 1.3, 0.8 dB) that appear nowhere in the figure. If these numbers carry the comparative argument, they deserve a small fifth panel showing the *distribution* of |estimate − truth| across frequency for each method (four overlaid ECDFs, or four box/violin summaries), rather than being reduced to a median in prose. A median over a strongly frequency-dependent error is exactly the kind of collapse this review flags.

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Not visible — Two distinct "n" notions are at stake and neither is fully exposed.
(i) Record length: $N=1024$ is stated in the caption but not annotated in the figure; segment counts (9 Hann segments of 192) and taper counts (K=7) are in panel titles, which is good practice worth preserving.
(ii) **Number of realizations is not stated anywhere.** The panels appear to show a single Monte Carlo draw (one red trace per method). Under the small-n rule, a single realization is the extreme case where the underlying variability must be shown, not merely summarized. The equivalent degrees of freedom $\nu$ (17.2 / 14.0 / 8.9 / 17.6) is a useful proxy for estimator variance and its inclusion in the titles is commendable, but $\nu$ is a theoretical quantity; it does not demonstrate that *this particular draw* is representative.

**COMPARISON:** Concern — The four estimators are evidently applied to the *same* input record ("the record of Fig. motif"), which makes this a paired/matched design. The visualization is unpaired: four separate axes, one estimate each. The paired structure is the strongest available evidence here and is currently discarded. A same-axes overlay of the four estimates (or four difference traces, estimate − truth, on common axes) would let the reader see directly where one method beats another at each frequency, rather than eyeballing across panels. Cross-panel comparison is also hampered by the fact that the reader must mentally align four separate black reference curves.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** Present, largely defensible — Top row is clipped at −40 dB and the bottom row at −80 dB. On a dB (log) axis a floor is unavoidable and conventional, so this is not the misleading bar-chart truncation pattern. Two specific risks remain: (1) in panel (g) the kernel plunges below the −80 dB floor and is silently cut off, so the reader cannot tell how far down it actually goes — this matters because the caption's claim "the Hann kernels (g, h) fall below −40 dB" is a claim about exactly that clipped region; (2) the differing floors between rows invite misreading of skirt depths across rows. State the clipping explicitly.

**OVERPLOTTING:** Present — In panel (b) the red multitaper trace in the 0.2–0.5 band is a dense high-variance oscillation that saturates into a solid block roughly 10 dB thick; individual excursions and the true local mean are both unreadable. Panel (a) has milder crowding. In panel (f) the rapid taper-sidelobe ripple similarly congeals. Reduce line width, add alpha, or overlay a smoothed median so the reader can distinguish the level from the scatter.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** Flag for verification — No inferential annotations (no p-values, no significance brackets) appear, so no formal correction issue exists. However, four methods are ranked on a single realization and the ordering (11.6 > 4.6 > 1.3 > 0.8 dB) is presented as a result. With one draw and $\nu \approx 9$–18, the gap between the two best methods (1.3 vs 0.8 dB) is plausibly within realization-to-realization noise. Authors should verify that the ranking is stable across seeds before the caption asserts it.

**TIME SERIES:** Not applicable — Frequency-domain traces on a uniform grid; no temporal gaps or interpolation across missing timepoints.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Number of Monte Carlo realizations is unreported. The figure reads as a single draw, so the reported median errors and the implied ranking of the four estimators cannot be assessed for stability. State the realization count and the RNG seed.
- [BLOCKING] Caption asserts "the Hann kernels (g, h) fall below −40 dB" while panel (g) is clipped at the −80 dB floor, so the supporting detail is not visible in the figure. Either extend the axis or soften the claim to what is shown.
- [ADVISORY] Paired design (one record, four estimators) is visualized unpaired across four axes; the matched structure is not exploited.
- [ADVISORY] Overplotting in panels (b) and (f) renders the dense high-frequency variance as a solid band.
- [ADVISORY] Quantitative claims in the caption (median errors) have no visual counterpart in the figure.
- [ADVISORY] $N=1024$ and $W=4/N$ live only in the caption; the figure is not self-contained if reproduced or viewed in isolation.
- [ADVISORY] Two-best-methods gap (1.3 vs 0.8 dB) may not be resolvable at the stated degrees of freedom.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Report the number of realizations and the seed in the caption. If the panels show one draw, say so explicitly: "a single realization; $\nu$ quantifies the expected variability."
- Strongly preferred: recompute over, say, 200 realizations and plot the pointwise median estimate with a shaded 10th–90th percentile band per method. This converts each panel from an anecdote into a distributional statement and makes the bias/variance trade-off between methods directly visible — which is the actual argument of the figure.
- Add a fifth panel (or a second row) showing ECDFs of |estimate − truth| in dB for the four methods on shared axes, with the four medians marked. This replaces the caption's four bare numbers with the full error distribution.
- Add a difference-from-truth overlay: four traces of (estimate − true PSD) on one axis, which respects the paired design and removes the need for cross-panel eyeballing.
- In panel (g), extend the y-limit (e.g. to −120 dB) or annotate the clip with a broken-axis marker and a note "kernel continues below panel floor."
- Reduce the estimate line width to ~0.6 pt and set alpha ≈ 0.5 in panels (a), (b), (f); optionally overlay a frequency-smoothed median in a darker shade so level and scatter are separable.
- Annotate $N=1024$ and $W=4/N$ inside the figure (e.g. in the panel (a) legend box) so the figure stands alone.
- Confirm that the ranking of the two best methods is stable across seeds; if it is not, state that (c) and (d) are statistically indistinguishable at this record length rather than reporting a strict ordering.
- State in the caption which dB reference and clipping convention apply to each row, since the two rows use different floors.

### figure7.png

---
PLOT TYPE:          Appropriate — Overlaid spectral traces plus a difference panel is the right choice for a continuous frequency-domain estimate; no bar/pie misuse. The right panel is, in effect, a Bland–Altman-style paired difference plot, which is the correct way to display three estimators computed on the *same* data. One structural concern: no uncertainty band (e.g., jackknife/χ²-based CI for the multitaper estimate) is drawn on either panel, so the central claim ("agree to within their own noise") must be read from the caption rather than from the figure.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Not visible — The figure gives no indication that this is a single EEG epoch (n = 1 record); it is only inferable from the caption's cross-reference to Fig. \ref{fig:abstract}. The effective sample size that actually governs the noise here — ν (14.0, 8.9, 17.6) and the number of Welch segments (9) — is partially given in the title/legend, which is good, but the number of epochs/channels/subjects is absent. Because n = 1, this is a demonstration, not evidence of general agreement; individual "data points" in the relevant sense (per-epoch median differences across many epochs) are not shown.

COMPARISON:         Valid, with one concern — The pairing is correctly respected: all three estimators are computed on the same epoch and the right panel shows within-epoch differences rather than unpaired overlays of summary spectra. Concern: the summary statistics "median 1.6 dB, 95% 4.9 dB" are quantiles taken over frequency bins that are strongly correlated (bin spacing ≪ half-power width ≈ 0.9–1.0 Hz), so the effective number of independent comparisons is far smaller than the number of plotted points. The reference values 4.3√(2/ν) are quoted in the caption but not drawn, so the reader cannot verify agreement graphically.

AXIS TRUNCATION:    None — Both panels are dB (log-power) scales where no zero baseline is meaningful, and the difference panel spans the full data range including the −18 dB excursion near 0.8 Hz. The asymmetric range (−15 to +12) is data-driven, not exaggerating. No misleading truncation.

OVERPLOTTING:       Present — Left panel: three traces with substantial oscillatory structure largely coincide, and the heavy black multitaper line occludes the blue Welch trace over much of 0–25 Hz; the reader cannot judge where (b) and (a) actually differ except via the right panel. Right panel: two dense, high-variance traces cross repeatedly in the ±5 dB band, and the 0–2 Hz region is compressed into an unresolvable spike cluster. Line weight and z-order do the visual work here, not the data.

MULTIPLE COMPARISONS: Flag for verification — No pairwise significance annotations are present, so no formal correction issue. However, the "95% 4.9 dB" and "95% 5.9 dB" figures are implicit tail statistics over ~thousands of correlated frequency bins; authors should confirm how the extreme-value behaviour was handled (a 95th percentile over correlated bins is not a 5% error rate, and the maximum deviation over the band will exceed the per-bin expectation even under perfect agreement).

TIME SERIES:        Not applicable — Frequency axis, uniformly sampled, no gaps or interpolation across missing points. (If the underlying epoch contained rejected/interpolated samples, that should be stated.)

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] n = 1 epoch is not visible in the figure and the caption's generalizing claim ("The everyday versions agree to within their own noise") is unsupported by a single realization. Either restrict the claim to this epoch or add a panel summarizing per-epoch median differences across multiple epochs/subjects.
  - [BLOCKING] The quantitative claim is not verifiable from the graphic: the predicted noise levels (1.6, 2.1, 1.5 dB; and their combination for the differences) are stated only in text and not drawn on the difference panel.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel labels are out of order — the legend and caption run (a), (c), (b), with (c) listed before (b) in the left legend. This is gratuitously confusing.
  - [ADVISORY] Occlusion in the left panel: the 2.5-pt black trace hides the 1-pt blue trace; apparent agreement is partly a drawing artifact.
  - [ADVISORY] "95%" quantiles over correlated frequency bins are reported without stating the effective number of independent bins (≈ bandwidth × duration), risking over- or under-statement of agreement.
  - [ADVISORY] The 0–2 Hz region, where the largest disagreements occur (−18 dB), is visually compressed and not discussed in the caption beyond the harmonic-valley explanation, which does not apply there.
  - [ADVISORY] No indication of whether the multitaper estimate is adaptively or equally weighted in the difference panel legend (stated only in the left legend); keep the specification attached to both.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - State n explicitly in the caption's first sentence ("a single 4.6-s EEG epoch from subject X, channel Y") and, if the agreement claim is meant to be general, add an inset or companion panel with a beeswarm/dot plot of the median |difference| across all epochs (one point per epoch), with the predicted 4.3√(2/ν) levels as horizontal reference lines.
  - Overlay the theoretical noise envelope on the right panel: horizontal dashed lines at ±1 and ±2 times the predicted difference SD for each comparison, colour-matched to the traces. This turns the caption's arithmetic into something the reader can check at a glance.
  - Add a shaded jackknife or χ²_ν confidence band around estimate (a) in the left panel (e.g., light grey ribbon) so the eye can see that inter-estimator spread lies inside single-estimator uncertainty.
  - Reduce the black line to ~1.6 pt and use alpha ≈ 0.8, or plot (a) last with a lighter weight; alternatively offset the three traces vertically by a stated constant and say so in the caption.
  - Renumber to (a), (b), (c) in a consistent order across title, legends, caption and text; put the multitaper first in both legends.
  - Report the effective number of independent frequency bins alongside the quantiles, or replace "95%" with the maximum deviation and its expected distribution under the null of pure estimator noise.
  - Consider a log or split frequency axis, or an inset for 0–3 Hz, so the large low-frequency discrepancy is resolvable and can be attributed (taper leakage vs. segment length vs. detrending).
  - Add ν and the effective bandwidth to the right-panel legend entries so each difference trace carries its own noise scale.
---

### figure8.png

---

**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — This is a single-record signal-processing demonstration, not a group-comparison figure; time series, spectrograms, line spectra and a histogram are the right choices. No bar graphs, no pie charts, no error-bar-over-distribution constructions. Two caveats: (i) the **jet/rainbow colormap** in (c)–(e) is perceptually non-uniform and creates artificial banding and false sharp edges (notably the cyan/blue transition near 365–400 s and the green–yellow band at 2–5 Hz), which can exaggerate apparent spectral structure in exactly the way a truncated axis exaggerates a bar difference; use viridis/magma or a diverging map. (ii) Panel (h) is a histogram of *pixels*, which is the correct display for the quantity but the unit of analysis is non-independent (see ISSUES).

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Not visible — Nothing in the figure states that this is n = 1 recording, n = 1 channel, or how many windows/pixels underlie (c)–(e) and (h). The histogram in (h) has a y-axis in "pixels" but no total N annotated; the reader cannot recover the number of time windows (≈ 598) or frequency bins. Because the effective n for the (d)−(e) comparison is small (one subject, one channel), the individual-level unit is already fully shown for (a), (b), (f), (g) — good — but (h) should state N pixels, N windows, and n = 1 subject explicitly in-panel.

**COMPARISON:** Concern — Three issues.
1. Panels (c)–(e) are meant to be compared pixel-for-pixel, but only **one colorbar is shown, attached to (d)**. If (c) and (e) are on different color limits the visual comparison — and the reader's intuition about the 1.5 dB median difference in (h) — is invalid. Shared limits must be stated or a colorbar placed on each panel.
2. The (d) vs (e) difference in (h) is an intrinsically **paired** comparison (same pixel, same window, same frequency) and is correctly displayed as a paired difference — this is good practice. However only the median |diff| is annotated; a paired display of the *signed* difference distribution with quantiles (e.g., 5th/50th/95th) marked in-panel would be more informative than a single number in the title.
3. Panels (a) and (b) appear to be on inconsistent amplitude conventions: (a) is clipped at ±500 µV per the caption, yet (b) shows excursions to ≈ −620 µV. The same signal is therefore rendered under two different clipping rules in adjacent panels, which misleads the reader about ictal amplitude.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** Present — Multiple instances, some disclosed, some not.
- (a) amplitude is **clipped** at ±500 µV. Clipping is a more severe distortion than axis truncation because it silently alters plotted values rather than the view window. It is disclosed in the caption but not in the panel, and it is contradicted by (b).
- (c)–(e) frequency axes stop at 30 Hz. Disclosed by the axis itself, acceptable, but the reader cannot see whether the Nyquist band (30–100 Hz) contains the muscle artifact that typically accompanies a generalized seizure.
- (c)–(e) time axes begin at ≈ 1 s rather than 0 and are visually offset from (a); the panels are stacked as if time-aligned but the alignment is not verifiable. Add a shared x-limit and a vertical guide across (a) and (c)–(e).
- (c)–(e) lack x-axis labels except (e); acceptable for stacked panels but tick labels should be suppressed on (c)/(d) if the axes are shared, or labelled if they are not.
- Color *scale* truncation: if the colorbar range (≈ 10–48 dB) saturates the ictal maximum, the apparent "red plateau" in (c)–(e) is a ceiling artifact. Confirm no saturation or state the clipped fraction.

**OVERPLOTTING:** Present — Mild and largely unavoidable. (f) and (g) superimpose four spectral estimates; the gray Hann periodogram is appropriately backgrounded, but the orange (Hann+box) and blue (Welch) traces are nearly coincident over 0–5 Hz and 20–30 Hz in (g), and the black multitaper trace occludes them. Consider slight vertical offsets, a stacked residual sub-panel (estimate minus multitaper), or reduced linewidth/alpha. In (a) the 200 Hz raw trace over 600 s is heavily overplotted (≈ 120,000 samples into ~880 px) so the trace thickness encodes local density, not amplitude envelope — a min/max envelope render or explicit downsampling statement is preferable, since apparent "amplitude" in (a) is partly a rasterization artifact.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** No concern — No pairwise significance annotations, p-values, or asterisks appear anywhere in the figure, so no correction question arises. One related flag for author verification: the caption states the displayed channel is "the one with the largest ictal rise in 2–20 Hz power," i.e. the channel was **selected on the basis of the effect being illustrated**. This is a selection-on-outcome step across ~19–21 channels. It is harmless for a methods demonstration but must not be read as a typical or representative channel, and the figure should say "selected channel, not representative" rather than leaving the selection rule only in the caption.

**TIME SERIES:** Concern — Three points.
1. There is an apparently **flat / near-zero segment in (a) from ≈ 365 to ≈ 400 s**, which reads as signal loss, amplifier saturation-recovery, or a data gap rather than physiological EEG. It is plotted as a continuous line with no gap marker, and in (c)–(e) it appears as an abrupt dark-blue block plus a sharp vertical discontinuity at ≈ 400 s. Missing or invalid data rendered as continuous low-amplitude signal is misleading interpolation across a gap. If these samples are absent or artifactual they must be broken out with a visible gap (NaN) in (a) and masked (e.g., hatched or gray) in (c)–(e), and excluded from (h).
2. The spectrograms use 2-s windows stepped 1 s, i.e. **50% overlap**, so adjacent columns are not independent. This is stated in the caption and is standard, but it means the time axis of (c)–(e) is smoothed and the pixel population in (h) is autocorrelated.
3. Narrow vertical stripes at ≈ 400 s, ≈ 430 s and ≈ 510 s in (c)–(e) look like transient artifacts (electrode pop / movement). Either annotate them as artifacts or confirm they are physiological, otherwise readers will attribute them to the estimators being compared.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Only one colorbar (on panel d) is provided for three spectrograms that the reader is asked to compare pixel-for-pixel, and panel (h) quantifies that comparison. If (c), (d), (e) do not share identical color limits, the visual comparison and the interpretation of the 1.5 dB median difference are invalid. State shared vmin/vmax in the caption or give each panel its own colorbar.
- [BLOCKING] The flat segment at ≈ 365–400 s in (a) is plotted as continuous data with no gap or invalid-data marker, and the corresponding region of (c)–(e) is rendered as a uniform low-power block. Missing/artifactual data must be shown as a gap, not interpolated or plotted as near-zero signal, and must be excluded from the pixel population in (h).
- [BLOCKING] Amplitude convention conflict between (a) (clipped at ±500 µV) and (b) (excursions to ≈ −620 µV). The same signal is shown under two different clipping rules in adjacent panels without in-figure notice.
- [ADVISORY] Sample size is not recoverable from the figure: n = 1 subject, 1 channel; N pixels, N windows and N frequency bins for (h) are all unstated. Add to panel (h) and to the caption.
- [ADVISORY] The pixels in (h) are strongly non-independent (50% window overlap along time, spectral smoothing along frequency in (e), and a smooth underlying spectrum). The histogram therefore looks far more precisely determined than the effective degrees of freedom warrant. The median |diff| of 1.5 dB should be accompanied by an uncertainty estimate that accounts for this, or the figure should state explicitly that (h) describes the pixel population only and is not an inferential statement.
- [ADVISORY] Jet colormap in (c)–(e) introduces perceptual banding and false edges; this is a known misleading practice in the same class as axis truncation.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (g) has a duplicated y-axis label ("dB" printed twice); panel (f) label placement also collides with the (d) colorbar region.
- [ADVISORY] Possible color-scale saturation at the ictal maximum in (c)–(e) (broad uniform red region) — the apparent plateau may be a ceiling artifact rather than a real flat spectrum.
- [ADVISORY] Spectra in (f) and (g) are shown without uncertainty, despite each estimator having a stated ν from which a χ²-based or jackknife confidence band is directly available. Since the figure's whole argument is variance reduction, showing the bands would make the comparison self-evidencing rather than asserted.
- [ADVISORY] Channel selected for maximal ictal 2–20 Hz rise; selection-on-outcome should be flagged in-figure so the panel is not read as representative.
- [ADVISORY] x-axis alignment between (a) and (c)–(e) is asserted by stacking but not verifiable; (c) and (d) carry unlabelled tick values.
- [ADVISORY] Unmarked transient stripes at ≈ 400, 430, 510 s in (c)–(e) may be artifacts and could be misread as estimator behaviour.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- State shared color limits for (c)–(e) explicitly (e.g., "all three panels on a common 10–48 dB scale") and either place a colorbar beside each panel or label the single colorbar as applying to (c)–(e).
- Replace the flat 365–400 s segment with an explicit NaN gap in (a), mask the corresponding columns in (c)–(e) with gray or hatching, exclude those pixels from (h), and add a caption sentence explaining the cause (electrode disconnection, amplifier recovery, etc.).
- Harmonise clipping: either clip (b) at ±500 µV as well, or remove clipping from (a) and expand its y-limits; annotate the clipping level with a dashed horizontal line and a "clipped" label in-panel.
- Switch (c)–(e) to a perceptually uniform colormap (viridis, magma, or cividis) and confirm/annotate that no pixels saturate at the upper limit; report the saturated fraction if nonzero.
- Annotate panel (h) with N pixels, N time windows, N frequency bins, and "n = 1 subject, 1 channel"; add vertical markers for the median and 5th/95th percentiles of the signed difference rather than reporting median |diff| only in the title.
- Add an effective-degrees-of-freedom or block-bootstrap uncertainty statement for the 1.5 dB figure that accounts for the 50% window overlap and spectral smoothing, or relabel (h) as descriptive only.
- Add χ²/jackknife confidence bands (or a shaded ±1 SE ribbon) to the multitaper, Welch, and Hann+box traces in (f) and (g), and add a residual sub-panel showing each estimator minus multitaper to resolve the overplotting in the 20–30 Hz range.
- Enforce shared x-limits and add a light vertical guide at t = 247 s and t = 318 s spanning (a) and (c)–(e) so the dashed instants are visibly aligned across panels; suppress redundant tick labels on (c) and (d) if axes are shared.
- Render (a) as a min/max envelope (or state the downsampling/decimation used) so trace thickness is not mistaken for amplitude.
- Mark the transient stripes at ≈ 400, 430, 510 s with small arrows and label them as artifacts (or confirm they are physiological in the caption).
- Fix the duplicated "dB" y-label in (g) and adjust the (f)/(d)-colorbar layout collision.
- Add to the caption: "Selected channel (largest ictal 2–20 Hz rise of 19 channels); shown as a methods illustration and not as a representative channel."

### figure9.png

---
**Scope note:** This is a single-record time–frequency methods figure (one channel, one subject, three spectral estimators). Items 1–3 and 6 of the statistical-visualization checklist concern group-level distributional displays and are largely **not applicable**; they are marked as such rather than passed. The relevant risks here are axis/panel alignment, dynamic-range clipping, colormap-induced false structure, and the unsupported equivalence claim in the caption.

---
PLOT TYPE:          Appropriate — Time-series trace plus three spectrograms is the correct display for continuous single-channel EEG; no bar/pie/error-bar constructs are used, so distribution-hiding concerns do not arise. One residual concern: the three estimators are shown only as images, so the caption's claim that two of them "render it identically" cannot be verified visually. A difference image (c − d, in dB) or a single-frequency power-vs-time overlay would make that claim checkable.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Not visible — Effective n is 1 subject / 1 channel / 1 ten-minute clip, and 2 seizure events. None of this is stated in the figure; the caption says "channel Fp1" but gives no subject or dataset identifier, no sampling rate, and no filter settings. For a methods illustration this is acceptable in principle, but the figure must not be read as evidence of generality. State n = 1 record explicitly and note that the comparison is illustrative, not a performance evaluation across recordings.

COMPARISON:         Concern — Two separate issues.
(i) *Panel-width mismatch.* Panel (a) spans the full axes width while panels (b)–(d) are inset to the left of the colorbar. The x-axes are therefore not shared, so the shaded seizure windows in (a) do not align vertically with the corresponding spectrogram features — a horizontal offset of roughly 1–2% of the 600 s span (order 5–10 s) is introduced purely by layout. Readers will make exactly this vertical comparison, so this is a substantive misalignment.
(ii) *Estimator comparison confound.* Panels (b), (c), (d) have different effective degrees of freedom (ν = 2, 6, 4.8) *and* different effective frequency resolutions. Comparing (c) to (d) as "identical" conflates smoothing amount with smoothing kernel. The resolution bandwidths should be stated for all three panels so the comparison is like-for-like.

AXIS TRUNCATION:    Present — Two forms.
(i) Panel (a) is clipped at ±500 µV and the trace visibly saturates at both rails during the high-amplitude epochs. This is disclosed in the caption, which is good practice, but the reader cannot tell how much true amplitude is hidden, and clipping in the displayed trace can be mistaken for amplifier saturation in the raw data.
(ii) Frequency axes are truncated at 30 Hz with no lower-bound statement; the caption's "descends from about 20 Hz to 3 Hz" claim sits close to the bottom edge, where the 0–3 Hz region is compressed and partly saturated in the colormap. Neither truncation exaggerates a group difference, so this is not the classic misleading bar-chart case, but both limit verification of the caption's claims.

OVERPLOTTING:       Present — Panel (a) plots ~600 s of high-rate EEG into roughly 1200 horizontal pixels, so each pixel column aggregates many hundreds of samples. The resulting solid black band is a rendering artifact, not a measured envelope, and thin isolated spikes may be either real transients or aliasing of the decimation used by the renderer. State the downsampling/decimation method, or plot a per-pixel min–max envelope so the display is an honest summary of the underlying samples.

MULTIPLE COMPARISONS: No concern — No statistical tests, p-values, or pairwise annotations are present; nothing requires correction.

TIME SERIES:        Concern — The record is drawn as continuous across the full 0–600 s span with no indication of whether any samples are missing, interpolated, or artifact-rejected. Several very narrow full-height vertical stripes appear in all three spectrograms (near ~200, ~360, ~470 s); these are consistent with brief artifacts or dropouts and are currently indistinguishable from genuine broadband neural events. If any interval was interpolated, bridged, or excluded, it must be marked. Panel (a) also carries no x tick labels, which is defensible only if the x-axis is genuinely shared — and per the COMPARISON note it is not.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (a) x-axis is not aligned with panels (b)–(d) because of the colorbar inset; the shaded seizure windows are horizontally offset from the spectrogram features they are meant to annotate. Any reader inference about seizure onset/offset timing relative to the harmonic stack is corrupted by a layout artifact.
  - [BLOCKING] Unlabeled full-height vertical stripes in (b)–(d) are not identified as either artifact or signal, and no artifact-handling or missing-data policy is stated. Interpolated or bridged intervals, if present, are currently invisible.
  - [BLOCKING] The caption asserts that (c) and (d) "render it identically." No quantitative support is shown, and the two panels differ in both ν and effective resolution bandwidth. Either substantiate with a difference image/quantitative metric or weaken to a qualitative statement.
  - [ADVISORY] Jet colormap is perceptually non-uniform. It manufactures apparent edges at the cyan–yellow and yellow–red transitions and compresses detail within the blue and red ranges, which can create the visual impression of discrete spectral bands where power varies smoothly. This directly affects readability of the "harmonic stack" claim.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) trace saturates at the ±500 µV clip limits; the extent of hidden amplitude is not recoverable from the figure.
  - [ADVISORY] Acquisition parameters absent: sampling rate, anti-alias/high-pass/low-pass filter settings, notch filtering, spectrogram window overlap and hop size, and dB reference. Without these the three estimators cannot be reproduced.
  - [ADVISORY] Effective n (1 subject, 1 channel, 2 events) is not stated in the figure or caption; risk of over-generalization from a single exemplar.
  - [ADVISORY] Seizure intervals are shaded only in (a); the reader must transfer them by eye to three other panels.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) has no x tick labels, which is unsafe given the axis misalignment.
  - [ADVISORY] Colorbar is shared across (b)–(d) but this is not stated; confirm identical vmin/vmax, since the comparison is meaningless otherwise.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Rebuild the layout with a genuinely shared x-axis (e.g. `sharex=True` across all four panels, with the colorbar placed in reserved figure space rather than stealing width from (b)–(d) only). Verify alignment by drawing a vertical guide line at a known time through all panels.
  - Replicate the seizure shading, or vertical onset/offset markers, in panels (b)–(d).
  - Identify every full-height vertical stripe: annotate as artifact, or state explicitly that no samples were removed or interpolated. If any interval was excluded, insert a visible axis break or grey mask rather than bridging it.
  - Replace jet with a perceptually uniform colormap (viridis, or turbo if a rainbow is required for reviewer familiarity), and state vmin/vmax and the dB reference on the colorbar.
  - Add a fifth panel or inset showing (c) − (d) in dB, or overlay power-vs-time traces at two representative frequencies for all three estimators, to substantiate the "identical" claim quantitatively.
  - Report the resolution bandwidth alongside ν in each panel title, so that ν = 6 vs ν = 4.8 is not mistaken for the only difference between (c) and (d).
  - For panel (a), plot a per-pixel min–max envelope and state the decimation method; either raise the clip limit so the trace no longer saturates, or add a second inset at full scale for one seizure so the hidden amplitude is visible.
  - Add to the caption: subject/record identifier and dataset, sampling rate, filter settings, window length, overlap/hop, and an explicit statement that this is a single illustrative record (n = 1) and not a performance comparison.

## Cross-Figure Consistency Orchestrator

---
**BLOCKING ISSUES (must resolve before submission):**

1. **Panel-labelling system is not uniform across the set, and in two figures the panel-letter namespace collides with method labels.** Four figures label panels as "(a)…(h)" embedded in centred panel titles (figure2, figure6, figure8, figure9); two label only the top row / columns, leaving the remaining panels unaddressable (efigure4 rows 2–4, figure5 bottom row); five have no panel labels at all and are navigated positionally in the caption ("left/right", "top left") (efigure1, efigure3, figure1, figure4, figure7); and in figure1 and figure7 the letters (a)/(b)/(c) are bound to *estimators* rather than panels, so "(b)" means a curve in those figures and a panel everywhere else. Choose one convention (bold lowercase letter at the top-left of every axes, outside the frame), apply it to all 13 figures, and rename the three routes in figure1/figure7 to non-letter tags. — affects: [efigure1, efigure3, efigure4, figure1, figure2, figure4, figure5, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9]

2. **The same estimator is drawn in a different colour in different figures.** Multitaper: red in efigure1/efigure2, black in figure1/figure7/figure8(f,g). Hann periodogram + box: green in efigure1/efigure2, orange in figure7/figure8(f,g). Untapered periodogram + box: blue in efigure1/efigure2, orange in figure1. Welch: grey in efigure3, blue in figure7/figure8(f,g). A reader tracking one method through the paper sees it change hue up to three times. Fix a single estimator→colour table, state it once, and apply it to every figure in which that estimator appears (including the kernel panels of figure2/figure6, where red currently means "the estimator under discussion" rather than a specific method). — affects: [efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, figure1, figure2, figure6, figure7, figure8]

3. **Equivalent text elements will print at different sizes because the 13 figures were authored on canvases of very different widths and then scaled to one column width.** Estimated smallest rendered type ranges from ≈3.5 pt (efigure4, figure5) to ≈6.6 pt (efigure2) — nearly a factor of two for the same nominal element (tick labels, legend text). Even after the per-figure legibility fixes the specialists requested, the set will not look typographically cohesive unless every figure is re-authored at its final physical width with one shared rcParams block. — affects: [all 13 figures]

4. **Two colour-encoding languages coexist, and the two raster figures are not mutually comparable.** figure8(c–e) and figure9(b–d) use `jet`; every other figure uses a categorical line palette. Worse, figure8's dB colour scale spans roughly 10–45 dB while figure9's spans roughly 13–43 dB, so the same colour denotes different power in the two seizure clips that the manuscript invites the reader to compare. Adopt one perceptually uniform map and one shared vmin/vmax across both figures, and state the shared range in both captions. — affects: [figure8, figure9]

5. **In-figure overall titles appear in 9 of 13 figures and are absent from 4, so even the presence of a title is inconsistent** — and where present, capitalisation style is mixed (sentence-fragment lower case in efigure1/efigure3/figure2/figure6 panel headers vs. full capitalised sentences in efigure2, efigure4, figure1, figure3, figure5, figure7). figure2, figure6, figure8 and figure9 (panel headers only, no suptitle) are the correct model; bring the other nine into line and unify header capitalisation across the whole set. — affects: [efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, efigure4, figure1, figure3, figure4, figure5, figure7 (vs. figure2, figure6, figure8, figure9)]

6. **Line-weight hierarchy reverses between figures for the same roles.** The reference/truth curve is a heavy black line in figure2 and figure6 but a thin black dashed line in efigure2; the multitaper estimate is the heaviest trace in figure1, figure7 and figure8(f,g) but a thin trace among equals in efigure1/efigure2; in figure7 the heaviest line is black in the left panel and orange in the right panel. Define one weight convention (e.g. reference = 1.8 pt black, estimators = 1.0 pt, single-realization periodogram = 0.6 pt light grey) and apply it everywhere. — affects: [efigure1, efigure2, figure1, figure2, figure6, figure7, figure8]

7. **Near-duplicate figures with identical titles, and a supplementary figure cited from the main text.** figure5 is a strict subset of efigure4 and carries a verbatim-identical suptitle, so the two cannot be told apart without reading both captions. Separately, efigure1 appears to be the same kernel comparison as the appendix `fig1_kernels.png`, and Table `tab:dof` (main text, adjacent to figure7) points the reader to it — a supplementary figure is therefore load-bearing for a main-text table. Resolve the duplication and make the main-text/supplement division explicit in both captions. — affects: [efigure1, efigure4, figure5, figure7/Table tab:dof]

8. **Axis-label wording and unit convention differ for identical quantities across figures.** Spectral power is variously "dB" (efigure2, figure2 top, figure6 top), "power (dB)" (figure1, figure7), "kernel (dB)" (efigure1), "kernel (dB re peak)" (figure2/figure6 bottom); the dB reference is stated in only two figures. Normalised frequency is written "frequency (1/N)" (efigure1), "frequency offset (1/N)" (efigure4, figure2, figure4, figure6) and "box half-width W (units of 1/N)" (figure3) — only the last is unambiguous. And "difference from (a), dB" (figure1, figure7) uses a comma where every other axis in the set uses parentheses. — affects: [efigure1, efigure2, efigure4, figure1, figure2, figure3, figure4, figure6, figure7]

9. **The uncertainty convention is inconsistent in where it lives, not just in what it is.** No figure shows error bars or bands — defensible, since ν is the operative noise measure — but ν is printed inside panels in figure2, figure6, figure8 and figure9; given only in the caption in figure1, figure7 and figure3; tabulated only in Table `tab:dof` for efigure1; and defined nowhere in the figure set. Additionally, figure3 is built from 300 realizations while every other estimate figure shows a single draw, and this difference is never signalled at figure level. Adopt one rule (ν annotated in-panel for every estimate figure, defined once at first use, realization count stated in every caption). — affects: [all estimate figures: efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, efigure4, figure1, figure2, figure3, figure5, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9]

Pick one (recommended: faint horizontal only, or none) and apply it to every panel. — affects: [efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, efigure4, figure1, figure2, figure3, figure4, figure5, figure7]

2. **Legend placement follows no convention.** Inside upper-right (efigure2, efigure3, figure1, figure7), inside lower-left (figure2e, figure6e, figure8f), inside mid-left over data (efigure1), below the axes (figure3), and entirely absent where needed (efigure1 right panel, efigure4/figure5 top rows, figure8g). Standardise on one relative position — or, given the number of overlap collisions the specialists found, on direct inline curve labelling throughout. — affects: [efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, efigure4, figure1, figure2, figure3, figure5, figure6, figure7, figure8]

Verify marker size matches between efigure3 and figure3/figure4, and use marker *shape* as the redundant channel in efigure3 where eight series currently rely on hue alone. — affects: [efigure3, figure3, figure4]

Both are legitimate (different segment configurations; N = 256 vs N = 1024) but read as contradictions. Annotate N and the configuration wherever these numbers appear, and add a table note explaining that the "multitaper, all N tapers" and "untapered periodogram, then box" rows are identical *by the paper's identity*, not by transcription error. — affects: [efigure4, figure5, figure6, figure7, Table tab:dof]

5. **The design-bandwidth regime differs between the methods figures and the empirical figures, and the supplementary kernel figure follows the former.** figures 1–6 and efigures 1–4 use NW = 4 / W = 4/N; figure8, figure9 and the band-power table use NW = 2, K = 3. efigure1 therefore illustrates kernels at a bandwidth the EEG analyses never use. Either add an NW = 2 panel to efigure1 or state the correspondence explicitly in both captions. — affects: [efigure1, figure8, figure9, Table tab:bandpower]

6. **Terminology and capitalisation drift for the same objects across figures:** "FP1" (figure9 axis) vs "Fp1" (figure9 caption); "AR4" (efigure2 legend) vs "AR(4)" (captions); "raw periodogram" (efigure1, efigure2, figure1, figure6) vs "untapered periodogram" (captions); "sidelobes" (efigure1) vs "peak sidelobe" (efigure3) vs "side lobe" (captions). — affects: [efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, figure1, figure6, figure9]

7. **Math notation register is mixed across the set:** proper mathtext (λ_k, ν, Ŝ(f), σ) in efigure1, efigure4, figure2, figure5, figure7 versus ASCII in figure4 ("1 - lambda_k", "|U_k(f)|^2") and figure6 ("|f| <= W"), plus inconsistent spacing around "=" ("N=256" vs "N = 256"). — affects: [efigure1, efigure2, efigure4, figure4, figure5, figure6, figure8, figure9]

Rebalancing (splitting efigure4, tightening the figure1/figure7 difference axes, re-rendering the spectrograms) would make the set read as one paper. — affects: [efigure4, figure1, figure5, figure7, figure8, figure9]

Verify every forward and backward reference after relabelling. — affects: [efigure4, figure3, figure5, figure6, figure7]

---
**OVERALL ASSESSMENT:**

**Publication readiness: Major revisions**

The scientific architecture of this figure set is strong and the narrative order is sound — the progression from the three-route identity through the kernel/bandwidth mechanism to the EEG demonstrations is exactly right, and figure1, figure2, figure6 and figure7 show genuinely disciplined design (role-based colour, matched axes, effect-above-cause panel pairing). But the set does not currently read as one paper: three distinct visual dialects coexist (the polished black/orange/blue line figures, the raw default-palette supplementary figures, and the `jet`-rendered spectrograms), and there is no shared convention for panel labels, estimator colours, axis-label wording, title placement, or where ν is reported.

The three highest-value actions are, in order: (1) re-author all 13 figures from source under one shared style block at final physical size — this single step resolves the type-size disparity, the axis-label and notation drift, the frame/gridline inconsistency, and most of the per-figure legibility findings at once; (2) fix one estimator→colour→line-weight table and one panel-label convention and apply both globally, renaming the figure1/figure7 route letters so they stop colliding with panel labels; (3) replace `jet` with a perceptually uniform map on a shared dB range in figure8 and figure9, and resolve the figure5/efigure4 and efigure1/`fig1_kernels` duplications along with the two mismatched caption blocks.
